# Market research digest (web sweep, September 2026)

> **Generated evidence appendix (2026-09-05/06).** Output of a twelve-pass web research sweep run for `Docs/MarketAnalysis/MARKET_GAP_PLAN_2026.md`. Each pass was an independent agent with a distinct lens, told to search at least twelve times, read at least five pages in full, and label every finding verified / likely / speculative with a source URL. A completeness critic then listed contradictions between passes and the weakest evidence; two follow-up passes answered the gaps it named. Read it as sourced raw material, not as the plan: the plan resolves the contradictions the critic lists at the end of this file.

12 lenses. Each: summary, findings with confidence and sources, comparables, market wants, implications for Cosmic Shore, open questions.



## LENS: Steam economics for a $15 indie multiplayer Early Access in 2025-2026

Steam in 2025-26 is a record-revenue platform ($17.7B in 2025; $11.1B in H1 2026, +14.5% YoY) that is simultaneously a lottery for the median release: 20,282 games shipped in 2025, roughly half got under 10 reviews, 65.9% grossed under $1,000, only 8% cleared $100k and only 608 (3%) reached 1,000 reviews. Discovery runs on wishlists converted at launch (median ~0.11-0.15x week-one sales per wishlist, 0.10x for games over $10) and on sales velocity; nothing else Valve does is a boost. Winners in 2025-26 cluster into two shapes: cheap ($3-10) co-op "friendslop" that spreads through friend groups and streamers (PEAK, R.E.P.O., Mage Arena, Meccha Chameleon), and single-genre, single-hook games with a demo out months before Next Fest (Sir, We Have an Orc Problem: 150k wishlists, $361k day one, two people). Paid competitive multiplayer from unknown IP is the shape that dies: Wildgate ($29.99, well-reviewed, Blizzard-founder-backed) fell from 7.8k to 1.4k CCU in 25 days and is now 50% off with Mostly Negative recent reviews; The Midnight Walkers ($16 EA, 87k wishlists) converted 1.3% in week one.

Early Access is the launch, not a soft launch: of 225 games that graduated in 2025, only 20% did better at 1.0 and the median 1.0 window earned 40% of the EA window; EA refunds run 12.4% median vs 8% for 1.0 launches; median EA lasts 437 days. Back catalog now supplies 79% of Steam revenue, so post-launch discount stair-steps and event visibility matter more than the 1.0 date.

For Cosmic Shore this is bad news on almost every axis the plan currently chooses. It is multi-genre (the data punishes mixed genres), priced at $15 (above the viral multiplayer band, below the single-player quality band), competitive rather than co-op, fragmented across ~16 vessel-locked modes and 4 intensities (matchmaking population divides accordingly), has cut Next Fest (the only free, once-per-game wishlist event, forfeited permanently once EA starts), has no demo plan stated, and offers no achievements or Steam Input to the "hardcore" buyer it now targets. The things it has that the market rewards are AI backfill (always playable solo), a free-to-fly hub with toys, and a genuinely novel ecosystem. The plan should re-centre on one lead genre and one lead mode for the store page, re-enter Next Fest with a demo, treat the EA day as the only launch it gets, and re-examine $15 against $9.99 with a stated 1.0 bump.

### Findings
- [verified] Steam release volume hit a record 20,282 games in 2025 (~55/day); roughly half received fewer than 10 user reviews and ~2,200 got zero; only ~1,200 (6%) passed 500 reviews and 608 (2.99%) reached 1,000 reviews.
  - evidence: Zukowski's year review counts 20,282 releases with 608 reaching 1,000 reviews (2.99%, up from 2.44% in 2024 on 18,239 releases). SteamDB-derived reporting: 'almost half of all games released in 2025 received fewer than 10 user reviews', 'around 2,200 titles have received no reviews at all', 'about 1,200 games released in 2025 surpassed 500 reviews'.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2026/01/27/what-the-hell-happened-in-2025/ | https://80.lv/articles/steam-earned-usd16b-in-2025-but-nearly-half-of-19-000-games-got-under-10-reviews | https://steamdb.info/stats/releases/
- [verified] Revenue is a long tail: in 2025 65.9% of Steam releases grossed under $1,000, ~40% never recouped the $100 Steam Direct fee, only 8% grossed over $100k; the median game grossed ~$249. Indies as a class still took ~25% ($4.4-4.5B) of a record $17.7B year.
  - evidence: 'about 65.9% of releases earned under $1,000', 'roughly 40% had not reached the $100 Steam Direct threshold', 'only 8% grossed more than $100,000' (Gamalytic-derived). Median gross of ~$249 reported by secondary aggregators; Alinea: indies ~$4.5B, ~25% of platform revenue.
  - sources: https://game-developers.org/2025-steam-game-revenue-distribution | https://wnhub.io/news/analytics/item-49645 | https://www.notebookcheck.net/Indie-games-accounted-for-25-of-Steam-s-revenue-in-2025.1189429.0.html
- [verified] Wishlist-to-week-1-sales conversion: median 0.15x for games launching with >25k wishlists, 0.17x for >10k, but only 0.10x for games priced above $10; outcomes vary 10x between similar games; low converters averaged a 67% (Mixed) review score vs 91% (Very Positive) for top converters. A June 2026 GDCo update reportedly lowered the overall median to ~0.11x.
  - evidence: GDCo, data Sept 2024-Sept 2025: 'median conversion 0.15x (>25k wishlists)', 'Games >$10 at launch: 0.10x median', '>10k wishlists: 0.17x median (50k wishlists = ~8,500 Week 1 sales)', underperformers at 0.07x had '67% (Mixed)' average score vs '91% (Very Positive)'. Secondary reporting of the June 2026 refresh: 'median Week 1 sales at about 0.11x the launch wishlist balance'.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/the-state-of-steam-wishlist-conversions | https://www.immutable.com/insights/steam-wishlist-conversion-rates
- [verified] Wishlist thresholds at launch (Zukowski benchmarks, updated June 2026): Bronze 5,000, Silver 8,000, Gold 50,000, Diamond 90,000, mapping to lifetime gross tiers of $0-10k / $10k-249k / $250k-999k / $1M+. Popular Upcoming placement is generally put at ~7,000 quality wishlists.
  - evidence: Benchmarks page: 'Bronze: 5,000 wishlists; Silver: 8,000; Gold: 50,000; Diamond: 90,000' and revenue tiers 'Bronze $0-$10,000; Silver $10,001-$249,000; Gold $250,000-$999,000; Diamond $1,000,000+'; 'updated in June 2026'. Multiple secondary sources citing GameDiscoverCo put Popular Upcoming at ~7,000 wishlists.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/benchmarks/ | https://www.steampageanalyzer.com/blog/steam-popular-upcoming-list | https://altheragames.com/en/blog/steam-wishlist-guide
- [verified] Valve confirmed (GDC 2025) that wishlists are weighted by account quality (bot/low-engagement/active-spender tiers), external traffic is NOT a visibility factor, and Next Fest uses an ML model over the first 48 hours then 'culls the herd' from Day 3; ~2,000 wishlists gained is the rough floor to stay visible after Day 3.
  - evidence: Zukowski's GDC notes: 'Valve confirmed wishlists receive differential weighting based on account quality' (three tiers), 'external traffic is NOT a visibility factor', 'by Day 3 they start to cull the herd and show the top performing games more', Valve 'cannot precisely identify which metrics ML prioritizes'. '~2,000 wishlists is the threshold where Next Fest starts working... below that you're basically invisible by day three'.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2025/04/14/what-i-heard-from-valve-at-gdc/
- [verified] Next Fest Feb 2026 had 3,500+ demos (+51% YoY); the median entrant gained ~200 wishlists / 11 followers (GDCo) or ~806 wishlists in Zukowski's self-reported sample (30th pct 382, 70th 1,839, 95th 13,461); the top 5% gained ~7,000, down from ~10,000 a year earlier. Breakouts now mostly come from games that already had momentum, and co-op did best at the top.
  - evidence: GDCo: 'Median game added 11 followers and approximately 200 wishlists', 'Top 5% ... approximately 350 Steam followers (roughly 7,000 wishlists)', previously '~520 followers (~10k wishlists)', 'games that already had interest are continuing to scale it', 'Co-op experiences performed particularly well'. Zukowski table Feb 2026: 30th 382 / median 806 / 70th 1,839 / 95th 13,461 / high 57,074; advice: 'Launch demo months and months before the festival'.

- [verified] Median launch price of top-50 new releases fell from $19.50 (Feb 2023) to $15.64 (Oct 2025) by units (-20%), and $23.70 to $20.35 by revenue (-14%), driven by viral multiplayer/co-op hits succeeding at $7.99-$9.99; $14.99-$19.99 still works for single-player games with production values; $25-$55 is the dead zone for new IP.
  - evidence: GDCo: 'February 2023: $19.50 median (by units sold)... October 2025: $15.64', by revenue '$23.70 ... $20.35', 'relatively little priced at $25-$55 as default'. Secondary: 'viral, multiplayer-first concepts can thrive at $7.99-$9.99' and 'Single-player narrative, action-adventure, and simulation games with strong production values continue to perform at $14.99-$19.99'. 2025 top sellers: R.E.P.O. $10, PEAK $8, Schedule I $20; Mage Arena $2.99; Meccha Chameleon $6 (H1 2026).

- [verified] Early Access is the launch: of 225 games (>5k EA copies) graduating in 2025 only 20% did better at 1.0; the median 30-day 1.0 revenue was ~40% of the median 30-day EA revenue (down from a ~70% estimate on 2015-2023 data); median EA duration 437 days, mean 643. 2026 YTD (91 graduates) is 21%.
  - evidence: GDCo: 'Of the 225 Steam games emerging from Early Access, only 20% (45) did better at 1.0', 'median 30-day revenue at 1.0 ... about 40% of the median 30-day Early Access revenue', 'median Early Access duration ... 437 days ... average was 643 days'. GDCo 2023: 'pretend that the EA tag isn't a soft launch'; 1.0 discovery advantages 'marginal'. Niche Gamer on 2026: 21% of 91 graduates.

- [verified] Refunds: Early Access games see 12.4% median unit refunds vs 8% for 1.0 launches (~150 devs surveyed); refunds rise with low playtime (<2h avg: 12.6%), review score under 80% (>11%), sandbox/base-building tags (~13.9%), deeper discounts, and Chinese buyers (28% in one case). No multiplayer-specific split exists in the data.
  - evidence: GDCo survey: 'Median 9.5% ... Average 10.8%'; 'Early Access games: 12.4% median'; 'Regular Steam games: 8% median'; '<2 hours played: 12.6%'; '90-94% positive: 7.2%'; 'Sandbox: 13.9%'; 'Larger discounts correlate with increased refunds'.

- [verified] Ten reviews unlocks the visible score but triggers no algorithmic boost (Valve confirmed); visibility is only penalised below 40% positive; the algorithm reacts to revenue velocity. At ~31 sales per review (median), 10 reviews needs ~300-600 Steam purchases; $15k gross unlocks trading cards, ~$150k Daily Deal eligibility.
  - evidence: Zukowski quoting Valve: 'there is no if 10 reviews, give visibility'; 'if your game dips below 40% into Mostly Negative, your game will be less likely to be featured'; 'Steam algorithms only care about money... huge spikes in dollars earned'; trading cards ~$15,000, Daily Deal ~$150,000. Benchmarks: sales per review '31 is median' (2021).
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2025/01/20/steam-vs-people-algorithms/ | https://howtomarketagame.com/benchmarks/
- [verified] Week-1 sales ≈ 21.8x peak Day-1 CCU (median, no pre-orders; top-50 March 2025 debuts). Inverted for a multiplayer game: sustaining 1,000 peak CCU implies ~20k week-1 sales and, at 0.10-0.15x conversion, ~130-200k launch wishlists.

- [likely] Launch discount mechanics: Steam suggests 10-15% for 7-14 days; a discount of 20%+ triggers wishlist emails; a price increase within 30 days of the EA→1.0 transition voids the 1.0 launch discount (so an EA price bump must land >30 days before 1.0).

Zukowski's standing advice is that mixing genres is 'exponentially' harder because Steam shoppers 'have very strict genre preferences'.

High wishlist counts do not protect multiplayer EA from weak conversion or Mixed scores.

### Comparables
- Wildgate (Moonshot Games / Dreamhaven) (2025, $$29.99 launch → $14.99 (50% off) by Sept 2026): Crew-based space PvPvE shooter; 79% positive; peak 7,799 CCU on Jul 23 2025, ~4k by Aug 1, ~1.4k by Aug 16; ~130k units in 5 weeks; recent reviews Mostly Negative (Sept 2026). LESSON: Population decay of ~80% in 25 days is the default for paid competitive multiplayer; a 50% price cut did not reverse it and dragged review sentiment down. Design for solo/bot viability from day one and never plan to be rescued by CCU.
- Rematch (Sloclap) (2025, $$29.99): 1M sales and 3M unique players in under a week (Game Pass day one); Steam peak 23,175 CCU → ~4,600 in Sept 2026; ~1.2M Steam copies / ~$29M gross. LESSON: Even the breakout PvP sport of 2025 retained ~20% of peak; it needed a million buyers to keep a few thousand CCU. A 16-mode fragmented queue cannot be fed by a Silver-tier launch.
- PEAK (Aggro Crab / Landfall) (2025, $$7.99): 6.8M+ copies, $40M+; converted 266x the wishlist median; #2 seller by units on Steam in 2025. LESSON: Social co-op at $8 beats competitive at $15; a small team's multiplayer succeeds through friend groups and creators, not matchmaking.
- R.E.P.O. (semiwork) (2025, $$9.99 (Early Access)): ~$147-150M gross; #1 seller by units on Steam in 2025; launched into EA Feb 2025. LESSON: EA is not a discount on ambition; it is the launch. The price point and co-op hook did the work, not the 1.0 date.
- Mage Arena (jrsjams, solo dev) (2025, $$2.99 EA → $4.99): 235k copies in ~5 days (Jul 24-28 2025), ~$286k gross at $2.99; 78x wishlist-conversion median; top-20 new game of 2025 by units. LESSON: One legible mechanic multiplied to absurdity plus a price nobody has to think about; Cosmic Shore's eleven-vessel breadth is the opposite bet.
- The Midnight Walkers (Hybrid Interactive) (2026, $~$16 (Early Access, Jan 28 2026)): 87k wishlists after Oct 2025 Next Fest; 1.3% week-one wishlist-to-buyer conversion, 1.6% at 30 days; 61% positive of 891 reviews. LESSON: PvP EA at $16 converted 3x worse than co-op/sim comps with the same wishlist pool; a Mixed score then locks in low conversion and higher refunds.
- Sir, We Have an Orc Problem (Mumpitz Games) (2026, $not stated (indie tier)): 150k wishlists at launch; $361,657 first 24h; 200k units in 20 days; 2 people, ~4 months, $200 ads; demo median playtime 1h26. LESSON: Single genre, one mechanic scaled 1000x, demo out early, playtime that proves the hook. Breadth is not a substitute for a hook the store page can state in one line.
- SpaceCraft (Shiro Games) (2026, $~$30 (Early Access, May 20 2026)): 300k+ wishlists pre-launch; 65% Mixed on 1,259 reviews within three weeks; combat deferred to post-launch. LESSON: Wishlists buy the first week only; an EA that ships with its core loop missing or fragmented across many half-populated modes gets scored as unfinished, and that score is the one that persists.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) Co-op / social play with friends that is funny to watch on stream, at an impulse price ($5-10)
- (hardcore, must-have) A store page that names ONE genre and one hook in a sentence; no genre mixing
- (both, must-have) A demo available months before Next Fest with 30+ minutes median playtime
- (both, must-have) Always playable solo (bots/AI backfill) so a small population never blocks a session
- (both, must-have) Very Positive score (≥85%) sustained from launch week
- (hardcore, strong) Visible content cadence and a stated EA roadmap; treat the EA day as the launch
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) Steam-native hooks the hardcore buyer filters on: achievements, controller glyphs/Steam Input, Deck Playable
- (casual, nice-to-have) Honest regional pricing (Valve recs, Poland -20-25%) and at least a localised store page
- (both, strong) A launch price the buyer does not have to justify, with a stair-stepped discount plan over the 12-month tail

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- The EA day is the only launch Cosmic Shore gets: median 1.0 revenue is 40% of the EA window and only 1 in 5 graduates does better at 1.0. Every marketing dollar and every design decision about 'what ships' has to be made against the EA date, not the 1.0 date 12 months later.
- Cutting Next Fest is the single most expensive cut in the plan. It is the only free, platform-wide wishlist event, it can be entered once per game and only before release, and it is forfeited permanently the day EA goes live. Put a demo up months ahead and enter the October 2026 (or Feb 2027) fest before EA; without it the game reaches launch with whatever wishlists an 8-person team can buy or beg.
- Do the wishlist arithmetic before setting a date. Popular Upcoming needs ~7k quality wishlists; Silver is 8k. At 0.10x conversion for a >$10 game, 8k wishlists is ~800 week-one sales, ~$8.4k gross, ~25 reviews, and a peak CCU in the tens. That population cannot fill 16 vessel-locked modes across 4 intensities and 3 domains. Either the launch needs 50k+ wishlists (Gold) or the mode surface at EA must collapse to 2-3 default queues with the rest gated behind the quest chain.
- $15 is the wrong number for what the game is. It sits above the band where 2025-26 multiplayer hits live ($3-10: PEAK, R.E.P.O., Mage Arena, YAPYAP, Meccha Chameleon) and below the band where single-player production value earns $15-20, and games over $10 convert at 0.10x vs 0.15x. Model $9.99 EA with a public commitment to raise at 1.0 (bump must land >30 days before the transition), or $14.99 with a 15% launch discount and a stair-step plan; do not launch at $15 and cut 50% in month one, which is the Wildgate path to Mostly Negative.
- The market rewards co-op friendslop; Cosmic Shore's mode list is almost entirely competitive domain races with one co-op mode (Co-op Wildlife Blitz). The emergent ecosystem, the hunt modes and the 'lava lamp' hub are the assets that could read as social/co-op on a store page; lead with a co-op-first mode and the ecology, not with 'party game for pilots' or a vessel matrix.
- The multi-genre premise is the thing Steam data most punishes: shoppers have strict genre preferences and mixed-genre pages convert badly. Pick ONE vessel and ONE mode as the store-page identity (the Squirrel trail-racer or the Sparrow ecology hunt are the most legible) and present the rest as content depth, not as the pitch. 'A casual game for hardcore gamers' is a slogan, not a genre; the capsule, tags and first 10 seconds of trailer must name a genre.
- AI backfill is the single most important retention feature the game already has; it must be first-class and stated on the store page ('always playable solo, up to 12 with bots'). Every comp shows CCU falling to 15-20% of peak within a month; a 1-4 player game with bots survives that, a matchmaking game does not. Do not tag the game 'Massively Multiplayer' or lead with 'online multiplayer' — that sets an expectation the population will not meet and drives refunds under the 2-hour rule (match-length sessions mean a buyer can play 5-10 rounds and still refund).
- Reviews are the conversion engine, not the algorithm: 10 reviews needs ~300-600 real Steam sales, and a score under 80% roughly doubles refunds and halves conversion. The 4,000-crystal vessel price against 200/50/0 crystal payouts is a grind gate inside a paid game and will be the first negative-review theme ('locked vessels', 'no content'); with no cosmetics or IAP there is no economic reason for it. Unlock vessels by play milestones or ship all vessels and let modes be the gating.
- Refund exposure is above median by construction: EA (12.4% median), low average playtime, sandbox-like tags, and discounts all raise it. Budget net revenue per $15 unit at roughly $9 after Valve's 30%, ~12% refunds and regional/VAT, i.e. a Silver launch of 800 units is ~$7k, not $12k.
- Back catalog is 79% of Steam revenue; the 12-month EA plan should be a calendar of stair-stepped discounts (10-15% launch, then each major update paired with the next rung and a Steam event) rather than a single 1.0 push. Never skip a rung; a 50% cut in month one (Wildgate) burns the tail.
- Regional pricing is a solved problem: accept Valve's recommendations and price Poland down 20-25%. English-only with no store-page localisation will suppress CN/JP/LATAM regardless, and Chinese buyers refund at much higher rates; at minimum localise the store page text and controls glossary before EA.
- Steam Deck is not a channel (0.7% of users, 2.75% of playtime, over-indexing single-player), but a Playable rating is cheap for a Unity/Proton game with gamepad support and is a trust badge for the 'hardcore' buyer. Deprioritise Verified; do not spend engineering on it before Next Fest and the demo.
- Dropping the Steamworks SDK removes achievements and Steam Input, the two Steam-native features the hardcore buyer treats as table stakes in a paid game. Achievements are days of work and the only Steam-visible retention hook the game will have without cosmetics or ranked; reconsider before EA, not at 1.0.
- Reconsider the roadmap's 'more vessels/modes' as the EA promise. Every winner in the dataset went deeper on one thing; every multiplayer loser spread population across modes. The EA roadmap that sells is 'the ecosystem gets deeper, the co-op hunt gets bigger, the racer gets more tracks', not 'vessel 9, 10 and 11'.

### Open questions
- What is the current Steam wishlist count, and how old is the Steam page? None of the plan documents mention a number; every benchmark above is meaningless without it.
- Is there a shippable demo, and can the team enter the October 2026 Next Fest before EA? If EA must precede Next Fest, what replaces the ~1-7k wishlists it would have produced?
- Which 2-3 modes are the default queue at EA, and what is the target peak CCU on launch day? Has anyone modelled how a 200-CCU population spreads over 16 modes x 4 intensities x 3 domains x vessel locks?
- Will the EA price rise at 1.0? If yes, the bump must land more than 30 days before the transition or the 1.0 launch discount is lost; if no, why is EA priced like a finished game?
- What is the refund exposure under the 2-hour rule given that a session is a 3-5 minute match? Has the team measured median session length in playtests?
- Is the vessel-purchase economy (4,000 crystals at 200/50/0 per match) staying for a paid PC launch with no IAP? What does it buy the game other than negative reviews?
- Which single genre and which single vessel will the capsule, tags and trailer name? 'Multigenre' cannot be a Steam tag.
- Is there a co-op-first mode (beyond Co-op Wildlife Blitz) that can lead the store page, given co-op is where 2025-26 multiplayer revenue is?
- Regional pricing: are Valve recommendations applied, is Poland priced down, and is the store page localised into CN/JP/DE/FR even if the game is not?
- Steam Deck: does the Unity build run under Proton with the gamepad path, and is a Playable rating a free win or a week of work?
- What does the 12-month discount and event calendar look like, and who owns the post-launch review-response cadence that the refund and conversion data say matters most?


## LENS: What "a casual game for hardcore gamers" means in the 2025-2026 market

The position Cosmic Shore wants is real, and in 2025-2026 the market gave it two concrete shapes, neither of which is "many genres in one box." The first is the run-based solo "forever game" (Balatro, Vampire Survivors, Nubby's, Mewgenics, Slay the Spire 2): one tiny rule set, 10-30 minute runs, zero-friction restart, combinatorial builds that let hardcore players "break the game," and a low price. Balatro hit 1M in under a month at $15 and ~7M by late 2025; Mewgenics did 1M copies in seven days in Feb 2026; Slay the Spire 2 hit ~340K CCU on day one in March 2026. The second is "friendslop" (Lethal Company, R.E.P.O., PEAK): $8-10 co-op for 4-6 friends on voice, physics chaos, failure as comedy, games that scale in friend groups and therefore never suffer the cold-start problem. R.E.P.O. and PEAK reached Steam's Platinum revenue tier in 2025 at $8; PEAK sold 10M in two months from a one-month game jam.

The property everything shares is elegance: depth from few rules (Rosewater's "lenticular design"), a sub-20-minute atomic loop, and social/streamer legibility. What separates the winners from the losers is not skill ceiling; it is whether the game works at 1-4 players without a public matchmaking population. GameDiscoverCo's rule still holds: multiplayer games settle at ~6% of launch CCU, PvP needs a far bigger atomic network than co-op, and every skill gate makes it bigger.

The vehicle/piloting segment is where the warning signs are loudest. Rocket League (1.09M CCU in Jan 2026) owns "casual for hardcore" in vehicles via one universally understood objective, one car, 5-minute matches, and F2P scale. Everything else arcade-vehicle in the last 18 months struggled: Psyonix's own Rocket Racing is shutting down (Epic, March 2026: "we failed to build something awesome enough to attract and retain a large player base"), KartRider: Drift died, SEGA called Sonic Racing CrossWorlds' 1M-in-27-days "below expectations," and AG racers like Redout 2 could not hold an online population at all. Steam racing revenue concentrates in sims and licensed AAA.

For Cosmic Shore the verdict is blunt: the tagline is defensible, the current product is not yet shaped like the games that own it. Sixteen vessel-locked versus modes multiply the cold-start problem sixteen ways at $15, Windows-only, no demo, no cross-play, no co-op-versus-the-ecosystem mode, no voice, no achievements, and a 20-wins-per-vessel unlock drip. The fix is to collapse the front door to two or three modes, lead with a friend-group co-op mode against the artificial-life ecosystem (the one thing no competitor has), make every session a 10-minute run with instant rematch, and ship a demo.

### Findings
- [verified] F1: The 2025-26 market's own name for 'casual game for hardcore gamers' in multiplayer is 'friendslop' — low-barrier co-op for friend groups, defined by Bushnell's Law entry difficulty, proximity voice chat, physics-driven chaos, low-fi 3D, sub-$20 pricing, and being 'generally unenjoyable when played solo'. The term went viral from a tweet about Lethal Company in March 2025 and usage spiked after PEAK (June 2025). Critics (Aftermath's Jay Castello, July 2025) contest the label but not the pattern.
  - evidence: Wikipedia's Friendslop article lists Among Us (2018), Lethal Company (2023), Content Warning/Webfishing (2024), PEAK/R.E.P.O./Guilty as Sock!/Mage Arena (2025), and five more 2026 titles; Inverse (Nov 23 2025) defines it as 'low-stakes multiplayer games focused on achieving a common goal, coordinating usually through voice chat', 'unserious and definitely not competitive', where 'failure is treated comedically'.
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friendslop | https://www.inverse.com/gaming/friendslop-peak-repo-fortnite-trend | https://www.creativebloq.com/3d/video-game-design/what-is-friendslop-and-why-it-it-taking-over-gaming-in-2026
- [verified] F2: Cheap co-op friend-group games reached Steam's top revenue tier in 2025 at a fraction of AAA prices. R.E.P.O. ($8, Early Access Feb 26 2025) peaked at 230K CCU its first weekend and was the highest-grossing paid game on Steam the week of Mar 11-18 2025; PEAK (June 16 2025, built in a one-month game jam by Aggro Crab + Landfall, up to 4 players) sold 1M in 6 days and 10M by mid-August 2025. Both landed in Steam Best of 2025 Platinum (>$125M est.) 'despite much lower price points'.
  - evidence: Wikipedia R.E.P.O. and Peak pages; GameDiscoverCo's Jan 6 2026 Best-of-2025 analysis notes Schedule I and R.E.P.O. in Platinum and that 31 of the top-100 grossers debuted in 2024-25 vs 24 the prior year. A Medium/SteamData roundup claims R.E.P.O., PEAK and Schedule I together moved 42M units / $377.5M in 2025 (unverified aggregate).
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R.E.P.O. | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peak_(video_game) | https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/who-won-steams-best-of-2025-revenue | https://medium.com/@research_86150/steam-in-2025-the-year-indie-games-ate-the-industry-alive-666abf04551f
- [verified] F3: The cold-start problem governs which multiplayer designs survive: a typical multiplayer PC/console game levels out at ~6% of peak launch CCU (best case 10-20%); co-op games have small 'atomic networks' because they scale in friend-group increments of 2-4, PvP needs enough skilled players across time zones for fast matchmaking, and 'high-skill gates make cold start harder'. GameDiscoverCo's recommendation: 'more online co-op in everyone's games'.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo applies Andrew Chen's framework; cites Spellbreak (a16z-backed, well-executed BR) failing to hold CCU. Note this analysis is from May 2023, older than the brief's preferred window, but it is the framework the 2025 friendslop wave validated.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/analysis-multiplayer-game-discovery
- [verified] F4: Week-1 Steam sales are roughly 20x Day-1 CCU (median 21.8x without pre-orders, 15.4x with, 20.4x blended, March 2025 cohort). Games with post-launch virality — 'casual/puzzle titles with broad appeal' — blow past it: Nubby's Number Factory ($5 pachinko roguelite) hit 224x, Schedule I 19x. Nubby's revenue is estimated around $1.7M.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo May 13 2025 analysis of March 2025 releases; games-stats.com revenue estimate for Nubby's; GameMaker blog notes 30K+ copies shortly after release.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/how-your-steam-launch-players-relate | https://games-stats.com/steam/game/nubbys-number-factory/ | https://gamemaker.io/en/blog/following-the-fun-nubbys-number-factory
- [verified] F5: Balatro is the reference solo 'casual for hardcore' game: $15, 1M units in under a month (80% PC), 100K demo downloads in its first three months, 5M by Jan 2025 and ~7M by Oct 2025, GDCA 2025 Game of the Year. LocalThunk's design rule: 'people need to be able to break a game like this... having a clear choice is same as no choice at all.' Runs are 10-20 minutes early, 30-45 at high antes, with auto-save so a session can stop anywhere.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo's Balatro launch analysis (Playstack's Liz Cheng-Moore credits early demo, streamer tournament, Discord); Game World Observer on 5M (Jan 21 2025); Playstack CEO talk titled '7-Million-Copy Indie Hit' (Oct 2025); TheGamer (Feb 4 2025) on short sessions and auto-save.

They're complicated. So, we made the game that we wanted to play.'

- [likely] F7: The run-based roguelite shape is still the strongest single-player 'one more run' market in 2026: Mewgenics (Feb 10 2026, full release, no Early Access) sold 1M in 7 days (~$25M gross), recouped costs in ~3 hours, peaked at 115,428 CCU (beating Hades II's peak); Slay the Spire 2 (EA Mar 5 2026) reportedly hit ~340K CCU on launch day, above the original's all-time peak.

- [verified] F8: Designers describe the target property as elegance — depth without complexity. Rosewater/Justin Gary (Feb 26 2026): 'elegance is one of the core values of game design — it is the secret to gaining depth without relying on complexity'; 'lenticular design' masks complexity from a beginner while an advanced player sees it. Game Developer's framework: easy-to-learn = inherent simplicity, coherency, progression, communication; hard-to-master = a large possibility space plus uncertainty; elegant systems are multi-purpose, trade-off-laden, combo-friendly, modular.

### Comparables
- Balatro (2024, $14.99): 1M in <1 month; 5M by Jan 2025; ~7M by Oct 2025; GDCA 2025 GOTY LESSON: One rule set, one screen, one run length; let players break the game; ship a demo early. Depth came from joker combinatorics, not from more modes.
- Vampire Survivors (2022, $4.99): 27M+ players by Apr 2026; studio scaled to 15+ projects LESSON: Lower the input floor to almost nothing and put the hardcore depth in build variety and unlock checklists; poncle 'made the game we wanted to play' because card games were too complicated.
- PEAK (2025, $7.99): 1M in 6 days, 10M by mid-Aug 2025; Platinum-tier revenue LESSON: Co-op that scales in friend groups has no cold-start problem; daily seed gives a shared challenge; failure is the content.
- R.E.P.O. (2025, $7.99): 230K CCU first weekend; top paid Steam grosser week of Mar 11-18 2025; Golden Joystick Best EA 2025 LESSON: Early Access works for friendslop because the friend group is the retention mechanism; verbal coordination and physics chaos are the design, not polish.
- Rocket League (2015 (F2P 2020), $Free (was 19.99)): 1.09M CCU Jan 25 2026, first 1M since 2020 LESSON: One universally understood objective, one vehicle, identical physics for all, 5-minute matches, cross-play, and a mastery ladder players can name rank by rank. Its scale depends on F2P.
- Trackmania (2020) (2020, $Free + 19.99/yr Club): ~1.2-2.7K Steam CCU in 2026; Metacritic 74; subscription backlash LESSON: Sub-minute tracks and instant restart make a solo hardcore loop; but a piloting game without co-op or F2P scale stays small on Steam.
- Mario Kart World (2025, $79.99 (bundled)): 15.39M by June 30 2026 LESSON: Raise the ceiling with optional techniques (charge jumps, rail grinds) that never change the floor — the lenticular pattern applied to driving.
- Sonic Racing: CrossWorlds (2025, $not verified (~60-70)): 1M in 27 days, Overwhelmingly Positive, yet SEGA: 'did not meet our expectations' LESSON: Quality and reviews do not save a vehicle game from a small addressable market; publishers price that market at ~2M units.
- Rocket Racing (Fortnite) (2023, $Free): Shutting down Oct 2026; Epic: 'failed to build something awesome enough to attract and retain' LESSON: Distribution and pedigree do not fix a racing loop that lacks a friend-group reason to return; 'lacked content' and 'undifferentiated from Mario Kart' were the critical verdicts.
- KartRider: Drift (2023, $Free): Global shutdown Feb 27 2025; 'commercial failure' LESSON: Live-service kart racing outside Nintendo has no proven Western audience; do not assume the vehicle genre carries the party-game audience.
- Redout 2 (2022, $29.99): 82% positive, but online 'effectively dead' (29 players, 412 ATH) LESSON: 'NEVER launch without full cross play'; an arcade-racer online mode needs either bots-first design or a co-op reason to exist.
- Marvel Rivals (2024, $Free): 644K Steam peak Jan 2025 -> ~90K peak Sept 2026 (-84%), still Platinum revenue LESSON: Casual-friendly rule choices buy the launch spike; hardcore retention still needs F2P scale and a balance team. At paid-indie scale this shape is unreachable.
- Deadlock (2024 (invite beta), $Free): 171K ATH Sept 2024; 125K Feb 2026; ~69K Aug 2026; no open beta LESSON: Skill gates enlarge the atomic network; a hardcore PvP loop without casual on-ramps plateaus even with Valve behind it.
- Mewgenics (2026, $~25 (est. from ~$25M/1M units)): 1M in 7 days; recouped in 3 hours; 115K CCU LESSON: Creator reputation plus a complete game at launch beats an EA roadmap for a campaign-style game; consider what 'complete' means for a mode-collection.
- Nubby's Number Factory (2025, $4.99): 224x Day1-CCU-to-Week1 multiplier; ~$1.7M est. LESSON: A single readable screen where every number visibly explodes is what streamers and 'one more run' need; presentation budget is irrelevant if the loop is legible.
- Jump Space (2025 (EA), $19.99): 500K demo players, 1M+ wishlists pre-launch LESSON: A spaceship game gets its audience from the co-op wrapper and the free demo; the piloting is the flavor, not the hook.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) An atomic loop of 5-20 minutes with a one-input restart and no friction between runs/matches
- (both, must-have) Play-with-friends co-op (2-6) that works at zero public concurrency — friend group as the retention mechanism, not matchmaking
- (both, must-have) Elegance: one small rule set a newcomer grasps in minutes, with depth emerging from combinations (lenticular design), not from more modes or menus
- (hardcore, strong) A nameable mastery ladder — techniques players can list by rank, learn from clips, and feel themselves climb
- (hardcore, strong) 'Break the game' build variety — combinatorial upgrades that let a run go absurd
- (casual, strong) Failure as comedy / low stakes — losing together is content, not punishment
- (both, strong) Price under $10 for social/short-session games, ~$15 only with Balatro-class solo longevity
- (both, strong) Streamer/clip legibility — moments readable in a 15-second clip on a single screen
- (both, strong) Free demo / Next Fest presence before launch
- (casual, strong) Casual-friendly competitive rules: no forced roles, instant queues, every role feels impactful; visible bots are accepted for solo play
- (both, must-have) Cross-play or a design that does not depend on a public lobby population at all
- (both, nice-to-have) Daily/weekly shared seed or challenge with a leaderboard
- (hardcore, strong) Meta-unlock checklist (achievements, characters, unlockables) that pays out per run
- (casual, strong) Voice/proximity chat built in, or frictionless Discord-style party flow

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- The tagline maps onto a real 2025-26 segment, but that segment is defined by elegance and friend-group co-op, not by genre breadth. Sixteen vessel-locked modes, eleven vessels, four elements and three domains is complexity, not depth; a new player sees a catalogue where Balatro/PEAK players see one thing. Collapse the front door to 2-3 modes and make the rest opt-in 'toys' or unlocks.
- Cosmic Shore's modes are overwhelmingly versus (domain races, duels, hoop courts). GameDiscoverCo's cold-start math says PvP at paid-indie scale plateaus at ~6% of launch CCU and every skill gate makes it worse; the games that won 2025 scale in friend groups. Build a flagship 2-4 player co-op mode where all humans share one domain against the ecosystem (fauna waves, cell control, wildlife) — Brood Rush / Wildlife Liberation inverted. The artificial-life ecosystem is the one asset no friendslop competitor has; it should be the co-op antagonist, not scenery.
- Vessel-locked modes multiply the cold-start problem: each mode is its own tiny matchmaking pool, and a friend who bought the game to fly the Sparrow cannot join a Rhino-only match. At minimum, every launch mode must be playable by every owned vessel or explicitly designed for AI backfill with visible bots (accepted by players per GameDiscoverCo). Design mode eligibility around 'my friend group can always play together'.
- The vehicle/piloting evidence is the bluntest: Rocket Racing (Psyonix, inside Fortnite), KartRider: Drift, Redout 2 and even SEGA's CrossWorlds all under-delivered or died in 2025-26, while Rocket League survives on one objective, one car, 5-minute matches and cross-play. Cosmic Shore's Scarab Scramble / Astro League (ball, hoops, sword-soccer) are the modes closest to that proven shape; Skim Race and the Squirrel's tube-riding are closest to Trackmania's time-attack loop. Lead with those two archetypes and cut or defer the rest for EA.
- Session structure needs to be enforced, not hoped for: every mode should target a ~10-minute match with instant rematch, and Maelstrom (random mode + intensity per round, race to 6) is the closest thing the game has to a Balatro-style 'one more run' container. Make Maelstrom the default play button and make it co-op-team capable.
- $15 is Balatro's price, but Balatro is a 100-hour solo forever game; the multiplayer comparables that reached Platinum tier cost $8. A paid $15 Early Access, Windows-only, English-only, no demo, no cross-play, no Steamworks, no voice, no achievements is the opposite of the friendslop launch profile. Either lower the price toward $8-10 or earn $15 with a solo forever-loop (Maelstrom vs bots with meta-unlocks) that a lone buyer can sink 30+ hours into.
- Cutting Next Fest and a demo contradicts the strongest discovery signal in the data: Balatro's 100K demo downloads and Jump Space's 500K demo players were both credited as decisive, and Zukowski's data calls Next Fest the single biggest wishlist accelerator. Reinstate a demo (Maelstrom vs bots, two vessels) before EA.
- Hardcore players want a nameable mastery ladder and a way to see it: Rocket League's community documents mechanics by rank. Cosmic Shore has genuinely emergent mechanics (drift tube-riding, blade contact velocity, chain spikes, Echo Sight) but no ranked, no ghosts, no replays, no per-run stat sheet. The cheapest hardcore hooks that need zero CCU are Trackmania-style ghost/time-attack leaderboards on Skim Race and a post-match run summary that names what the player pulled off.
- The 4-abilities x 4-elements x level-5-transform contract is the game's 'break the run' engine and its best analogue to joker/build synergy — but it is invisible in the marketing and only partly legible in play. Surface it: show element-combo outcomes on the run summary, seed each match with a build-defining crystal choice, and let the hardcore audience theorycraft it the way they theorycraft jokers.
- Progression as designed (200 crystals/win, 4,000 per vessel = 20 wins per hull; no cosmetics, no achievements) gates the game's own selling point (genre variety) behind a slow drip while offering none of the per-run checklist payoff that VS/Balatro use for retention. Cut vessel unlock costs by 4-5x for EA and add Steam achievements at launch even without the rest of Steamworks; The Conversation's analysis names the achievement checklist as a core retention mechanism.
- Friendslop lives on voice. UGS Friends/party invites exist, but with no in-game voice or proximity chat the 'watch your friend bungle it' loop happens only if the group already lives in Discord. At minimum make the invite-to-lobby flow one click and Discord-rich-presence friendly; ideally add voice.
- Streamer legibility is the other half of virality: R.E.P.O., PEAK and Nubby's succeed on single-screen, readable moments. Abstract prism trails in a 3D volume are hard to read in a 15-second clip. Invest in spectator/replay cameras, a clip-friendly kill-cam for jousts/blasts, and extend the existing WebGL painting share export into a general 'share this run' surface.
- Stop using 'midcore' in positioning: it is a mobile F2P segment term (F2P, 30-50 age band, LTV-driven). The Steam premium vocabulary that matches the tagline is 'easy to learn, hard to master' / 'one more run' / 'play with friends', and the design word behind all of them is elegance.

### Open questions
- What is the measured median match length per mode today, and how many seconds elapse between a match ending and the next one starting (rematch friction)?
- Is there any mode in which all human players share one domain against the ecosystem (true PvE co-op)? If not, which existing mode (Brood Rush, Wildlife Liberation, Salvo) is cheapest to convert?
- Which player is the launch bet: the solo player who sinks 50 hours into Maelstrom vs bots (Balatro/VS shape) or the 3-4 person friend group (friendslop shape)? The two need different front doors, pricing and marketing, and the current plan serves neither fully.
- If forced to ship only three modes in EA, which three — and can every owned vessel play all three?
- Why were Next Fest and a demo cut from the EA plan when the 2025 data credits demos as decisive for Balatro and Jump Space?
- What is the voice/proximity chat plan, and how many clicks does it take for a friend who just bought the game to be in the same match as the buyer who invited them?
- Given Windows-only and no host migration at launch, what happens to a 4-player party when the host drops — and is that failure funny (friendslop) or fatal (rage-quit)?
- Does the team have any external evidence that a mode-collection is what the hardcore audience wants, versus the data here showing they want one deep loop? What would falsify the multigenre thesis?


## LENS: The 2024-2026 friend-group co-op / party hit wave ("friendslop")

The 2024-2026 friend-group wave is the most reliable commercial pattern on Steam right now, and it is brutally consistent. Co-op titles took $4.1B on Steam in H1 2025 (+11% YoY) and every top-5 new 2025 game by copies had co-op; co-op is ~5% of releases but ~46% of copies sold, with a median co-op game selling ~8x a median single-player one. The hits share a template: $5-$10 (PEAK $7.99, R.E.P.O. ~$8, Chained Together $4.99, Lethal Company $10, Meccha Chameleon ~$5-6, How to Fish $7.99/$4.95 launch), a one-sentence premise a viewer understands in a 15-second clip, built-in proximity voice, physics that manufactures failure comedy, 4-player default (scaling 2-8), a 20-50 minute run inside a 1-3 hour session, and a launch engineered as an event for streamers (Content Warning's free 24 hours, Landfall's repeatable model, LORT's low-six-figure influencer spend, YapYap's TikTok-to-Next-Fest pipeline). The median price of top-selling new Steam games fell to $15.64 by Oct 2025 precisely because of this wave.

Co-op is the stronger default, but 2026's biggest game is competitive: Meccha Chameleon (hide-and-seek, 20M copies by Aug 2026, 340k CCU) plus Super Battle Golf and Mage Arena show that "friends interfering with each other" competition works when it is comedic, asymmetric and low-stakes. Skill-ladder PvP does not: Mario Kart World's 15.39M is a Nintendo-console phenomenon (and fans turned on the $80 price), while Sonic Racing CrossWorlds missed SEGA's expectations at 1M, and the legacy PC physics-brawlers (Party Animals, Gang Beasts, Stumble Guys) are shrinking on Steam.

For Cosmic Shore this is uncomfortable. It has the physics, emergent ecology and readable objectives to produce clips, and AI backfill plus a party system are the right bones. But it violates most of the template: $15 (1.5-3x the norm), no in-game voice, ~90% competitive modes locked to single vessels, a quest-chain that gates modes and a 4000-crystal wall on vessels (the wave sells "everything available for the whole group on night one"), no Next Fest, no Steamworks (so no Steam invites, and today no friend-request UI at all), no host migration, and no single one-sentence front door. "A casual game for hardcore gamers" describes what friendslop already is; the product has to earn the "casual" half by being trivially joinable.

### Findings
- [verified] Co-op is the dominant commercial pattern on Steam: $4.1B in H1 2025 (+11% YoY, a record), and all five top new 2025 games by copies sold have co-op. R.E.P.O. had 16.4M copies / $128.2M and PEAK 7M / $42M by end of July 2025; ~50% of R.E.P.O. players had previously played Lethal Company or Phasmophobia (the audience is a reusable cohort).
  - evidence: Alinea Analytics H1 2025 report (read in full): $4.1B co-op revenue, top-5 by copies all co-op, per-game tables, and three named success pillars: social engagement, replayability, creator appeal.
  - sources: https://alineaanalytics.substack.com/p/games-with-co-op-generated-over-4
- [verified] Co-op is ~5% of Steam releases but ~46% of copies sold (2024); the median co-op game sells ~40,000 copies vs ~5,000 for single-player (627% better at the median, 1313% better in the top quartile).
  - evidence: VG Insights cooperative games study summarised by GameDevReports: 5%/46% split for 2024, 6%/36% for 2023, median 40k vs 5k copies; Content Warning 2.5M copies, 204k peak CCU in 2024.
  - sources: https://gamedevreports.substack.com/p/vg-insights-cooperative-games-on
- [likely] The wave pulled Steam's price floor down: the median price of top-selling new Steam games fell from $19.50 to $15.64 (Feb 2023 to Oct 2025) and GameDiscoverCo says charging over $25 is 'getting trickier as players compare value to the $10-$15 indie titles'. Every friend-group hit is $5-$10.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo research reported by GamesRadar and GameDevReports; price points: PEAK $7.99 ($5 launch), R.E.P.O. ~$8, Chained Together $4.99, Lethal Company $10, Content Warning $7.99, Meccha Chameleon ~$5-6, YapYap $10, How to Fish $7.99/$4.95.

- [verified] Built-in proximity voice is the load-bearing mechanic of the wave, not a feature. Lethal Company ($10, solo dev) is described by its creator as 'a game about laughing at death', with proximity chat producing the horror-then-comedy beats that made it stream-native; 10M+ copies / $100M+ from a 611-CCU launch that grew purely by word of mouth. R.E.P.O. added physics to the same voice formula; Mage Arena and YapYap made the microphone itself the input.

  - evidence: Wikipedia 'Friendslop' entry (read in full) and Aftermath essay (read in full, Jul 17 2025).
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friendslop | https://aftermath.site/friendslop-peak-lethal-company-social-media-discourse/
- [likely] Co-op wins structurally because it scales with the friend group and PvP does not: co-op's share of >$100k-revenue Steam games rose from 3.2% (2022) to 5.8% (2023), which GameDiscoverCo attributes to 'easier and more stable upscaling and downscaling' than PvP; Steam-forum sentiment is blunt ('people like PvE more with friends than PvP'), and indie PvP titles routinely die to empty lobbies (Duck Duck Die shut its servers Oct 2025 with zero players in 30 days).
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo description-keyword study (read in full); Steam forum thread; itch.io shutdown post.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/what-game-descriptions-tell-us-about | https://steamcommunity.com/discussions/forum/7/622954023423581408 | https://oxycleanman.itch.io/duck-duck-die
- [likely] Longevity in this genre is update-driven spikes on a small base, not a live-service curve: Lethal Company sits at ~4k CCU in 2026 but its April 2026 content update doubled the monthly peak to 18,989, with 508k reviews at 96% positive; Content Warning is at ~1.3-1.5k CCU two years on; PEAK has declared its last major update. The money is made in the first weeks.
  - evidence: SteamDB/tracker summaries for Lethal Company and Content Warning; PEAK Final Ascent coverage.
  - sources: https://steamdb.info/app/1966720/charts/ | https://activeplayer.io/content-warning/ | https://gamedaily.com/games/peak-final-ascent-last-major-update
- [likely] Discord is becoming infrastructure and distribution for exactly this audience: the Discord Social SDK (Mar 17 2025, Unity/Unreal/C++) added in-game voice, cross-platform messaging and linked channels in Aug 2025, and at GDC 2026 Discord shipped Game Shop / social-commerce and Activities so games can be launched from inside Discord with the voice channel's roster.
  - evidence: Discord press releases and developer newsletter; StraySpark 2026 playbook.
  - sources: https://discord.com/press-releases/social-sdk-ingame-communications | https://discord.com/developer-newsletter/december-2025 | https://www.strayspark.studio/blog/discord-activities-embedded-app-sdk-indie-game-distribution-2026
- [likely] The PC premium competitive party niche still exists but is small and old: Pummel Party (2018, two-person team, 4-8 online/local board-game party) has 2-5M owners and an estimated $13-32M gross — the reference point for 'friends competing without a skill ladder' on PC — while newer competitive friendslop (Super Battle Golf, Feb 19 2026, $12.99, 1-8 players, 100k in 48h, ~19.7k CCU) sells on comedy interference, not ranking.
  - evidence: Steam Spy / games-stats estimates for Pummel Party; Games.gg and Steambase for Super Battle Golf.
  - sources: https://steamspy.com/app/880940 | https://games-stats.com/steam/game/pummel-party/ | https://games.gg/news/super-battle-golf-hits-100k-sales-in-two-days-on-steam/ | https://steambase.io/games/super-battle-golf/steam-charts

### Comparables
- PEAK (Aggro Crab + Landfall) (2025, $7.99 (5.00 at launch)): 1M in 6 days, 7M/$42M by Jul 2025, 10M+ by Dec 2025; 4-player co-op; final major update Aug 2026; PS5/Xbox crossplay announced LESSON: Scope down to one readable loop and price under $10; a 4-player default with a 50-minute run is the shape; declare an end to major updates rather than pretend to be live service.
- R.E.P.O. (semiwork) (2025, $~8): 16.4M copies / $128M by Jul 2025; Golden Joystick Best EA 2025; still PC-only Early Access, no drop-in LESSON: Physics that everyone can grab and break is the clip engine; EA at a low price with 'overwhelmingly positive' reviews is a stronger signal than polish. Late-join absence is its top community complaint.
- Lethal Company (Zeekerss) (2023, $10): 10M+ / $100M+; 240k peak CCU; ~4k baseline CCU in 2026 with update spikes to ~19k LESSON: Voice is the product. Updates create return spikes on a small base; the revenue is front-loaded.
- Content Warning (Landfall) (2024, $7.99 (free first 24h)): 6.2M free claims, 204k CCU, 2.5M paid copies in 2024, consoles Apr 2026 LESSON: A launch moment that gets thousands of friend groups playing simultaneously beats months of steady marketing; plan the first 24 hours as the campaign.
- Chained Together (Anegar Games) (2024, $4.99): 85k CCU launch week; Stream Game of the Year Dec 2024; 10M copies by Jun 2026 LESSON: Design the mechanic so players are forced to talk and blame each other; the game's name should be its pitch.
- Meccha Chameleon (lemorion_1224 + Haganeiro) (2026, $~5-6): 15M in 26 days, 20M by Aug 12 2026, 340,535 peak CCU; 2 devs, ~2 months, UE5, EOS matchmaking LESSON: Competitive works if losing is funny and the mechanic is legible from a thumbnail; big flexible lobbies matter more than balance.
- YapYap (Maison Bap) (2026, $10): 1.15M copies / ~$10M in 12 weeks; #3 Oct 2025 Next Fest; 900k wishlists at launch LESSON: Next Fest is not optional — it converted a viral clip into 900k wishlists. Skipping it is leaving the single largest free discovery event on the table.
- LORT (Big Distraction) (2026, $15 (10 at launch)): 281k units in two weeks, #4 2026 Steam debut, ~10k CCU; low-six-figure influencer spend LESSON: $15 is survivable with a $10 launch discount, 8-player scaling, a deliberate 'PvE alternative to PvP' pitch, and real creator spend.
- Split Fiction (Hazelight / EA) (2025, $49.99 (Friend's Pass free)): 1M in 48h, 7M by Apr 2026; Hazelight 50M lifetime LESSON: One purchase should bring the whole friend group; a guest/friend pass is cheaper than a price cut and Cosmic Shore's AI backfill already means content is not gated by seats.
- Mario Kart World (Nintendo) (2025, $80): 15.39M by Jun 30 2026; Knockout Tour acclaimed; price and content-drip backlash LESSON: Knockout-style elimination rounds are the modern competitive-party format; but this sells on a Nintendo console with local play — not evidence that PC competitive racing sells.
- Sonic Racing: CrossWorlds (SEGA) / Kirby Air Riders (2025, $60): 1M by Nov 2025, 'did not meet expectations'; Kirby 1.9M by Jun 2026 LESSON: A competitive racer needs a reason to exist beyond quality; Cosmic Shore's Squirrel/Skim Race cannot be the front door on its own.
- Party Animals / Gang Beasts / Stumble Guys (2023-2026, $19.99 / 19.99 / F2P): Steam CCU shrinking: Party Animals 31k peak (Oct 2025) to 3.6k avg (Aug 2026); Gang Beasts ~400 avg; Stumble Guys IAP -69% YoY LESSON: Competitive party without a viral premise decays fast; retention needs the co-op/friend-group hook, not more minigames.
- Pummel Party (Rebuilt Games) (2018, $14.99): 2-5M owners, ~$13-32M gross est., 88% positive from 57k reviews LESSON: Mode variety works when every round is 2-5 minutes, every friend plays every round, and nothing is locked.
- Bombanana! (Lefto Studio) (2026, $7.99 (4.95 launch)): #1 demo June 2026 Next Fest (40k CCU, 675k uniques); launched Sep 2 2026 LESSON: Asymmetric roles create forced communication; Cosmic Shore's multi-vessel fleet could be a role-asymmetry system rather than eleven separate genres.
- Phasmophobia (Kinetic Games) (2020-2026, $19.99): 22M total; 3M console by Apr 2025; 1.0 and Switch 2 in 2026 LESSON: Early Access can run for years if the core is social; crossplay/console is the expansion, not the launch.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) Built-in proximity voice chat (spatial, in-game, default on)
- (both, must-have) Price at or under $10 (or a $15 list with a $10 launch price)
- (both, must-have) A one-sentence premise readable in a 15-second clip; the mechanic is the pitch
- (both, must-have) 4-player default that scales 2-8 without breaking (AI fill for absent friends)
- (casual, strong) Everything unlocked for the whole group on night one — no progression gates before friends can play the good modes
- (casual, strong) Failure comedy: physics that makes losing funny and shareable
- (casual, must-have) Co-op vs the world as the default; competition only when it is asymmetric, comedic and low-stakes
- (both, strong) Late join / drop-in and sessions with natural stop points (20-50 min runs, sub-10-min rounds)
- (both, must-have) Frictionless party formation where friends already are (Steam invites, Discord)
- (casual, strong) A Friend's Pass / guest slots so one purchase brings the group
- (both, strong) A launch engineered as an event for streamers (free window, seeded keys, Next Fest demo)
- (both, nice-to-have) Console + crossplay within 12-18 months
- (hardcore, strong) Regular content updates that give the group a reason to return (new biome/mode/creature), not cosmetics
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) Depth for the hardcore member of the group (skill ceiling, mastery) without a ranked ladder

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Price: $15 is 1.5-3x the wave's norm and above the $15.64 median of top-selling new Steam games. The only $15 comparable that worked (LORT) launched at $10 with 8-player scaling and paid creator seeding. Either list at $9.99, or list $14.99 with a hard $9.99 launch price and be ready to stay there.
- Voice: Cosmic Shore has no in-game voice. Every hit in this wave has proximity voice and several use the microphone as the core verb. This is the single largest gap versus the market. UGS ships Vivox; Discord Social SDK supports Unity. Ship spatial voice at EA, default on, with per-domain team channels — proximity voice in a 3D arena (you hear teammates only when close) is a comedy engine in Astro League, Scarab Scramble and Brood Rush for free.
- Party formation is a launch blocker: no Steamworks SDK means no Steam friends/invites, and the friends panel currently has no UI to send a friend request at all. A friend group cannot reliably form a party on night one. Add at minimum a join code plus Discord invite links (Social SDK), or reverse the no-Steamworks decision for invites only.
- Co-op is the safer spread mechanism and Cosmic Shore is ~90% competitive with modes locked to one vessel. The ecology (fauna waves, cells, nucleus control) is a ready-made PvE substrate: ship one co-op-vs-the-cell mode (all humans on one domain, AI or ecology as the opponent) as the front door, and let Maelstrom's competitive suite be the second night.
- The friend-group case is four humans; with three domains a party of four becomes 2/1/1 or 2v2 with AI. Make 2-domain (2v2) the default for four-human lobbies, and let 6-8 humans in — R.E.P.O. (6), Windrose (8), Meccha (2-12) show the group is often bigger than four.
- Unlock gating contradicts the category: quest-chain-locked modes and 4000-crystal vessels (with 200 crystals per win) mean a group cannot play the good stuff together on night one. Unlock everything for Early Access; if progression must exist, make it cosmetic-adjacent or vessel mastery, never access.
- Legibility: eleven vessels x sixteen modes x four elements is not a 15-second clip. Choose ONE mode+vessel as the marketed identity (the Scarab hoop court or the Dolphin skim-and-blast are the most clip-legible) and let the rest be discovered. Ask for every trailer: can a viewer state the rule in one sentence?
- Reinstate Steam Next Fest. YapYap converted a viral TikTok into 900k wishlists via Next Fest; Bombanana went #1 of ~4,400 demos to launch. Cutting Next Fest removes the largest free discovery event available to a game with no Steamworks marketing surface.
- Plan the launch as an event: a free 24-48 hour window or a Friend's Pass (one copy brings three friends for the session; AI backfill already means no content is seat-gated) plus keys seeded to multiplayer-focused creators (LORT: low six figures; Mage Arena: 500+ streamers organically). Windows-only Steam EA is fine; the wave is Steam-first.
- Drop-in and host loss: the community's top complaint about R.E.P.O./PEAK is no late join. Cosmic Shore's lobby + AI backfill can be a genuine differentiator — but host migration was cut, so the host alt-tabbing kills the match for everyone. Re-prioritise host migration (or host-loss round recovery) above new vessels.
- Round length is right, session container needs work: sub-10-minute rounds inside Maelstrom's race-to-6 matches the Mario Kart/Pummel Party shape. Make the between-round lobby instant and keep the group together across modes without a re-invite.
- The tagline 'a casual game for hardcore gamers' describes friendslop's actual audience (young, 'normie', plays what streamers play) but the product must earn 'casual': today it is casual to fly and hardcore to enter. Fix entry friction before spending on the slogan.
- Clip capture: the connect-the-dots WebGL share is the right instinct but is not a clip. Add a 'save last 30 seconds' replay export or at least a one-key replay camera moment; the wave is marketed by players' clips, not by trailers.
- Expectations: front-load. Lethal Company/PEAK/Content Warning made their money in the first weeks and now run at 1-4k CCU with update spikes. A 12-month EA roadmap of 'more vessels/modes/episodes' should be re-cut as three or four dated content events that give groups a reason to reassemble.

### Open questions
- What is the measured median match length and lobby-to-lobby turnaround in Cosmic Shore today (PostHog), and does a 4-human party stay together across a Maelstrom session without re-inviting?
- Can UGS Vivox or the Discord Social SDK be integrated for spatial voice within the EA window given Netcode for GameObjects + Relay, and what is the per-CCU cost at 10k concurrent?
- Is dropping Steamworks entirely a hard constraint (legal/time) or a scope choice? Steam invites alone would remove the party-formation blocker; achievements are irrelevant.
- Which single mode+vessel produces the most legible 15-second clip in playtests — Scarab Scramble, Dolphin Rampage/Bends, or Astro League — and can that mode be the Next Fest demo?
- Would a Friend's Pass (host owns, up to 3 guests free per session) be technically possible with anonymous UGS auth and no IAP, and how does it interact with AI backfill and crystal rewards?
- How large are Cosmic Shore's actual friend groups (party size distribution from TestFlight/itch builds)? If most parties are 2-3, the 3-domain design is less of a problem than assumed.
- Does the ecology produce reliably funny failure moments (fauna eating your trail, a blast backfiring) at friendslop density, or does emergent behaviour read as noise to a spectator? This decides whether 'failure comedy' is available without new physics jank.
- What is the plan for host loss without host migration in a 4-human party, and how often do EA playtests end a match that way?
- Is the young (under-35) friendslop cohort actually the target, or is the 'hardcore gamer' in the tagline older and more solo-oriented? The two audiences want different entry flows.


## LENS: Arcade racer, space-combat and vehicle-skill games market

The vehicle-skill market in 2025-26 splits cleanly into two populations, and Cosmic Shore is currently built for the one that dies. Population A is single-player-complete skill games with ghosts, medals and leaderboards: Distance, Hyper Demon, Sky Rogue, House of the Dying Sun, Everspace 2, Tokyo Xtreme Racer. They all fall to 10-300 Steam CCU within months and stay Very Positive for years, because nobody needs a lobby. Population B is lobby-dependent multiplayer: Rematch fell from a 91,374 Steam peak (June 2025) to a 2,226 monthly average by March 2026 despite 5M unique players and a DICE award; Star Wars Squadrons went 35,721 (Oct 2020) to under 100 by 2026 after EA said "you've paid the $40, this is the game"; Redout 2, an AA anti-grav racer with a publisher, never exceeded 286 CCU. GameDiscoverCo's rule of thumb (multiplayer stabilizes at ~6% of launch CCU, 10-20% best case) held or was beaten on the downside by every comparable. The one counter-example, Trackmania, grew its Steam average from 882 (Jan 2024) to ~1,400 (Aug 2026) on a design that is the literal definition of "casual game for hardcore gamers": sub-minute attempts, 1.5-second restarts, four medal tiers per track, a ghost as guide, "drivers near you" rankings, and a curated Track of the Day every single day - all free, with $19.99/yr for the extras.

What this says about Cosmic Shore: the fundamentals (11 hull genres, emergent ecosystem, prismscape trails) are genuinely differentiated, but the package is a Population-B game - 16 modes, most locked to one vessel, split by 4 intensities and 3 domains, shipping to Windows-only Steam EA at $15 with no ranked, no cosmetics, missions cut, and one weekly challenge attempt per day. At realistic EA scale (the $24 THQ-backed Wreckfest 2 EA peaked at 1,901 CCU and settled at ~180-250), no public matchmaking pool in Cosmic Shore will ever fill; the AI backfill is not a fallback, it is the game. The daily loop must be re-cut Trackmania-style: every mode a sub-2-minute solo attempt against ghosts and a medal ladder, a seeded "Track of the Day" with a leaderboard, unlimited retries, and 1.0 treated as a footnote (only 20% of 2025 EA graduates did better at 1.0; median 1.0 revenue was 40% of EA). Also non-negotiable for this audience: uncapped framerate, remappable controls, one-thumb hulls in front, and no grind-gated vessels in a paid game.

### Findings
- [verified] Trackmania is the one vehicle-skill comparable whose Steam population GREW over 2024-2026, on a daily-content, sub-minute-attempt model: monthly average CCU rose from 882 (Jan 2024) to 1,398 (Aug 2026) with peaks of 3.3k-5.9k; all-time Steam peak 21,864 (Nov 2023).
  - evidence: SteamCharts monthly table: 2024 avg 725-1,145; 2025 avg 1,195-1,822 (peak 5,422 in Jul 2025); 2026 avg 1,399-1,627 (peak 5,922 Jan 2026). Steam is a minority storefront for Trackmania (Ubisoft Connect + consoles carry most players), so these are a floor. Store page: Mostly Positive, 79% of 14,470 reviews; free-to-play with $19.99/yr Club Access.
  - sources: https://steamcharts.com/app/2225070 | https://store.steampowered.com/app/2225070/Trackmania/
- [verified] Trackmania's 'casual for hardcore' loop is a specific, copyable mechanism: near-instant restart (1.5 s countdown), attempts rarely over one minute, four medal tiers per track (bronze/silver/gold/author) so every skill level has a target, a ghost that doubles as a guide, then 'drivers near you' rankings escalating to global leaderboards.
  - evidence: Todd Parker (Jan 19 2025): 'At any time, you can press a button which restarts the track near instantaneously'; 'Now real people play the role of being your target to beat and your guide to improve and this can go all the way up to being the best in the world.' Trackmania's own 2023 tiering: free players keep Ranked, Royal and 10 campaign tracks per season; Track of the Day / Cup of the Day are the paid hook. A 2021 dataset of Track-of-the-Day medals shows the ladder absorbs both extremes (one map's author time achieved by 12 players; another's by nearly everyone).
  - sources: https://tparkergames.substack.com/p/trackmania-and-the-one-more-run-feeling | https://www.trackmania.com/news/7739 | https://vjeux.github.io/trackmania-totd-medal-distribution/
- [verified] Rematch (Sloclap, $29.99, June 19 2025) is the 2025 reference case for a hit skill-multiplayer game bleeding out: Steam CCU fell from a 91,374 peak (June 2025) to a 2,226 monthly average in March 2026 (-95% on average CCU), despite 1M sales in 5 days, 5M unique players in a month and a 2026 D.I.C.E. Sports Game of the Year.
  - evidence: SteamCharts: June 2025 avg 42,573 / peak 91,374; Aug 2025 avg 8,037; Nov 2025 avg 2,742; Mar 2026 avg 2,226; seasonal rebound Jul 2026 avg 5,293; last 30 days avg 3,464. Store page today: Mostly Positive 70% of 16,994 reviews, Season 4. Cited causes: netcode/lag, crashes, inconsistent matchmaking, cheaters, no crossplay until Sept 2025, no cross-progression ('no plans').
  - sources: https://steamcharts.com/app/2138720 | https://gamerant.com/rematch-player-count-down/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rematch_(video_game) | https://store.steampowered.com/app/2138720/REMATCH/ | https://gamingbolt.com/rematch-reaches-5-million-unique-players
- [verified] Star Wars: Squadrons' multiplayer died because it shipped as a $40 'self-contained' box with no post-launch plan and no retention hook; Steam went from a 35,721 peak (Oct 2020) to a 1,016 average by Jan 2021 and 48 average by Aug 2026.
  - evidence: Ian Frazier, Oct 8 2020: 'we're not trying to treat the game as a live service'; 'You've paid the $40, this is the game and it's entirely self-contained'; 'We're not planning to add more content.' Steam players three weeks after launch: 'does lack a retention hook which would make people commit to it long term'; 'the FPS lock issue is killing this game' for high-refresh/VR users; 'should have been a singleplayer/co-op game'. By Jan 2022 queues were 2-5 min only in NA peak hours and Fleet Battle vs AI was dead.
  - sources: https://www.nme.com/news/gaming-news/no-plans-for-star-wars-squadrons-post-launch-content-says-ea-2775974 | https://steamcharts.com/app/1222730 | https://steamcommunity.com/app/1222730/discussions/0/3013431044274641409/ | https://steamcommunity.com/app/1222730/discussions/0/3202622271465473065/
- [verified] Redout 2 - a polished, publisher-backed (Saber/Embracer) anti-gravity racer with 36 tracks, 12-player online and a 'super-high skill ceiling' - never exceeded 286 concurrent Steam players and sits at ~14 average in 2025; players blame a brutal difficulty curve, tutorials that gate progression, dual-stick steering, launch bugs and marketing spent on one YouTuber.
  - evidence: SteamCharts: all-time peak 286 (June 2022); Aug 2025 avg 11.95. Store: Very Positive 82% of 1,159 reviews, $19.99 (50% off at $9.99). Community thread (Apr 2024): ~100k copies estimated; 'released the game in a poor state with collision bugs and huge balance issues'; 'Saber has pulled everyone from Redout 2 to another project'. Reviews: 'The difficulty curve is insane'; 'having to use both thumbsticks to turn' is 'the most consistently difficult aspect of control'. GameSensor reported ~50k copies/~$1M in month one.
  - sources: https://steamcharts.com/app/1799930 | https://store.steampowered.com/app/1799930/Redout_2/ | https://steamcommunity.com/app/1799930/discussions/0/4361247268673572209/ | https://gamesensor.info/news/redout_2 | https://cogconnected.com/review/redout-2-review-the-fast-the-furious-and-frustrating/
- [verified] Every vehicle-skill comparable that is still healthy years later at tiny concurrency is single-player-complete with ghosts/leaderboards: Distance (93% of 4,218 reviews, ~16 avg CCU, all-time peak 452), Hyper Demon (94% of ~2.7-3.2k reviews, ~9 avg CCU, peak 346), Sky Rogue (92% of 1,056), House of the Dying Sun (89% of 1,938, 14 scenarios, Steam leaderboards, tagged 'Short').
  - evidence: Distance store page: Arcade mode 'ghost racing and leaderboard competitions with medal unlocks', level editor with 2,500+ Workshop tracks, 12-player online, split-screen; $24.99. Hyper Demon: 'A replay feature is seamlessly baked in so you can watch any player on the leaderboard's best run'; SteamSpy ~200k owners (estimate). SteamCharts Aug 2025/2026 averages as stated. None of these depends on a live pool to be worth $20-25.
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/233610/Distance/ | https://steamcharts.com/app/233610 | https://steamcharts.com/app/1743850 | https://steamspy.com/app/1743850 | https://store.steampowered.com/app/381020/Sky_Rogue/ | https://store.steampowered.com/app/283160/House_of_the_Dying_Sun/ | https://www.gamespot.com/reviews/hyper-demon-review-slayer/1900-6417967/
- [verified] Single-player space combat can still sell at premium prices from a small team if Early Access is run as a real launch: Everspace 2 (25 people) did 300,000 Steam units / ~$10M gross by mid-2023 at $39.99-$49.99, peaked at 9,735 CCU at 1.0 (Apr 2023), and its 2025 expansion lifted it back to an 884 peak in Jan 2026.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo deep dive: 260k wishlists at EA start; 'having a time-unlimited, always up-to-date demo throughout Early Access helped tremendously to manage expectations and reduce the negative reviews'; only a 25% discount ever; PC Game Pass 'no negatives' for Steam sales; 88% positive. SteamCharts: 2026 monthly avg 191-367.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/deep-dive-how-everspace-2-made-its | https://steamcharts.com/app/1128920 | https://gameworldobserver.com/2023/06/16/everspace-2-interview-rockfish-early-access-sales-game-pass
- [verified] Steam Early Access '1.0 bump' is mostly gone: of 225 games that left EA in 2025, only 20% (45) sold better at 1.0 than in EA; median 30-day revenue at 1.0 was ~40% of median 30-day EA revenue (down from ~70% in 2023); median EA duration 437 days.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo, Dec 9 2025: 'the concept that a slow launch can be redeemed by a 1.0 release is increasingly out of step with reality.' Corroborated by Tokyo Xtreme Racer's trajectory: EA peak 15,655 (Jan 2025) vs 1.0 peak 12,009 (Sept 2025, price $29.99 to $49.99), then 174 avg by Aug 2026.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/the-state-of-steam-early-access-graduates | https://steamcharts.com/app/2634950 | https://www.gematsu.com/2025/09/tokyo-xtreme-racer-launches-september-25
- [verified] The realistic ceiling for a mid-priced racing Early Access on Steam without a franchise is ~2,000 CCU at launch settling to a couple of hundred: Wreckfest 2 (Bugbear/THQ Nordic, $24 rising to $30, EA Mar 20 2025) peaked at 1,901 and averaged 127-275 for the following 17 months, with 72% of 3,558 reviews positive and only 4 maps / 4 cars at EA launch.
  - evidence: SteamCharts monthly table Mar 2025-Aug 2026. Steam store review counts. Coverage headline: 'Wreckfest 2 Launched Today, But Maybe It Shouldn't Have' (The Drive).
  - sources: https://steamcharts.com/app/1203190 | https://steamcommunity.com/app/1203190/reviews/ | https://www.thedrive.com/news/wreckfest-2-launched-today-but-maybe-it-shouldnt-have
- [verified] Industry analysts converge on the same warning for small-team multiplayer: PC/console multiplayer games typically stabilize at ~6% of launch CCU (10-20% best case), and the only structural mitigations are smaller 'atomic networks' (co-op, fewer players per match, mixed-skill play) - or making a single-player game first.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo 'cold start problem' (May 17 2023): 'we're advocating for more online co-op in everyone's games, but not necessarily more traditional vs. multiplayer'; Overwatch 2 cut 6v6 to 5v5 for matchmaking. HowToMarketAGame (Jul 3 2023): 'There is a serious bootstrapping problem where to have a self-sustaining multiplayer game you need players and you can't get players without having players'; Battlebit took 4 years to reach 100 CCU; 'If you are planning to make a multiplayer game, dont! Make a single player game (if this is your first game).' GameDiscoverCo also relays Farbs' advice that obvious bots are fine - 'players don't mind' (Fortnite, .io games).
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/analysis-multiplayer-game-discovery | https://howtomarketagame.com/2023/07/03/how-to-market-a-multiplayer-the-battlebit-remastered-story/
- [likely] Rocket League is aging rather than dying, but the under-Epic story is an object lesson in what hardcore players punish: trading removed Dec 5 2023 to serve cross-game cosmetics (petition-level backlash, Rocket League Garage's 5M-user community declared 'fraudulent'), a Dec 2024 esports piece describing falling attendance and viewership, and persistent 'smurfs are everywhere at every rank' complaints from new players; Steam-only CCU (delisted for new buyers since 2020) slid from a 19,284 average (Dec 2023) to 15,100 (Aug 2025).
  - evidence: Windows Central / Rocket League Garage on the trading removal; Northern Light (Dec 11 2024): 'One-time changes like making the game free-to-play aren't sustainable in continuing to increase player count'; Steam discussions: 'The amount of smurfing is out of hand'. SteamCharts Steam-only table. Third-party claims of 55-60M MAU / 1.09M all-platform peak in Jan 2026 come from low-quality aggregator sites and are NOT verified.
  - sources: https://www.windowscentral.com/gaming/epic-games-and-psyonix-earn-a-backlash-for-removing-this-popular-rocket-league-feature | https://rocket-league.com/news/the-end-of-trading-or-rocket-league | https://www.thenorthernlight.org/stories/can-professional-rocket-league-esports-be-saved | https://steamcharts.com/app/252950 | https://steamcommunity.com/app/252950/discussions/0/5913784177858450776
- [verified] Even a Very-Positive, Metacritic-84 kart racer with time trials + global leaderboards, ranked 'Legend Competitions', crossplay and 12-player online (Sonic Racing: CrossWorlds, Sept 25 2025, $69.99, 93% of 8,486 Steam reviews) 'did not meet' SEGA's expectations at 1M units by Nov 2025, versus Mario Kart World's 9.57M by Dec 31 2025 at $80 - quality and feature completeness did not overcome an entrenched incumbent.
  - evidence: Push Square (Feb 2026): SEGA - 'initial performance did not meet our expectations'; target another 1M by Mar 31 2026 via 'ongoing release of additional downloadable content'. Shacknews/Nintendo: MKW 9.57M. Meanwhile MKW's own hardcore fans criticise it: intermission tracks feel like 'waiting around for the actual race to start', 'free roam doesn't add much', 'severe lack of new content since launch'.
  - sources: https://www.pushsquare.com/news/2026/02/sonic-racing-crossworlds-initial-sales-didnt-meet-segas-expectations | https://store.steampowered.com/app/2486820/Sonic_Racing_CrossWorlds/ | https://www.shacknews.com/article/146659/mario-kart-world-november-2025-nintendo-sales | https://www.operationsports.com/mario-kart-world-sells-big-but-some-fans-say-its-the-most-disappointing-entry-ever/
- [verified] F-Zero 99 shows what keeps a niche vehicle multiplayer alive past its novelty: free with the subscription, bot backfill that keeps 99-slot races full, and content updates 19 months on (v1.6.0, Apr 22 2025: five mirror tracks, five classic tracks, a Mini World Tour, cosmetics) - a player reports 'a mere handful of fellow humans and 90-odd bots' yet still races.
  - evidence: Nintendo Life, Apr 22 2025 update notes and comments; one commenter: 'F-Zero 99 has been supported for a longer period of time than Splatoon 1'. No official player numbers exist.
  - sources: https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2025/04/f-zero-99-has-been-updated-to-version-1-6-0-heres-what-you-can-expect
- [likely] Descenders is the vehicle-skill indie that scaled without a lobby: procedural tracks + Game Pass took it to 250,000 units / >$5M and ~3.5M players by Nov 2020 (250k new Game Pass players per month); its sequel Descenders Next (EA Jul 2025) is at 79% of 384 reviews with players saying they 'weren't really playing together' in multiplayer and the developer apologising while 'working on getting the feel and flow of Descenders back'.
