  - evidence: Pure Xbox / ResetEra (2020) sales figures and Mike Rose: 'Game Pass was a major turning point for the game'. Steam discussions and PC Gamer (2025) on Descenders Next's multiplayer and feel.
  - sources: https://www.purexbox.com/news/2020/09/descenders_approaches_3_million_players_with_xbox_game_pass | https://www.resetera.com/threads/descenders-has-sold-over-250-000-units-nearly-3-5-million-people-have-now-played-descenders-across-all-platforms.324676/ | https://steamcommunity.com/app/2375530/discussions/0/597394373362083881/ | https://www.pcgamer.com/games/racing/descenders-next-gets-a-big-multiplayer-update-as-its-developer-apologises-for-taking-so-long-with-it-were-slowly-working-on-getting-the-feel-and-flow-of-descenders-back-into-descenders-next/
- [likely] Publisher-scale space-combat single-player also fails when the loop is thin: Chorus (Fishlabs/Deep Silver, Dec 3 2021) sits at 81% of 2,340 Steam reviews but Embracer said it 'had not reached sales expectations', and ~50 Fishlabs staff were laid off in 2023; Riders Republic (Ubisoft, 64-player) peaked at only 8,043 Steam CCU (June 2025) and averaged 214-1,129 across 2025-26.
  - evidence: Chorus Steam store page; VGC/ResetEra on layoffs and Embracer statement. SteamCharts Riders Republic table.
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/1153640/Chorus/ | https://www.videogameschronicle.com/news/sources-embracer-lays-off-50-at-chorus-developer-fishlabs/ | https://steamcharts.com/app/2290180
- [likely] Indie 'Rocket League-likes' and other small-team competitive live-service games mostly shut down within ~2 years: Omega Strikers (Odyssey) and Knockout City (Velan) both ended public servers in June 2023, Rocket Arena in March 2024; the survivors (Rocket League, Fall Guys) survived by being acquired by Epic.
  - evidence: ResetEra shutdown threads; Medium analysis: 'Too many games-as-a-service titles compete in an already crowded space... it's usually because of acquisition' that indie live-service survives.
  - sources: https://www.resetera.com/threads/knockout-city-to-shut-down-june-6th-player-host-pc-version-will-be-released-so-fans-can-keep-the-game-alive.682792/ | https://www.resetera.com/threads/rocket-arena-will-shut-down-march-21st-2024.795525/ | https://medium.com/@averybarker5/omega-strikers-is-dying-a-sad-fate-for-independent-games-0f2bab038f16
- [likely] PC hardcore vehicle players treat technical baseline as review-determining: an FPS lock was named as 'killing' Squadrons on PC three weeks in; Steam users cite '60 fps lock' and 'no achievements' as refund reasons; Redout 2 players blamed poor controller handling until they swapped to premium pads; Sonic Racing praise centred on 'smooth drifting and air tricks'.
  - evidence: Squadrons Steam thread (Oct 2020); PC Gamer refund-abuse coverage quoting a refund citing 'no achievements, 60 fps lock, and forced launcher'; Redout 2 review on controller quality; Tom's Guide on CrossWorlds drift feel.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/1222730/discussions/0/3013431044274641409/ | https://www.pcgamer.com/gaming-industry/indie-dev-defends-criticism-of-steam-refund-abuse-amid-review-bombing-of-his-game-its-wrong-to-refund-a-game-after-having-fun-with-it-and-completing-it/ | https://www.tech-gaming.com/redout-2/ | https://www.tomsguide.com/gaming/sonic-racing-crossworlds-review
- [likely] Free + single-player-first is how a tiny kart racer got half a million downloads: Nightmare Kart (LWMedia, 2024) is 97% positive on ~4,300 reviews with 500k+ downloads while showing ~4 concurrent players - a signal that goodwill and reach are cheap when there is no pool to protect, and that CCU is a meaningless health metric for a solo-first game.
  - evidence: GamesRadar and Steambase figures; Steam page Overwhelmingly Positive.
  - sources: https://www.gamesradar.com/games/racing/as-bloodborne-pc-port-demands-intensify-once-again-its-free-unofficial-mario-kart-spin-off-becomes-one-of-the-highest-rated-steam-games-of-the-month/ | https://steambase.io/games/nightmare-kart/steam-charts

### Comparables
- Trackmania (Ubisoft Nadeo) (2020 / Steam 2023, $Free + $19.99/yr Club Access): Only comparable growing on Steam 2024-2026 (avg 882 to ~1,400 CCU); console launch 'surpassed our highest expectations' (Oct 2023) LESSON: Cadence beats content volume: one curated track every day plus a four-tier medal ladder and a ghost on everything sustains a niche for 20 years. Cosmic Shore's weekly challenge with one attempt per day is the opposite of 'one more run'.
- Rocket League (Psyonix/Epic) (2015 / F2P 2020, $Free (was $19.99)): Still top-tier but flat-to-declining; trading removed Dec 2023 with backlash; esports shrinking (Dec 2024); smurfing dominates new-player complaints LESSON: It scaled because of PS Plus giveaway + 3v3 atomic network + one mode everyone plays; it aged when the economy was changed against the community and the skill floor for newcomers went unmanaged. Do not fragment one great sport across two vessel-locked modes.
- Rematch (Sloclap) (2025, $$29.99): 1M sold in 5 days, 5M unique players; Steam CCU -95% (91,374 peak to 2,226 avg) in 9 months; 70% Mostly Positive LESSON: A skill-sport with only lobby play has no floor; when the pool thins the game is worth nothing to the remaining buyer. At Cosmic Shore's scale, the AI-backfilled match must be as good as the human one.
- Star Wars: Squadrons (EA Motive) (2020, $$39.99): 35,721 Steam peak to ~1,000 avg in 3 months and <100 in 2026; 'no post-launch content' LESSON: 'You've paid the $40, this is the game' plus an FPS lock killed a licensed space-combat multiplayer in a quarter. A 12-month EA with visible drops is the antidote, but only if the drops are daily/weekly loops, not new modes.
- Redout 2 (34BigThings/Saber) (2022, $$19.99 (often $9.99)): All-time Steam peak 286 CCU; ~50-100k copies; 82% positive; team pulled off by publisher LESSON: Skill ceiling without an on-ramp loses the hardcore too: brutal tutorials gating progression and dual-stick steering were the top complaints. Cosmic Shore's dual-stick hulls and level-5 transformations need a one-thumb, medal-laddered on-ramp.
- Distance (Refract) (2018 (EA 2014), $$24.99): 93% of 4,218 reviews; 2,500+ Workshop tracks; ~16 avg CCU but a healthy 8-year tail LESSON: A vehicle-skill indie survives on ghosts, medals, an editor and a campaign; online is a bonus. Its live pool has been ~15 players for years and nobody cares.
- Hyper Demon (Sorath) (2022, $$24.99): 94% positive, ~200k owners (est.), ~9 avg CCU LESSON: Leaderboards + watchable replays are enough progression for hardcore players; no ranked, no seasons, no cosmetics required.
- Everspace 2 (Rockfish) (EA 2021 / 1.0 2023, $$39.99-$49.99): 300k Steam units / ~$10M by mid-2023; 88% positive; 2025 expansion revived it LESSON: Single-player space combat sells at premium prices when the loop is complete; a permanent demo through EA 'reduced negative reviews'. Cosmic Shore's freestyle hub is a demo waiting to be one.
- Chorus (Fishlabs/Deep Silver) (2021, $$24.99 (launched $39.99)): 81% positive but 'had not reached sales expectations'; ~50 layoffs in 2023 LESSON: Novel powers do not sell space combat on their own; the loop and the hook (why come back tomorrow) do.
- Sky Rogue (Fractal Phase) / House of the Dying Sun (Marauder) (2017 / 2016, $$19.99 each): 92% of 1,056 and 89% of 1,938 reviews respectively; both single-player, both alive LESSON: At $20, ~2-4 hours of tight, replayable skill content with leaderboards earns Very Positive for a decade. Depth of one loop beats breadth of sixteen.
- F-Zero 99 (Nintendo) (2023, updated Apr 2025, $Free with NSO): Still supported 19 months on; lobbies reportedly '90-odd bots' at times LESSON: Bots that fill the room without pretending are accepted; the update cadence, not the human count, is what players notice.
- Sonic Racing: CrossWorlds (SEGA) vs Mario Kart World (Nintendo) (2025, $$69.99 vs $79.99): 1M vs 9.57M units; SEGA: 'did not meet our expectations' despite 93%/Metacritic 84 LESSON: Feature parity is table stakes, not differentiation. Cosmic Shore's differentiation must be the thing nobody else has (living arena, trail-as-track, 11 genres in one physics), and it must be legible in 10 seconds of footage.
- Descenders / Descenders Next (RageSquid/No More Robots) (2019 / EA 2025, $$24.99 / $29.99): 250k units, 3.5M players via Game Pass (2020); sequel 79% with 'feel and flow' apology LESSON: Procedural tracks + a run structure make a solo vehicle game infinite; subscriptions (Game Pass) are the distribution play for a niche skill game once it has a solo loop. Handling 'feel' is the product - lose it and reviews collapse.
- Wreckfest 2 (Bugbear/THQ Nordic) / Tokyo Xtreme Racer (Genki) (EA 2025, $$24-30 / $29.99-49.99): Peaks 1,901 and 15,655 CCU; both settled to ~175-250 avg within months LESSON: Even with a brand, Steam racing EA peaks in the low thousands and lives in the low hundreds; content-light EA (4 maps / 4 cars) draws 72% reviews. Plan the whole EA around a few hundred concurrent players, mostly solo.
- Omega Strikers / Knockout City / Rocket Arena (2022-2024, $Free / $19.99 / $29.99): All shut down within ~2 years LESSON: Indie competitive live-service without a solo floor dies on the pool, regardless of quality; the survivors were acquired.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) A sub-2-minute attempt with instant restart and no lobby: play, fail, retry in under two seconds, alone, any time
- (both, must-have) A tiered target on every activity (bronze/silver/gold/author or equivalent), a ghost that shows how, and a leaderboard that starts with 'drivers near you' before 'global'
- (both, strong) Curated daily/weekly content that rotates (Track of the Day, weekly Shorts, seasonal campaign) rather than new modes
- (both, must-have) Solo-complete value: a campaign/run structure or arcade ladder that justifies the price with zero other humans online
- (both, must-have) Honest bot backfill that keeps every mode playable at 3 a.m. with one human
- (both, strong) Readable, learnable handling with a real on-ramp: no two-stick steering as the default, tutorials that never gate progression, difficulty that scales per event
- (hardcore, strong) Uncapped/high framerate, full controller + rebinding support, no forced launcher; Steam achievements expected by default
- (hardcore, strong) Deterministic vessels for time-attack: a leaderboard run must be comparable run to run (fixed loadout or seeded pickups), or the leaderboard is noise
- (both, must-have) Concentrated matchmaking: one or two queues everyone shares, small team sizes, mixed skill allowed; not sixteen vessel-locked pools
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) Watchable replays / shareable ghosts and clips as the social layer
- (hardcore, strong) Track/UGC editor with Workshop sharing as the long-tail content engine
- (casual, nice-to-have) Cosmetic goals (skins, cards, medals on profile) that are earned, never traded away; economies changed against players are punished
- (both, strong) Crossplay and cross-progression the day multiplayer launches on a second platform
- (casual, nice-to-have) A permanent, always-current demo during Early Access

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Plan every EA number around a few hundred concurrent players, mostly solo. The two 2025 racing EAs nearest your plan (Wreckfest 2 at $24 with THQ Nordic, Tokyo Xtreme Racer at $30 with Genki's name) peaked at 1,901 and 15,655 and both live at ~175-250 CCU; Redout 2 never passed 286. Sixteen modes x 4 intensities x 3 domains cannot be filled by that; the public-matchmaking story should be de-emphasised and the AI-backfilled match presented as the real product, with Relay parties for friends.
- Re-cut the daily loop to Trackmania's shape, because that is what 'a casual game for hardcore gamers' literally means: (a) a seeded Skim Race 'Track of the Day' (the deterministic seed already exists) with bronze/silver/gold/author targets, a ghost, and a UGS leaderboard; (b) instant restart from anywhere in a solo run; (c) unlimited attempts. The current weekly challenge with ONE attempt per UTC day is anti-'one more run' and should become unlimited attempts, best time counts.
- Cosmic Shore has no ghosts, no per-mode medals and no local leaderboards. Every long-lived comparable at your scale (Distance, Hyper Demon, House of the Dying Sun, Sky Rogue) has all three and none has a live pool. Ship ghosts + medals + 'players near you' leaderboards for solo runs in EVERY mode before adding another mode.
- Elemental crystals that change handling mid-run are a hardcore-hostile time-attack design unless controlled: offer a 'fixed loadout' or seeded-pickup time-trial variant so leaderboard runs are comparable. The level-5 transformations are good depth for matches; they are noise on a leaderboard.
- The vessel on-ramp is a Redout 2 risk: dual-WASD/dual-stick hulls and tutorials that gate. Lead with the one-thumb vessels (Sparrow, Scarab, Serpent) and the free Squirrel, keep Skim Race/Astro/Scramble variants playable at intensity 1 without any tutorial gate, and let difficulty scale per event, never per campaign.
- Treat EA launch as THE launch: only 20% of 2025 EA graduates did better at 1.0, and median 1.0 revenue was 40% of EA revenue. Wishlists (10k+ minimum per Zukowski), a permanent demo (Everspace 2's lesson - the freestyle hub can be it), and week-one review count matter more than the 12-month roadmap.
- Technical baseline is review-determining for this audience: Squadrons' FPS lock and Steam refund reviews citing '60fps lock, no achievements, forced launcher' show what gets punished. Ship uncapped framerate, full rebinding, and reconsider 'no Steamworks at launch' - Steam Input and achievements are cheap and their absence is a visible negative on a Windows-only Steam product.
- The crystal economy (4,000 per vessel at 200 per win = 20 wins per hull) is a free-to-play retention pattern inside a $15 paid game with no IAP; hardcore buyers read that as grind, and Rocket League/Mario Kart World both show economies and content droughts are the fastest way to lose goodwill. Unlock vessels via medals/challenges (Trackmania's 'prestige skins through achievements' shape), not wins.
- Concentrate the pool: pick the two or three modes that best demonstrate the fundamentals (Skim Race on trails, Scarab Scramble as the Rocket-League-shaped sport, Maelstrom as the party wrapper) and make them the only public queues; keep the rest as solo/party/AI content. Rocket League scaled on one 3v3 mode; Rematch's 5v5 pool halved monthly even with millions of owners.
- Your genuine differentiator is the living arena and trail-as-track - no competitor has it. Sonic Racing shipped the entire hardcore checklist (time trials, global leaderboards, ranked, crossplay) and still lost 9.5:1 to the incumbent, so feature parity is not the pitch; a 10-second clip where a Squirrel tube-rides a rival's trail while fauna graze it is.
- Distribution for a niche skill game is subscription/free reach once the solo loop exists (Descenders on Game Pass, Nightmare Kart free, F-Zero 99 on NSO). Keep the mobile/itch origins as a later reach play rather than cutting them forever, but only after the solo loop is worth carrying.
- Cosmetics are cut, so the profile must carry the medal ladder: earned skins/decals tied to author-medal counts are the cheapest goal system the casual half responds to (Trackmania 'prestigious car skins through achievements', F-Zero 99 pilot cards).

### Open questions
- What are Cosmic Shore's actual playtest retention numbers (D1/D7) and median session length, and how much of a session is spent waiting on lobbies versus in a run?
- Does the AI backfill produce a credible match in every vessel-locked mode (Astro League sword play, Scarab Scramble, The Bends), or only in the racing modes? If not, which modes should be solo-only at EA launch?
- Is there any per-mode solo time-trial with ghost and local leaderboard today, or is the weekly challenge the only leaderboarded activity?
- Is the framerate uncapped on PC and are all controls rebindable? Is 'no Steamworks SDK at launch' a firm decision, given Steam Input/achievements are the cheapest review-negative to remove?
- How many Steam wishlists exist today, and what is the demo/Next Fest plan given Next Fest was cut?
- Can the Skim Race seed be exposed as a shareable 'daily track' with a fixed loadout so runs are comparable on the UGS leaderboard?
- Which of the 16 modes are actually played in playtests? Is there data to justify concentrating public matchmaking into 2-3 queues?
- Is the crystal-per-win vessel economy tuned from data, or inherited from the mobile design? What is the intended time-to-second-vessel for a median player?
- What is the plan for the 12-month EA cadence: new modes/vessels (breadth) versus daily/weekly rotating content (cadence)? The comparables reward cadence.
- What is the collider/CPU budget at 120 fps on mid-range hardware in the heaviest arenas (Atlantis ~69k prisms, Lattice ~43k), given hardcore players will judge feel at high refresh?


## LENS: Hardcore vs casual requirements, and where they overlap ("a casual game for hardcore gamers")

The 2025-2026 evidence splits the market into three things that are often conflated. (1) Games that genuinely serve both audiences do it with ONE tight loop and LAYERED, OPT-IN difficulty or rulesets: Rocket League (one mode, design restraint, ranked with 8 tiers/10 placements/no decay), Trackmania (four medals per map, daily Track of the Day, Weekly Shorts with hidden leaderboards, ranked shrunk to 2v2 in July 2025 so you can queue with one friend), Balatro (8 stakes, 20 challenge decks, seeds, endless; 5M by Jan 2025, 7M+ by Oct 2025), Smash Ultimate (items/hazards toggles and saved rule presets; 37M by Dec 2025), Mario Kart World (items kept but stuns shortened, no full stop, first place gets coins; 15.39M by June 2026). (2) Games that PITCHED "accessible for newcomers, deep for competitive" as live PvP arenas are mostly dead: Knockout City (2M players week one, shut June 2023), Rumbleverse (6 months), Omega Strikers (peak 19,236 CCU, ~146 avg now, dev wound down Nov 2023 over retention), and Riot's 2XKO (EA Oct 2025, active dev ends Dec 2026: "diehard tag fighter players loved 2XKO, but most other fighting game players struggled to find reasons to stick"). (3) The play-with-friends growth is CO-OP "friendslop", not PvP: Big Walk sold 1M in ~6 days (Aug 2026), 75% audience overlap with PEAK; GameDiscoverCo notes these games "require lower amounts of manual dexterity".

Hardcore requirements are concrete and cheap to verify: consistent physics and server authority (Valve: "strongly suggested"; P2P must cross-validate), rebinding (a PEAK buyer refunded on day one in June 2025 for its absence), readable frame pacing (NVIDIA: 12 ms vs 20 ms latency = 182 ms aiming difference; 50.5% of Steam is still 1080p), and comeback mechanics they can switch OFF. Casual requirements: a mode explainable in three words (Fall Guys' internal rule), short rounds, no demotion at low ranks (Fall Guys Ranked: none below Silver), safe defaults (GDC 2024 prosocial talks; TIGG playbook), and 18 px minimum HUD text at 1080p (XAG 101).

For Cosmic Shore the diagnosis is blunt. Its unique asset — the emergent ecology and the freestyle toybox — is the casual, solo-and-co-op-friendly half and is what nobody else has. Its competitive half is currently shaped like the graveyard: sixteen mostly vessel-locked live modes splitting a paid-EA population, AI backfill as the default opponent, a comeback system that hands element levels (and level-5 ability transformations) to the trailing team with no off switch, client-local fauna so peers see different worlds in ecology-scored modes, no ranked, no replays, no ghosts, no medals, no in-game rebinding UI, no Steam Input, no anti-cheat and no ban tooling. "A casual game for hardcore gamers" is the Balatro/Trackmania positioning: short sessions, high ceiling, mastery you can measure, and not dependent on a live population. The roadmap should cut toward that — medals and ghosts on deterministic tracks, a weekly-shorts-style hidden-leaderboard cadence, a comeback toggle that is off wherever a score is published, and co-op-vs-ecology as the friends mode — before adding a seventeenth vessel-locked PvP mode.

### Findings
- [verified] The design literature's consensus mechanism for serving both audiences is 'opt-in complexity' inside a shared 'fun zone': the core loop must be playable by anyone, with deeper layers available but never required; the ceiling for hardcore/esports must be 'beyond human potential'. Sunspear's seven techniques (forgiving experimentation, proportional consequences, guiding feedback, skill displacement, easy intention-to-action, transferable skills across factions, unified basic/advanced control layers) are the most concrete published list.
  - evidence: Sunspear Games (IMMORTAL: Gates of Pyre) design posts define skill floor as 'how much work a player must put into a game before they begin having fun' and the ceiling for hardcore titles as needing to be 'beyond human potential'; the 'fun zone' is the range where you are good enough to have fun AND improvement still changes outcomes. Their lowering-the-floor post lists the seven techniques with RTS examples (unified command card with basic/advanced layers, automated worker management, merged production+supply, automated control groups).
  - sources: https://sunspeargames.com/skill-ceiling/ | https://sunspeargames.com/lowering-the-skill-floor-without-harming-the-skill-ceiling/
- [verified] Rocket League — the reference 'casual AND competitive' game — got there by design restraint: ONE mode, feature cuts, physics that are trivially readable but deep. Its ranked shape is 8 tiers x 3 divisions, 10 placement matches, soft seasonal resets, no rank decay, top rank ~1% of players. It still averages ~16,200 Steam players/month in Aug 2026 (all-time peak 146,902) eleven years after launch.
  - evidence: Psyonix design director Corey Davis's GDC 2016 talk describes focusing on the single 'car soccer' mode and cutting features to preserve 'a simple, but deep, experience'. Free-to-play on 23 Sept 2020 produced a 1M+ concurrent peak the next day. Fragster's 2025 guide: eight tiers, three divisions each, ten placement matches, Supersonic Legend ~1%, no rank decay. Steam Charts on 2026-09-04: 27,989 current, Aug 2026 average 16,232.
  - sources: https://gdcvault.com/play/1023197/Rocket-League-The-Road-From | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_League | https://www.fragster.com/rocket-league-ranks-explained-complete-tier-list-divisions-and-ranking-system-2025/ | https://steamcharts.com/app/252950
- [verified] Even in Rocket League, casual players actively avoid ranked because of sweat and toxicity, not skill — a Diamond-level player said 'I don't plan to sweat myself off while playing a game, but to get rid of everyday stress'; respondents report griefing/early forfeit/AFK 'about 1 x per 3 matches' post-F2P and that reports rarely lead to bans unless slurs are used. 'Casual' and 'competitive' are attitudes, and the casual attitude wants a queue where its mistakes do not cost teammates.
  - evidence: Steam forum thread 'Casual Vs. Competitive' (24 Jul 2022) on the Rocket League hub; moderator response notes poor play is not reportable griefing; consensus advice was to disable chat or take breaks.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/252950/discussions/0/3464974615690308422/
- [likely] Nintendo's 2025 answer to the comeback/rubber-band debate was not to remove items but to make being hit cheaper and being in first less punished: Mario Kart World shortens stun time, no longer brings you to a complete stop, hands first place coins as its most common item, and makes some items location-specific; bananas and green shells were nearly cut to keep races flowing. It sold 15.39M by 30 June 2026. Subsequent patches added player-configurable item rules (v1.4) and nerfed the boomerang after competitive feedback (v1.6.0, Apr 2026).
  - evidence: GameRant (11 Jun 2025) details the stun/recovery/item-distribution changes; Wikipedia records 15.39M copies by 30 June 2026 and the v1.1.2/1.3.0 course-weighting backlash; Game8 describes v1.4's 'Custom Items feature gives players full control when setting up item rules'; GodisaGeek reports the 1.6.0 boomerang nerf (Apr 2026). The custom-items and 1.6.0 details come from search snippets, not full reads.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/mario-kart-world-item-balancing-improved-good-why/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Kart_World | https://game8.co/articles/latest/mario-kart-world-v1-4-adds-item-rules-track-layouts-and-more | https://godisageek.com/2026/04/mario-kart-world-update-1-6-0-bob-omb-blast-boomerang-nerf/
- [verified] The failure mode of comeback mechanics is when the game's MAIN VERB is gated on losing: Split/Second's power plays were only available to trailing drivers, so 'being in the lead isn't a particularly fun experience when you can't trigger the game's main selling point', and skilled players were passed by AI 'with relative ease'. Rubber-banding reads as fair only when it changes odds at the margins, never when it hands the trailing side capabilities the leader cannot have.
  - evidence: Dave Mark, 'Rubber-Banding as a Design Requirement', Game Developer (2 Jun 2010), contrasting Mario Kart: Double Dash (no blue shell in first) with Split/Second.
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/rubber-banding-as-a-design-requirement
- [verified] Smash Ultimate serves both crowds with the SAME game through RULESETS, not modes: items, Final Smashes and stage hazards can be toggled, rule sets are saved as presets, every stage has Battlefield/Omega forms, and Elite Smash / Global Smash Power give the competitive crowd a ladder. Sakurai's stated goal was to speed the game up 'although not to an extent that would alienate players unfamiliar with the series'. Its online play was heavily criticised for lag and matchmaking that ignored preferences — netcode quality is a hardcore requirement even in a party game. 37M+ sold by Dec 2025.
  - evidence: Wikipedia's Super Smash Bros. Ultimate article (features, Sakurai quote, online reception including a dedicated Reddit complaint thread, sales).
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Smash_Bros._Ultimate
- [verified] Trackmania is the cleanest 'casual for hardcore' racing template: every map carries Bronze/Silver/Gold/Author medals, campaigns are 25 maps in 5 difficulty tiers refreshed quarterly, Track of the Day rotates daily with a 24-hour leaderboard, Weekly Shorts (17 Dec 2024) are five short maps with HIDDEN leaderboards, and Ranked moved to 2v2 in July 2025 so a player can queue with one friend and maps are picked for the lowest-ranked player. Community framing: 'professional players try to have the best time... fighting for 0.001 second difference, you and your friends can try to get silver medals on all maps.' The cost: IGN called the 2020 game 'aimed solely at the hardcore audience' because of subscription gating (Metacritic 74).
  - evidence: Trackmania (2020) Wikipedia (dates, Weekly Shorts, ranked 2v2, reception); Trackmania docs on seasonal campaigns, TOTD and Ranked 2v2; TMStats dev post (May 2022) for the medal-goal framing; vjeux's TOTD medal tool exists to answer 'whether you are not good or the map is hard'.
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trackmania_(2020_video_game) | https://doc.trackmania.com/play/what-is-a-seasonal-campaign/ | https://doc.trackmania.com/play/what-is-totd/ | https://doc.trackmania.com/play/what-is-ranked-2v2/ | https://dev.to/meldiron/tmstats-trackmania-tracker-1k1a | https://vjeux.github.io/trackmania-totd-medal-distribution/
- [verified] Balatro is the archetype of 'a casual game for hardcore gamers' and it is single-player, session-bounded and population-independent: familiar poker hands as the floor; 8 stakes, 20 challenge decks, seeded runs and endless mode as opt-in layers; 5M sold by Jan 2025 (half mobile), 7M+ by Oct 2025. LocalThunk deliberately hides score previews so the casual player gets the 'Rube Goldberg machine' reveal while optimisers use external calculators — the two audiences play the same loop at different depths.
  - evidence: Wikipedia (release 20 Feb 2024; 1M in first month; 5M Jan 2025; endless mode); Deconstructor of Fun (6 Oct 2025: 7M+, 'simplicity doesn't mean lack of depth'); balatrowiki (8 stakes with per-deck unlocks; 20 challenge decks; seeds); GMTK newsletter (2 Apr 2024) on the hidden-score design and its casual/optimiser tension.
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balatro | https://www.deconstructoroffun.com/blog/2025/10/06/how-do-you-find-and-scale-an-indie-hit-like-balatro | https://balatrowiki.org/w/Stakes | https://balatrowiki.org/w/Challenges | https://gmtk.substack.com/p/balatros-cursed-design-problem
- [verified] Fall Guys' casual contract was an internal rule that every mode must be explainable in three words, plus physics where 'falling over is funny'. It waited almost five years to add ranked (Ranked Knockout, 1 Apr 2025), and when it did, the ranked design protects casuals: eight-week cycles, no demotion at all from Rookie through Silver, and above that only sub-rank drops.
  - evidence: Wikipedia (1.5M players in 24h, 50M+ within two weeks of F2P June 2022, three-word rule, ragdoll intent); official Ranked Knockout post (1 Apr 2025).
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Guys | https://www.fallguys.com/en-US/news/fall-guys-ranked-knockout
- [verified] 'Accessible for newcomers, deep for competitive players' is the most common pitch among DEAD live-competitive games: Knockout City (2M players in week one, May 2021; F2P June 2022; servers closed 6 Jun 2023), Rumbleverse (Aug 2022 to Feb 2023), Omega Strikers (ex-Riot founders, all-time Steam peak 19,236, ~146 average in Aug 2026, development wound down 3 Nov 2023 over retention), and Riot's own 2XKO (EA 7 Oct 2025, #3 NA downloads Jan 2026, ~80 laid off Feb 2026, active development ends Dec 2026 announced 20 Aug 2026: 'diehard tag fighter players loved 2XKO, but most other fighting game players struggled to find reasons to stick'; 'costs us substantially more to operate than it brings in'; ranked disabled, seasons removed). Accessibility got them installs; it did not buy retention against an incumbent.
  - evidence: Wikipedia pages for Knockout City, Rumbleverse, Omega Strikers, 2XKO; Steam Charts for Omega Strikers; Riot's official announcement.
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knockout_City | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rumbleverse | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_Strikers | https://steamcharts.com/app/1869590 | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2XKO | https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/2xko-active-development-ends-december-2026
- [verified] The 'play with friends' segment that is actually growing on Steam in 2025-2026 is co-op, not PvP. Big Walk sold 1M in ~6 days (Aug 2026; 46k Steam CCU, ~1M wishlists) with 75% audience overlap with PEAK and 67% with Lethal Company; GameDiscoverCo argues these games win because they are 'super streamable' and 'require lower amounts of manual dexterity'. Chris Zukowski's July 2026 data says 'friendslop' is not saturated — concurrent players across the category keep rising.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo newsletter (21 Aug 2026); How To Market A Game 'Is Friendslop saturated?' (30 Jul 2026).
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/will-big-walks-1m-sales-usher-in | https://howtomarketagame.com/2026/07/30/is-friendslop-saturated/
- [verified] Indie multiplayer's chicken-and-egg problem is measured, not folklore: Battlebit took FOUR YEARS to reach 100 concurrent players, built through weekend-only scheduled playtests of 20-30 hand-picked players, then Steam Playtest; the developer's advice is 'If you are planning to make a multiplayer game, dont!' unless you can sustain years of community building. Valve's own Playtest docs endorse opening servers only in limited time windows and using event tools to concentrate players.
  - evidence: How To Market A Game Battlebit case study (3 Jul 2023: 800k wishlists, 87,323 peak CCU, 1.8M units in two weeks); Steamworks Playtest documentation.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2023/07/03/how-to-market-a-multiplayer-the-battlebit-remastered-story/ | https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/playtest
- [verified] Control rebinding and controller/glyph correctness are table stakes on PC in 2025, and skipping Steamworks forfeits Valve's free implementation. A PEAK buyer refunded on day one because no rebinding was 'an instant deal breaker' that 'should be basic functionality on PC'; the Game Accessibility Guidelines list remapping, text size, colour-blindness and subtitles as the four most-complained-about issues; Steam Deck Verified requires a default full-controller config, glyphs matching the active device, text no smaller than 9 px at 1280x800 (12 px recommended) and 30 fps at 800p by default; Steam Input supplies remapping, community configs and device glyphs to any game that integrates it.
  - evidence: PEAK Steam thread (30 Jun 2025); gameaccessibilityguidelines.com basic list; Steamworks Deck compatibility doc; Steamworks Steam Input doc.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/3527290/discussions/0/592901506443802418/ | https://gameaccessibilityguidelines.com/basic/ | https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/steamdeck/compat | https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/steam_controller
- [verified] Readable screens have published numbers: Xbox Accessibility Guideline 101 sets minimum default text at 18 px body height at 1080p for PC (26 px on console), scalable to 200%, with glyph text meeting the same minimum, sans-serif option, and 1.5x line spacing — and states platform magnifiers are not an acceptable mitigation.
  - evidence: Microsoft Learn XAG 101 (originally 9 May 2022, page updated 17 Jun 2026).
  - sources: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/accessibility/xbox-accessibility-guidelines/101
- [verified] Competitive integrity expectations for a small PC multiplayer game: Valve 'strongly suggests' dedicated authoritative servers and says that in P2P 'each user should validate the others to prevent the host from having full control'; banned/kicked players must get clear in-game messaging; Steam players now expect anti-cheat to be DISCLOSED (a 2025 thread demands it; a Nov 2025 GamingOnLinux piece shows kernel anti-cheat is contested and Linux/Deck is ~3% of Steam). Any published ladder built on client-reported results is assumed spoofable by the hardcore crowd.
  - evidence: Steamworks anti-cheat documentation; Steam Community suggestions thread (18-21 Oct 2025); GamingOnLinux (13 Nov 2025).
  - sources: https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/anticheat | https://steamcommunity.com/discussions/forum/10/669472405704690642/ | https://www.gamingonlinux.com/2025/11/anti-cheat-will-still-be-one-of-the-biggest-problems-for-the-new-steam-machine/
- [verified] Frame-rate and latency are measurable skill variables: NVIDIA Research found an 8 ms difference in system latency (12 ms vs 20 ms) changed aiming-task completion by 182 ms, and most gamers sit at 50-100 ms; higher FPS correlated with better K/D in PUBG/Fortnite. The Steam audience a Windows-only EA game ships to in Aug 2026 is 50.5% 1080p and 21.9% 1440p, Windows 11 71%, with RTX 3060/4060-class GPUs at the top of the survey — i.e. the target is a stable 60+ fps at 1080p on a 3060, with a frame cap and vsync exposed.
  - evidence: NVIDIA Reflex launch article (1 Sep 2020); Steam Hardware Survey (Aug 2026).
  - sources: https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/reflex-low-latency-platform/ | https://store.steampowered.com/hwsurvey/
- [verified] Mark Brown's current position: difficulty earns retention only when failure is FAIR and the learning path visible — players who can blame themselves stay, while 'sadistic' design (repetitive runs to a boss, unpredictable attacks, forced farming) makes them quit; assists and 'explore elsewhere' options do not harm the hardcore. Nintendo's version is 'three pipes' of invisible difficulty in one game (Miyamoto: the player adjusts difficulty 'while playing'), with assists carrying diminished rewards (bronze stars) so mastery still means something.
  - evidence: GMTK newsletter 'What's The Point Of Hard Games, Anyway?' (16 Sep 2025) and 'Super Mario's Invisible Difficulty Settings' (19 Jan 2024).
  - sources: https://gmtk.substack.com/p/whats-the-point-of-hard-games-anyway | https://gmtk.substack.com/p/super-marios-invisible-difficulty
- [verified] The oldest statement of the strategy — Damion Schubert's 'Double-Coding' (GDC Online 2011) — argues the two markets are symbiotic: 'leverage the hardcore to build your casual market' and 'convert your casual players into online devotees'; mainstream success 'does not mean sacrificing the hardcore for the casual'. Hardcore players are the content, streaming and community engine that makes a small game discoverable. Supercell's mission statement ('games that as many people as possible play for years') and its record — 11 cancelled projects vs 7 shipped, Squad Busters killed <18 months after launch (30 Oct 2025) — show that midcore is judged on RETENTION, not on breadth of appeal.
  - evidence: GDC Vault session page (2011); Supercell site and Wikipedia.
  - sources: https://gdcvault.com/play/1015137/Double-Coding-Making-Online-Games | https://supercell.com/en/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercell_(video_game_company)
- [likely] 'No toxicity' is now a design discipline with an industry body behind it: GDC 2024 ran 'Kind Games: Designing for Prosocial Multiplayer' (Daniel Cook) and 'Designing to Reduce Toxicity in Online Games' (Jenova Chen); the Thriving in Games Group (Riot, Supercell, Discord, Tencent) publishes a 'Safety by Design as a Gameplay Practice' playbook. Casual players expect safe DEFAULTS (chat off or opt-in, mute/block/report in two clicks), not a moderation queue.
  - evidence: GDC Vault free 2024 listing; thrivingingames.org and digitalthrivingplaybook.org.
  - sources: https://gdcvault.com/free/gdc-24 | https://thrivingingames.org/ | https://www.digitalthrivingplaybook.org

### Comparables
- Rocket League (Psyonix/Epic) (2015, $$19.99 at launch (2015); free-to-play since Sep 2020): Peak 146,902 Steam CCU; ~16.2k monthly average in Aug 2026, eleven years on. LESSON: One mode, physics anyone can read, ranked as a separate opt-in queue with placements and no decay; still needed F2P and cross-play to reach scale, and its casual queue is where non-sweats hide from toxicity.
- Trackmania (Nadeo/Ubisoft) (2020, $Free tier; Club access $19.99/yr since Oct 2023): Live since 2020; Weekly Shorts added Dec 2024; ranked to 2v2 Jul 2025; Metacritic 74. LESSON: Four medals per map + daily/weekly rotation + hidden weekly leaderboards give casuals and hunters the same map with different goals; ranked shrank to 2v2 so a small population still queues with a friend. Subscription gating got it labelled 'hardcore only'.
- Balatro (LocalThunk/Playstack) (2024, $$14.99): 5M by Jan 2025, 7M+ by Oct 2025; GDCA 2025 Game of the Year. LESSON: Depth is layered ON the same loop via opt-in stakes and challenge decks; the designer protects the casual moment (hidden score) and lets optimisers dig with tools.
- Fall Guys (Mediatonic/Epic) (2020, $$19.99 at launch; free since Jun 2022): 1.5M players in 24h (2020); 50M+ after F2P (2022); Ranked Knockout Apr 2025. LESSON: 'Explainable in three words' per mode; ranked with no demotion at low tiers and eight-week cycles; F2P was what unlocked 50M players.
- Mario Kart World (Nintendo) (2025, $$79.99): 15.39M sold by 30 Jun 2026; v1.4 custom items; v1.6.0 boomerang nerf Apr 2026. LESSON: Keep the comeback, cut its punishment (shorter stuns, no full stop, coins in first) and hand hosts an item-rules screen; patch items on competitive feedback.
- Super Smash Bros. Ultimate (Nintendo) (2018, $$59.99): 37M+ by Dec 2025. LESSON: Rule presets (items/hazards off, Omega stages) plus a separate Elite ladder; bad netcode was the one thing critics would not forgive.
- Omega Strikers (Odyssey Interactive) (2022, $Free-to-play): Peak 19,236 Steam CCU (Sep 2022); ~146 average Aug 2026; development wound down Nov 2023. LESSON: Accessibility converts installs, not retention; without a reason to stick beyond 'it's easy to start', the population collapsed within a year and the team pivoted.
- Knockout City (Velan/EA) (2021, $$19.99 at launch; F2P from Jun 2022): Servers closed 6 Jun 2023. LESSON: 2M players in week one did not sustain; paid-then-F2P with a small team could not fund live ops; shut in two years.
- 2XKO (Riot Games) (2025, $Free-to-play): EA 7 Oct 2025; active development ends Dec 2026 (announced 20 Aug 2026); refunds issued. LESSON: Even Riot's IP, budget and free model could not make non-diehards stick; 'engagement stayed relatively flat despite larger updates'; ranked and seasons were switched off at the end.
- Big Walk / PEAK / Lethal Company ('friendslop') (2026, $Big Walk ~$30; PEAK $7.99): Big Walk 1M in ~6 days (Aug 2026), 46k Steam CCU; category concurrent players still rising per Zukowski (Jul 2026). LESSON: If Cosmic Shore wants the 'casual' half to include friends, co-op against the ecology is the trend-aligned shape; competitive 1v1/2v2 vessel duels are swimming upstream.
- Battlebit Remastered (SgtOkiDoki) (2023, $$14.99): 800k wishlists, 1.8M units in two weeks, 87k peak CCU (Jun 2023). LESSON: Four years to 100 CCU; weekend-only scheduled playtests; Steam Playtest; the developer's blunt advice is not to make a multiplayer game as your first game.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) One readable core loop with opt-in layers of complexity (never mandatory depth)
- (hardcore, must-have) Consistent, deterministic physics and server-authoritative results; identical world state on every peer in anything scored
- (both, must-have) Comeback/rubber-band mechanics that can be switched OFF (rule presets / custom item rules), and that never gate the game's main verb on losing
- (both, must-have) Full control rebinding (keyboard, mouse, gamepad), sensitivity, device-correct glyphs
- (hardcore, must-have) Stable 60+ fps at 1080p on mainstream GPUs with frame cap/vsync options; low input latency
- (both, strong) Measurable mastery per map/mode: medals (bronze/silver/gold/author), personal bests, ghosts, replays
- (hardcore, strong) A ranked/ELO queue that is opt-in, separate from casual, with placements, soft resets, no decay, and no demotion at low tiers
- (both, strong) Daily/weekly time-boxed challenges with hidden or friends-scoped leaderboards (compete asynchronously, no live population required)
- (casual, must-have) Quick-to-fun: mode explainable in three words, sub-5-minute rounds, fast loads, no lobby dead time
- (casual, must-have) Play with friends easily (invite, queue with one friend, co-op vs the world) — co-op is the growth segment
- (casual, strong) Forgiving onboarding: proportional consequences, guiding feedback, transferable skills across vessels/modes, invisible difficulty pipes
- (casual, strong) Safe defaults and two-click mute/block/report; no forced voice/text
- (both, strong) Readable screens: HUD/menu text >= 18 px at 1080p, scalable to 200%, colour never the sole carrier of information
- (hardcore, strong) No pay-to-win; anti-cheat posture disclosed; cheaters removable
- (both, strong) Low commitment per session but a reason to return for years (stakes, challenge decks, rotating content), not a live-population dependency
- (both, nice-to-have) Bots that are honest and good: practice/training vs bots is welcome; bot-filled 'ranked' is not

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Reposition, then build to the position. 'A casual game for hardcore gamers' is the Balatro/Trackmania/Vampire Survivors slot: short sessions, high ceiling, mastery you can MEASURE, no dependence on a live population. Cosmic Shore's competitive half today is shaped like the Omega Strikers / Knockout City / 2XKO graveyard (accessible live PvP arenas splitting a small population), and those had F2P, consoles and far more money. The ecology + toybox is the genuinely differentiated, population-independent half — lead with it.
- Sixteen modes, most locked to one vessel, at 1-4 players, is the opposite of Rocket League's 'one mode' and Fall Guys' 'three words'. In a paid Windows-only EA at $15, expect low hundreds of CCU at best (Battlebit: four years to 100 CCU). Sixteen queues means every queue is AI backfill, and hardcore players read a bot-filled match as a dead game. Ship EA with 3-4 hero modes surfaced (Skim Race, Maelstrom, one co-op-vs-ecology mode, freestyle) and fold the rest into Maelstrom's draw pool or a 'Lab' tab. Do not add a seventeenth vessel-locked PvP mode before retention data exists.
- Give the comeback system an OFF switch and default it off wherever a number is published. `ElementalComebackSystem` hands the trailing DOMAIN element levels — and the authoring scripts assert a quarter-of-target deficit must buy a whole element level, which at level 5 hands out ability TRANSFORMATIONS. That is Split/Second's mistake: the losing side gets verbs the leader cannot have. Party lobbies can keep it (Mario Kart World keeps items but cut their punishment); the weekly challenge, any ladder, any ghost/medal time, and any future ranked queue must run with it off, and the lobby card must show the setting like Smash's rule presets.
- Fix competitive integrity before claiming it. Fauna are client-local (every peer runs its own spawner off local Random rolls; kills are client-reported via RPC), so in Wildlife Liberation, Brood Rush and any ecology-scored mode two players are literally scoring different worlds; Relay is host-authoritative with no cross-validation, no anti-cheat, no ban tooling. Either make ecology-scored modes host-authoritative for anything that scores, or keep those modes explicitly 'party' and out of every leaderboard. State the anti-cheat posture ('none; host-authoritative; friends-scale') on the Steam page — players now expect disclosure.
- Exploit what is already deterministic. Skim Race builds identical tracks from a seed on every client — that is Trackmania for free: seed-of-the-day and weekly-shorts rotations, bronze/silver/gold/author medals per seed x intensity, personal-best ghosts (record vessel pose per tick; the `RewindSystem` already exists), friends-scoped and hidden leaderboards that reveal at week end. This gives hardcore players an async ladder and casual players silver medals on the same track without needing a live opponent, and it makes the weekly challenge (currently completion-time only) a daily habit.
- Rebinding and Steam Input are table stakes, not polish. There is a full Input System with an `InputHintBindingMap` and `ControlGlyphSetSO`, but no in-game rebinding UI and a fixed QWER+Space map; the decision to skip the Steamworks SDK also forfeits Steam Input's remapping, community configs and device glyphs. A June-2025 buyer refunded PEAK on day one for exactly this. Ship a rebinding screen in EA (keyboard+mouse and pad), and reconsider Steamworks for Input and Rich Presence even if achievements stay cut.
- Performance is a hardcore requirement here, not a nicety. The prism system carries tens of thousands of live prisms (Lattice cell ~42k, Atlantis ~69k) and multi-second arena builds behind a load veil with a 45 s cap. The Aug-2026 Steam audience is 50% 1080p on RTX 3060-class GPUs; NVIDIA's data says an 8 ms latency difference is measurable in aim. Publish a minimum spec that holds 60 fps at 1080p on a 3060, expose frame cap/vsync/quality in-game (only `BootstrapConfigSO` carries vsync today), and treat any mode whose ladder cannot hold 60 fps as not shippable in that intensity.
- Make the casual half actually casual. Quick-to-fun currently means: quest chain to unlock modes, intensity tiers gated by play, 4,000-crystal vessels at 200 per win (20 wins per hull), and a connecting panel that can hold 45 s. Casual players in 2026 expect the three-word pitch and sub-5-minute rounds — Joust and Bends (race to 3) and Maelstrom are the right length; Peel the Cage's 2,000-prism target is not. Unlock at least one 'friends' mode and the Squirrel+Sparrow pair from minute zero, and shorten the first-session path to freestyle + one race.
- Aim the 'play with friends' promise at co-op vs the ecology, which is the growth segment (Big Walk 1M in six days; 75% overlap with PEAK) and is the one thing Cosmic Shore uniquely has: a living, grazing, starving antagonist. Co-Op Wildlife Blitz and 2v2-vs-AI already exist; promote a 2-4 player co-op cell-defence/liberation loop to a hero mode. Competitive vessel duels (Dog Fight, The Bends, Joust) are fine as opt-in, but they are not what will make friends buy four copies.
- Adopt the Fall Guys/Rocket League ranked shape only when the population exists, and never before: opt-in queue, 10 placements, soft seasonal reset, no decay, no demotion below the equivalent of Silver, 2-player parties allowed, comeback off, host-authoritative only. Until then, publish per-mode medals and hidden weekly leaderboards instead of an ELO, so there is no dead ranked queue on the store page.
- Readability audit against numbers, not taste: XAG 101's 18 px minimum body height at 1080p (scalable to 200%) and Deck Verified's 9-12 px at 1280x800. The HUD's authored sizes (e.g. 16 pt objective labels, 22 px keyboard chips) sit at or below those floors; the domain palette work (Jade reading as blue) is already a colour-as-sole-carrier problem the docs record. Add a UI scale slider and ensure no scoring information is colour-only.
- Safety by default at friends scale is cheap and expected: no open text/voice by default, one-tap mute/block (UGS Friends already has block), a report path that at least records, and a lobby that lets a host kick (exists). Say so on the store page; casual buyers screen for it.
- Explicit 'casual for hardcore' checklist a 2026 buyer expects, with Cosmic Shore status: (1) one loop explainable in three words — partial (per mode, not per game); (2) opt-in complexity layers — yes (elements, level-5 upgrades, intensities); (3) comeback/handicap toggle, off for anything published — NO; (4) deterministic physics + authoritative results — partial (host-auth vessels, client-local ecology); (5) medals/PBs/ghosts/replays — NO (rewind system exists, unused for this); (6) daily/weekly rotating challenge with hidden or friends leaderboards — partial (one weekly time board); (7) opt-in ranked with placements/no decay/no low-tier demotion — NO (and correctly deferred); (8) rebinding + Steam Input glyphs — NO; (9) 60 fps at 1080p on mainstream GPU with in-game options — unverified; (10) sub-5-minute rounds and fast loads — partial; (11) play with a friend in two clicks, co-op vs the world — partial (party system yes; co-op hero mode no); (12) honest bots for practice, never disguised as ranked — partial (AI backfill everywhere); (13) safe defaults + mute/block/report — partial; (14) text >= 18 px @1080p, colour never sole carrier, UI scale — unverified/partial; (15) no P2W, anti-cheat posture disclosed — yes/NO (nothing disclosed); (16) reasons to return for years without a live population — yes in principle (ecology, toys, painting gallery) but under-surfaced.

### Open questions
- Web search budget was exhausted after seven queries; several claims (Mario Kart World v1.4 custom item rules, the 1.6.0 boomerang nerf, Rocket League's 2025 rank-system changes at rocketleague.com, Valve's Oct-2024 anti-cheat disclosure announcement text) were reached only via snippets or blocked pages and should be re-verified.
- Steam Charts shows a 'Knockout City Cross-Play Beta' app with ~9,700 current players and a 116,340% 30-day gain (Sep 2026) — a relaunch or free re-release may have happened in Aug 2026 that Wikipedia does not yet record; if so it is a live comparable for 'accessible competitive' resurrection and worth a direct look.
- Adam Millard (Architect of Games) and Sakurai's specific videos on comeback mechanics and risk/reward could not be fetched; their positions are not represented beyond secondary summaries.
- No 2025-2026 GDC talk specifically on ranked/matchmaking for small populations was found in the free vault listings; the Accelbyte 'Boom vs Rush' (GDC 2025) and Daniel Cook's 'Kind Games' (GDC 2024) sessions are the closest and were not read.
- What is Cosmic Shore's actual measured frame time at 1080p on an RTX 3060 in the heaviest cells (Lattice ~42k prisms, Atlantis ~69k)? The whole hardcore performance argument hinges on a number the team can measure today.
- Is there an appetite to make Skim Race's deterministic-seed track the spine of an async ladder (medals/ghosts/daily seed) — and does the rewind system already capture enough pose data for a ghost?
- How large is the itch.io/TestFlight cohort, and what does PostHog say about first-session length and mode-switching? The 'quick-to-fun' and 'three-word' claims here should be checked against real funnel data before cutting modes.
- Will the team accept a comeback OFF default in published contexts, given the comeback tuning is currently authored as a build-failing invariant in several mode generators?


## LENS: Retention and live-ops expectations for a paid, no-IAP PC multiplayer indie

The 2025-2026 evidence is unambiguous on one point: matchmade PvP at indie scale dies, every time. Chris Zukowski's Q2-2025 audit of every indie multiplayer shooter that reached 1,000 reviews shows launch-to-present CCU collapses of 95-99% (Steel Hunters 4,473→45, Neighbors 3,308→102, OUTLAWED 873→14) and concludes "indie developers should never make them." The multiplayer indies that did win in 2025 (R.E.P.O., PEAK, Schedule I) are co-op, friend-recruited, and playable with the people you already know; none depends on a public queue. Paid PvP with big launches (Boundary 100k copies day one, Rematch 1M week one, Splitgate 2) still lost 60-90% of players within a month and either shut down or went "back to beta."

Retention baselines are brutal: GameAnalytics' 2026 report (3,582 PC games) puts median PC D1/D7/D30 at ~7%/4%/1%; top-10% D30 is 2.5%. Median session is 18 minutes, which suits party-length modes but means D30 survival comes from a reason to return, not from match quality alone.

Live-ops expectations are now codified by Valve itself: a store-page warning after 12 months without an update (Feb 2025), an optional planned-1.0 date field (Feb 2026), and a doc that says "do not ask customers to bet on the future." The small teams that kept trust ran a visible rhythm: R.E.P.O. posts devlogs roughly every two weeks and ships a named update every few months; BattleBit went silent for ~7 months and its recent reviews fell to 27% positive. And 1.0 is not a second launch: of 225 games leaving EA in 2025, only 20% did better at 1.0, with median 1.0 revenue ~40% of EA's first 30 days.

For Cosmic Shore this means the EA launch is the launch, and its structure works against it: ~16 modes, most vessel-locked, times four intensities, is dozens of queues for a population that will realistically be tens to hundreds of CCU. The architecture already has the right hedge (AI backfill, party invites, single-player toys, a weekly challenge). The market wants that hedge made explicit and total: every mode playable solo/with bots with zero wait, humans dropping into bot matches, and the store page saying so. "No IAP" is a genuine reputation asset (R.E.P.O. 96% positive, explicitly credited), but "no cosmetics at all" is a cost: earned cosmetics are the most-requested feature in that same community, and a supporter-pack DLC (2-7% attach) is standard and not read as IAP. Skipping the Steamworks SDK forfeits achievements (a hygiene item) and, more damagingly for a party game, Steam friend invites, the exact growth channel 2025's hits rode.

### Findings
- [verified] Indie-scale matchmade multiplayer shooters lose 90-99% of their concurrent players and are effectively dead within months, even when they are review 'hits'.
  - evidence: Zukowski's Q2-2025 audit lists every indie multiplayer shooter that reached 1,000+ Steam reviews with launch vs current CCU: Splitgate 2 25,785→1,122; Wild Assault 2,274→325; Neighbors: Suburban Warfare 3,308→102; Steel Hunters 4,473→45; OUTLAWED 873→14; Last Remains 751→14; Cheaters Cheetah 956→8. Verdict: 'Even if a multiplayer shooter is a hit with 1000 reviews, it quickly dies. Every single time.' Reviews on the sub-100-CCU titles read 'the lobbies are dead'.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2025/08/04/2025-q2-games-that-are-selling/
- [verified] PC retention baselines are low: median D1 ~7%, D7 ~4%, D30 ~1%; top-10% D30 is only 2.5%; top-1% D30 ~10% and falling.
  - evidence: GameAnalytics 2026 Mobile & PC Benchmarks, sample 3,582 PC games with >=100 MAU, 2025 data. Median D1 ~7% (top 25% 15-16%, top 1% 50-60%); D7 median ~4% (top 10% ~10%, top 1% 20%+); D30 median ~1% (top 10% 2.5%, top 1% 10%, 'declined over the course of 2025'). Median session 18 min, median daily playtime 32-33 min, median 1.65-1.7 sessions/day.
  - sources: https://gamedevreports.substack.com/p/gameanalytics-mobile-and-pc-game | https://www.gameanalytics.com/reports/2026-mobile-pc-gaming-benchmarks
- [verified] 1,000 CCU is an indie hit but is still too thin for good matchmaking; the design answer is fewer queues, drop-in/drop-out, and loosened criteria, not a bigger population.
  - evidence: Joost van Dongen (Awesomenauts): '1000 concurrent players isn't much from a matchmaking perspective'; even 100+ searches per minute cannot satisfy strict regional/skill criteria at off-peak. Recommends reducing matchmaking criteria, drop-in/drop-out, spectating while waiting, and limiting game modes to prevent fragmentation. (2017 source, still the standard reference; 2025 CCU tables above confirm the floor.)
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/the-requirements-of-good-matchmaking
- [likely] The multiplayer indies that won in 2025-2026 are co-op and friend-recruited, not matchmade PvP.
  - evidence: R.E.P.O. (Semiwork, EA) crossed 200k+ peak CCU and is 96% positive on 260k+ reviews; PEAK (Aggro Crab + Landfall) sold 1M in six days, 4.5M in a month, 10M by Aug 2026 and hit 171k CCU after its Aug-2026 update; Schedule I (3 people) peaked at 459,075 CCU. Analyses attribute the highest wishlist-to-sale conversions in Steam history to 'friend recruitment dynamics and streaming culture'.
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/business/aggro-crab-side-hustle-peak-has-sold-100-000-copies-in-24-hours | https://www.tweaktown.com/news/107179/peak-confirmed-to-have-sold-more-than-10-million-copies/index.html | https://alineaanalytics.substack.com/p/peak-sold-18-million-copies-last | https://en.softonic.com/articles/one-of-the-games-that-has-been-the-biggest-hit-this-year-continues-to-reject-microtransactions | https://altheragames.com/en/blog/steam-wishlist-guide
- [verified] Valve now polices Early Access communication directly: a store-page warning after 12 months without an update+event post (Feb 2025) and an optional planned-1.0-date field (Feb 2026); its guidance forbids 'specific promises about future events'.
  - evidence: Steamworks EA doc: 'If it has been more than 12 months since the developers... have updated the game and posted an update type event... Steam will add a note to the store page'; 'Do not make specific promises about future events'; 'Do not ask your customers to bet on the future of your game'; 'Be as specific as you can' about EA duration. Warning introduced 2025-02-07. Planned release date field added Feb 2026 because devs were already posting 1.0 dates in store text/events/socials.
  - sources: https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/store/earlyaccess | https://www.alternativeto.net/news/2025/2/steam-now-warns-you-about-early-access-games-that-haven-t-been-updated-in-over-a-year/ | https://www.gamingonlinux.com/2026/02/steam-early-access-games-can-now-have-a-planned-release-date-listed/
- [verified] 1.0 is not a second launch: only 20% of 2025 EA graduates did better at 1.0 than at EA launch; median 1.0 revenue was ~40% of EA's first 30 days.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo (Dec 9 2025): 225 games with >5k EA copies left EA in 2025; median EA duration 437 days, average 643; 45 (20%) beat their EA launch; 'the concept that a slow launch can be redeemed by a 1.0 release is increasingly out of step with reality'. Games with frequent updates and discounting during EA saw 85-95% revenue declines at 1.0. Winners re-viralized via novelty (News Tower +138%) or added languages (Mars First Logistics +181%).
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/the-state-of-steam-early-access-graduates
- [verified] The cadence small teams that kept trust actually ran: a devlog roughly every two weeks plus a named content update every few months; silence longer than a quarter converts reviews to negative.
  - evidence: R.E.P.O.: devlogs ~every two weeks, Monster Update 2025-10-30, Cosmetics Update 2026-05-07, recent reviews 94% positive. PEAK: three major updates (2025-08-11, 2025-11-05, 2026-08-11) then a stated wind-down to new projects. BattleBit Remastered (3 devs): 87,323 peak CCU June 2023 → 1,046 June 2025; last Discord/Twitter posts Nov/Dec 2024 until a June 2025 tease; all-time reviews 84% positive but recent 30 days 27% positive. Guides converge on 'at least monthly patch notes, major content every 2-3 months, silence is worse than a small patch'.
  - sources: https://www.repo-game.org/en/repogame-update | https://games.mxdwn.com/news/r-e-p-o-developer-semiwork-wraps-up-successful-early-access-release-year-with-exciting-news/ | https://www.techtimes.com/articles/324007/20260811/peaks-final-update-brings-monster-that-hunts-scoutmaster-who-speaks.htm | https://www.pcgamesn.com/battlebit-remastered/new-update | https://www.steampageanalyzer.com/blog/steam-early-access-strategy
- [verified] Launch-window technical instability (netcode, crashes, cheaters) plus thin content is the first churn driver even for well-funded paid PvP; Rematch lost 60% of Steam players July→August 2025 despite 1M first-week sales.
  - evidence: Rematch (Sloclap, launched 2025-06-19): July peak 50,434 / avg 20,256; August peak 20,546 / avg 8,037 (−60.32%); Xbox −78.4%, PS5 −60.1% from peak; causes cited: netcode/lag, crashes, inconsistent matchmaking, hackers; crossplay only arrived in the third update (Sept 2025). Splitgate 2 launched 2025-06-06, went 'back to beta' in July with 45 layoffs; founders: 'we launched too early... bit off more than we could chew'.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/rematch-player-count-down/ | https://www.pcgamer.com/games/fps/splitgate-2-is-going-back-into-beta-1047-will-keep-the-floundering-fps-online-and-lay-off-more-staff-as-it-goes-back-to-the-drawing-board/
- [verified] Small paid multiplayer teams that underperform now end development within weeks or months rather than iterating; 'servers stay up, no more content' is the common exit.
  - evidence: Level Zero: Extraction (Doghowl/tinyBuild): EA Aug 2024, 1.0 2025-01-29, development ended two weeks later — 'no longer feasible for our relatively small teams to sustain the game from a financial standpoint'. Hyper Light Breaker: EA 2025-01-14, Mixed on 2,500+ reviews, development ended Oct 2025 with layoffs. Boundary: EA 2023-04-13 at $25, 100k copies in 24h, lost 90.74% of players in month one, delisted June 2024 amid publisher/dev dispute over 'lack of updates'; still unavailable Jan 2026. Steel Hunters shut ~3 months after launch (2025).
  - sources: https://www.pcgamer.com/games/fps/just-two-weeks-after-launch-extraction-survival-horror-game-level-zero-extraction-ends-development-its-no-longer-feasible-for-our-relatively-small-teams-to-sustain-the-game/ | https://www.gamedeveloper.com/business/heart-machine-making-layoffs-and-ending-development-on-hyper-light-breaker | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boundary_(video_game) | https://www.pcgamer.com/games/fps/zero-g-pvp-shooter-that-sold-100k-in-a-day-is-closing-after-a-yearpublisher-blames-developer-developer-blames-them-right-back-players-just-review-bomb-it/ | https://apptrigger.com/rip-to-these-games-that-died-in-2025-01kd3s3ey5kf
- [likely] Bots and offline/co-op play are the recognised hedge against the empty-lobby spiral, and a six-person team has proven it commercially.
  - evidence: Hypercharge: Unboxed (Digital Cybercherries, 6 staff): ships offline play with commandable bots; 100k Xbox sales in under a month (2024) after 13 free major updates since 2020. DICE confirmed bots auto-fill empty servers in Battlefield 6 (2025). BattleBit players petitioned for a bot/single-player mode to survive low population; consensus in dev/player threads: 'even simple bots would be enjoyable in low-population lobbies'.
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/business/hypercharge-unboxed-sells-50-000-xbox-copies-in-under-a-week | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypercharge:_Unboxed | https://steamcommunity.com/app/671860/discussions/4/6717729343882955206/ | https://www.neogaf.com/threads/dice-confirms-ai-bots-are-returning-in-battlefield-6-to-fill-empty-servers-wont-switch-to-unreal-engine-5.1686983/
- [likely] 'No microtransactions' is a measurable reputation asset for a paid game in 2025-2026, but zero cosmetics is a cost: earned cosmetics are the most-requested feature in the same community.
  - evidence: R.E.P.O.'s 96% positive rating on 260k+ reviews is explicitly attributed to being paid with no microtransactions; players 'don't mind paying for a game if it is actually good'. Semiwork's May 2026 Cosmetics Update added 500+ unlockable cosmetics while rejecting microtransactions; 'cosmetics have long been one of the most requested additions'. Steam curators 'No Microtransactions' / 'Microtransaction Check' exist to flag this at purchase time.
  - sources: https://en.softonic.com/articles/one-of-the-games-that-has-been-the-biggest-hit-this-year-continues-to-reject-microtransactions | https://egw.news/gaming/news/30922/repos-upcoming-update-expands-cosmetics-while-reje-eWYC-Ypop | https://www.repo-game.org/en/repogame-update | https://store.steampowered.com/curator/35359784-No-Microtransactions/
- [verified] Paid cosmetic/supporter DLC is a standard, low-effort tip jar for premium indies (2-7% attach in the $150k+ tiers, 22% for tiny games) and is not read by players as IAP.
  - evidence: Zukowski DLC benchmark (2026-03-03): supporter-pack attach 2.3% Diamond ($1M+), 3.4% Gold, 6.8% Silver, 22% Bronze; advice: Diamond/Gold games 'should launch with at least a soundtrack and a supporter pack and bundle them'. GamesRadar: a 2.59% attach earned one dev $11,531 for 'an $11,000 gold texture'. Legend of Keepers' supporter pack sold 42,000 units. Monetization guides warn against drip-fed cosmetic DLC that 'cheapens the base game'; substantial expansions every 6-12 months are the premium pattern.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2026/03/03/benchmark-how-much-money-can-you-make-from-dlc/ | https://www.gamesradar.com/games/that-is-an-usd11-000-gold-texture-steam-researcher-says-supporter-packs-make-a-lot-of-free-money-because-pc-gamers-love-to-spend-on-their-favorite-games/ | https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/in-depth-how-legend-of-keepers-hit | https://www.strayspark.studio/blog/indie-game-monetization-2026-pricing-strategy
- [verified] Official UGC/Workshop support is the strongest measured longevity lever: +75% CCU retention at 2 years and +115% at 5 years, +31% revenue at year 5.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo (Sept 2025 update of 2024 study), ~1,200 games with >$1M first-month Steam revenue: ~20% have official UGC; revenue +8% year 1, +31% year 5; CCU retention +75% after 2 years, +115% after 5; per-DLC revenue +105%; SnowRunner/mod.io: UGC-engaged players 2.4x more likely to buy DLC. Caveat stated: genre selection may drive part of the effect.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/analysis-ugc-still-powers-sales-and | https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/does-ugc-power-sales-and-retention
- [likely] Daily/weekly seeded challenges are cheap to build and produce a small, steady daily return, but the evidence is old and genre-specific (roguelikes); they are a supplement, not a retention engine.
  - evidence: Game Developer 'The 24-hour ticket' (2017): Caveblazers implemented dailies 'in a single weekend' and saw 'fall off in numbers each day is very small'; Dead Cells reported ~7,000 daily runs/day; OlliOlli used dailies as 'experience tourism'. Steam's most popular 2025 roguelike (Megabonk) had its leaderboards broken by cheaters, forcing a fix. No 2025-2026 PC study quantifies the DAU lift of dailies; mobile/PC guides treat them as standard hygiene.
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/the-24-hour-ticket-examining-daily-runs- | https://www.pcgamer.com/games/roguelike/after-cheaters-broke-steams-most-popular-roguelikes-leaderboards-its-dev-issued-a-fix-and-a-warning-cheaters-you-better-watch-your-ass-or-im-clapping-your-cheeks-back-to-the-shadow-realm-where-you-belong/ | https://featureupvote.com/blog/game-retention/
- [speculative] No quantitative evidence links Steam achievements to sales or retention; they are a hygiene expectation, and skipping the Steamworks SDK costs more than achievements (friend invites, overlay, Steam Input).
  - evidence: Searches across Zukowski, GameDiscoverCo, VG Insights and academic sources found no study isolating achievements' effect on sales; academic work only shows short games get higher completion (Stanley Parable 74%). Statista-cited data: only ~21% of 2024 Steam releases exposed Steam XP/badges. Valve's Stats & Achievements doc treats them as a standard Steamworks feature; player complaints cluster on broken achievements rather than absent ones. Inference: absence is noticed by completionists but is not a purchase driver.
  - sources: https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/achievements | https://www.twoaveragegamers.com/game-completion-rates/ | https://www.statista.com/statistics/1558688/steam-game-releases-by-social-features | https://steamcommunity.com/discussions/forum/10/4339860800345976009/
- [verified] Party-brawler/party-sports launches without a pre-built social graph flop on Steam even with a major publisher; Game Pass or Game Preview exposure does not translate to a Steam population.
  - evidence: Kiln (Double Fine/Xbox, online party brawler, launched 2026-04-23 day-one on Game Pass): 193 peak unique Steam users in first 24 hours; negatives cite level-locked mechanics and few modes. Turbo Golf Racing (Hugecalf, car-golf party racer): EA Aug 2022, 1M Game Preview players, 1.0 April 2024, 5 concurrent Steam players April 2026. Omega Strikers (3v3 'footbrawler', $19M Series A): peak 19,236 CCU, development ended, ~200 CCU by early 2026, 50% layoffs.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/kiln-steam-player-count-bad/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turbo_Golf_Racing | https://news.xbox.com/en-us/2024/03/05/turbo-golf-racing-release-date-million-players/ | https://medium.com/@averybarker5/omega-strikers-is-dying-a-sad-fate-for-independent-games-0f2bab038f16 | https://techraptor.net/gaming/news/omega-strikers-odyssey-interactive-layoffs
- [likely] Community events keep a small game warm only while an event is running; between events the population goes quiet, and the last thing dying live-service games ship is custom lobbies so the community can self-organise.
  - evidence: Hitman community: 'small gaps between main tournaments meant the community often went quiet outside of tournament weeks'. Omega Strikers' final content was a custom-games update before development ended; Splitgate 1 shipped peer-to-peer play so it stays playable after server shutdown (2025). Grassroots tournaments kept Rivals of Aether / Lethal League alive for years, but those are fighting games with a pre-existing FGC.
  - sources: https://hitmanrr.substack.com/p/the-community-events-of-a-hitman | https://medium.com/@averybarker5/omega-strikers-is-dying-a-sad-fate-for-independent-games-0f2bab038f16 | https://www.pcgamer.com/games/fps/splitgate-fans-rejoice-peer-to-peer-multiplayer-is-coming-next-week-so-you-can-keep-on-playing-after-the-servers-shut-down/ | https://www.highlandernews.org/74887/the-war-on-wifi-fighting-game-communities-unite-to-thrive-and-survive-in-laggy-quarantine-times/

### Comparables
- R.E.P.O. (Semiwork) (2025-2026, $9.99): 200k+ peak CCU, 96% positive on 260k+ reviews, still in EA with Cosmetics Update May 2026 LESSON: Biweekly devlogs + a named update every few months + earned (never sold) cosmetics kept recent reviews at 94% positive; no-IAP is explicitly credited by press and players as part of its reputation.
- PEAK (Aggro Crab + Landfall) (2025-2026, $7.99): 10M copies by Aug 2026 LESSON: Three named content drops in 12 months each re-spiked CCU (171k after Aug-2026 Mesa/Final Ascent); the teams then openly announced a wind-down rather than going silent.
- BattleBit Remastered (3 devs) (2023-2025, $14.99): 87,323 peak CCU (Jun 2023) → 1,046 (Jun 2025) → ~200 (2026) LESSON: ~7 months of developer silence (Nov 2024 → Jun 2025) turned an 84% all-time score into 27% recent; cadence and visibility matter more than launch size.
- Boundary (Surgical Scalpels) (2023-2024, $24.99): Delisted June 2024, still unavailable Jan 2026 LESSON: 100k copies in 24h did not matter: −90.74% players in month one, then publisher/dev fight over missing updates ended it in 14 months.
- Rematch (Sloclap) (2025, $29.99): 1M week one → avg 8,037 CCU by Aug 2025, ~3k by late 2025 LESSON: Netcode, cheaters, no crossplay at launch and thin content cost 60% of Steam players July→August 2025; launch-window stability is the first retention gate.
- Kiln (Double Fine / Xbox) (2026, $19.99 (Game Pass day one)): Dead on Steam at launch LESSON: 193 peak unique Steam users on day one despite a major publisher; few modes and locked mechanics were the top complaints. Party games need a party to already exist.
- Turbo Golf Racing (Hugecalf) (2022-2026, $19.99): 1.0 April 2024, effectively dead on Steam LESSON: Console/Game Pass exposure and a 1.0 did not build a Steam population: 5 concurrent Steam players in April 2026.
- Omega Strikers (Odyssey Interactive) (2023-2026, $F2P): 19,236 peak CCU → ~200 by 2026 LESSON: Even a F2P sports-brawler with funding could not hold a queue; development ended with a custom-games update and 50% layoffs.
- Level Zero: Extraction (Doghowl / tinyBuild) (2024-2025, $19.99): Development ended Feb 2025 LESSON: 'No longer feasible for our relatively small teams' is the modern failure mode: the exit is fast, public, and leaves servers up with no content.
- Hypercharge: Unboxed (Digital Cybercherries, 6 people) (2020-2025, $19.99): Alive and selling in 2024-2025 LESSON: Offline play with commandable bots + 13 free updates over four years produced 100k Xbox sales in under a month in 2024 and a community that never needs a queue.
- Splitgate 2 (1047 Games) (2025, $F2P): Reverted to beta July 2025 LESSON: Launched too early with pricey MTX, went 'back to beta' in seven weeks with 45 layoffs; even survival at ~1k CCU comes with 'dead lobbies' reviews.
- Rocket League (Psyonix, 2015 — dated) (2015, $19.99 at launch (free via PS Plus)): Genre-defining hit LESSON: It seeded its population by giving the game away (PS Plus, 5M downloads in month one) with day-one crossplay and bots/splitscreen; a paid, single-platform, no-bots launch is the opposite of what worked.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) Every mode playable instantly solo or with bots, with humans dropping into bot matches; the store page says so
- (both, must-have) A visible, sustainable update rhythm: devlog every ~2 weeks, patch monthly, named content update every 2-3 months, public roadmap on the store page
- (both, must-have) Stable netcode and matches that do not die when one player leaves
- (hardcore, must-have) Few, consolidated queues rather than many vessel-locked ones
- (hardcore, strong) No microtransactions in a paid game, stated up front
- (both, strong) Earned cosmetics / self-expression (vessel and domain looks) unlocked by play
- (both, strong) Steam friend invites via the overlay and Steam achievements (Steamworks hygiene)
- (hardcore, strong) Honest player-count expectations (players check SteamDB before buying multiplayer indies)
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) Daily/weekly seeded challenge with a trustworthy leaderboard
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) UGC / Workshop or share-code sharing of player creations
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) A low-cost supporter pack DLC as a tip jar (not read as IAP)
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) Scheduled community play nights / custom lobbies the community can run itself

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Plan for a launch population of tens to low hundreds of CCU, not thousands: every 2025 data point says a paid indie PvP game's queue is dead within weeks. Cosmic Shore's ~16 modes x vessel lock x 4 intensities x 1-4 seats is dozens of matchmaking pools; at 100 CCU that is a few players per pool. Collapse public matchmaking to one or two entry points (e.g. 'Maelstrom now' and 'party with friends') and treat mode/intensity choice as something a host sets for a lobby, not a queue key.
- The AI backfill up to 12 and the single-player hub are the correct death-spiral hedge; make them the default rather than the fallback. Every match should start in seconds with bots, humans should drop into running bot matches, and the store page should state 'fully playable solo and with bots' in the first paragraph. This is also the only defence against 'dead game' reviews, which players write on the basis of SteamDB before they buy.
- Cutting host migration is a retention risk specific to player-hosted Relay: when the host quits, everyone's match ends. Rematch and Splitgate 2 show launch-window instability is the first churn driver. If migration cannot ship, at minimum guarantee that a host leaving returns the party to a lobby with bots in one step and never to the main menu, and prefer letting the AI-heavy match continue on the remaining machine.
- 'No Steamworks SDK at launch' is a bigger cost than it looks: achievements are hygiene, but Steam overlay friend invites are the exact friend-recruitment channel that carried R.E.P.O., PEAK and Schedule I. UGS Friends is not where Steam players' friends are. Wire Steamworks for friends/invites/rich presence (and achievements while there) before EA launch; it is a small lift relative to the platform already built.
- Adopt and publish a cadence the 8-person team can hold for 12 months: a devlog every two weeks, a patch monthly, a named content update every quarter (a vessel, a mode, or an episode), and a roadmap block on the store page. Use Valve's planned-1.0 field only when the date is real; never go more than ~6 weeks without an update-type event post. BattleBit's 84%->27% review swing was communication, not code.
- Stop treating 1.0 as the real launch. Only 20% of 2025 EA graduates did better at 1.0 and median 1.0 revenue was ~40% of EA's first month. The EA launch is the launch: the vessel roster, the bots, the social hooks and the no-IAP story all need to be present on day one, and the 12-month roadmap should be about re-viralizing moments (a new vessel that changes how a mode plays, a new episode) rather than a graduation.
- Keep 'no IAP' and say it loudly (R.E.P.O.'s reputation is explicitly built on it), but 'no cosmetics' is a different and costly decision.
