Earned cosmetics are the top request in the R.E.P.O. community and Cosmic Shore already has a vessel customization system and 4000-crystal vessel unlocks; add earned vessel/domain looks tied to challenges and mode mastery as the D30 reason to return. A $3-5 supporter pack DLC (2-7% attach in the data) is standard and not read as IAP.
- The weekly challenge + UGS leaderboard is the right shape but is a supplement, not a retention engine; add a daily seeded run on the same machinery (cheap) and plan the anti-cheat story for an anonymous-auth leaderboard now, because a broken leaderboard is a visible failure (Megabonk).
- The 16-painting gallery with WebGL share export and the Wanderway microscene conveyor are seeds of UGC, and UGC is the strongest measured longevity lever (+75% CCU retention at 2 years). The cheap version is share codes for paintings/microscene recipes in-game; Workshop can wait for post-1.0. Do not spend on this before bots, friends and cadence.
- Community events should be a fixed weekly dev-hosted play night (the population goes quiet between events), not tournaments; and ship custom lobbies/private matches from day one so the community can run its own events without the studio.
- Given median PC D30 of ~1% and 18-minute sessions, Cosmic Shore's short party modes fit the session shape, but the progression (20 wins per vessel, no cosmetics, no achievements) gives a $15 buyer thin reasons to return at week four. Whatever D30 hook is chosen (earned looks, episodes, weekly/daily) must be visible at purchase, because 'a casual game for hardcore gamers' promises depth that the current progression does not show.
- Bench the 'more vessels and modes' roadmap against fragmentation: every vessel-locked mode added splits the population further. Prioritise roadmap items that add reasons to return to existing modes (new cells, new episodes, earned looks, a vessel that plays across several modes) over new single-vessel modes.

### Open questions
- What is the realistic launch CCU given current wishlists? Below ~500 CCU, public PvP matchmaking should be treated as decorative and the launch designed around parties and bots.
- Can join-in-progress be implemented on the Relay/NGO stack so a human can replace an AI mid-match, and what does a host quit do to the remaining players today?
- Is 'no Steamworks SDK' a hard constraint (legal/time) or a scheduling choice? Friends/invites and achievements are the two features this lens would pull forward.
- Is 'no cosmetics' a design philosophy (the vessel is the expression) or a resourcing gap? Earned, non-purchasable looks are compatible with the no-IAP stance and are the most-requested item in comparable communities.
- What cadence can 8 people actually sustain for 12 months alongside platform work, and who owns the devlog? A cadence promised and missed is worse than a smaller one kept.
- How will the weekly-challenge leaderboard survive cheating on anonymous UGS auth, and will UGS Friends have any population when Steam friends are not surfaced?
- Are there PostHog funnels planned for D1/D7/D30 and for 'time to first match start' so the bot-first design can be measured against the ~7%/4%/1% PC medians?


## LENS: How small indie games get found and how a tagline works in 2025-2026

Discovery in 2025-26 runs on three things, and the tagline is downstream of all of them. First, the Steam algorithm plus a demo, not social media: Zukowski's Aug 2026 case studies show YouTube Shorts worth 769 wishlists against 36,946 from Steam's New & Trending Free and 15,800 from Next Fest, while TikTok organic reach for devlogs fell from ~800k views to 300-800. Second, friend groups: the 2025 unit-sales leaders (R.E.P.O., Schedule I, PEAK, Chained Together, Content Warning, Webfishing) are all co-op, all under $10, all proximity-voice, all launched with a one-sentence mechanic and a moment strangers can clip; they converted wishlists at 67-266x the median. "Co-op" is the fastest-rising word in the descriptions of games earning >$100k; PvP does not appear. Third, momentum before the fest: Next Fest is an amplifier, not a discovery engine (pre-fest wishlists vs gain, Spearman 0.825; entrants with <1k wishlists gained a median 322 in Feb 2026).

Cosmic Shore's structural position against this: no Steam page found, no wishlists, a $15 price at 2-3x the breakout band, PvP-first domain modes with AI backfill instead of co-op, no voice chat, Windows-only, English-only, and the October 2026 Next Fest registration already closed on Aug 31. The only Next Fest still open is Feb 22-Mar 1 2027 (register by Jan 10, demo by Feb 8), and an Early Access launch counts as release, so an EA date before March 1 2027 forfeits Next Fest forever.

On the tagline: "a casual game for hardcore gamers" is a positioning statement, not a hook. It names an audience, not a mechanic; every breakout one-liner names a genre plus one twist ("the poker roguelike", "a co-op climbing game where the slightest mistake can spell your doom", "soccer with rocket cars"). Zukowski's own essay argues Steam buyers are hardcore and "casual" is a word developers use as a talisman; capsule best practice is zero to three words and no taglines. It reads as neither/nor because it asks the hardcore reader to accept a self-applied "casual" label (a signal of shallowness) while the casual reader is not on Steam reading it. It is also structurally the "easy to learn, hard to master" cliche. Keep it for the deck; put a mechanic on the capsule. The game already contains several candidate hooks (trail becomes the track; sword-soccer; a living ecosystem that eats your trail), and the honest next step is to pick ONE vessel-mode pair as the beachhead, write three mechanic hooks, and test them on a Steam page and in 15-second vertical clips before spending anything else.

### Findings
- [verified] Steam Next Fest is an amplifier, not a discovery engine: June 2026 had 4,382 tracked demos (+66% YoY), a true median of ~200 wishlists gained, a top-10% floor of ~3,000 (down ~25% YoY), and a 0.825 Spearman correlation between pre-fest wishlists and wishlists gained.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo counted 4,382 demos; the Recognizing Patterns analysis of the survey data reports 806 survey median vs ~200 true median, 95th percentile at 13,461, top earner 57,074, and Spearman 0.825 between pre-fest count and gain. Top 10% added ~3,000, down ~25% from June 2025 due to saturation.

- [verified] It takes wishlists to earn wishlists: entering Next Fest with <1,000 wishlists yields a median 322 gained; 1k-10k yields 1,006; 10k-100k yields 5,215. Demos released well before the fest earned ~2.5x more, and 68-88% of fest wishlists come from the store page, not from playing the demo.

- [verified] Cosmic Shore has already missed October 2026 Next Fest; the only remaining edition it can use is Feb 22-Mar 1 2027 (register by Jan 10 2027, demo by Feb 8), the game must stay unreleased until after Mar 1 2027, an Early Access launch counts as a release, and a game may appear in exactly one Next Fest ever.

### Comparables
- PEAK (Aggro Crab + Landfall) (2025, $7.99): 1M copies in 6 days, 11M+ by 2026, on a <$200k, 4-week jam build; revealed and launched within 5 days on the back of existing community managers and streamer pickup. LESSON: The hook is a mechanic plus a consequence, the price lets a whole friend group buy in, and the 5-day reveal only worked because the studios already owned an audience; a first-time studio cannot skip the runway.
- R.E.P.O. (semiwork) (2025, $9.99): 2025's best-selling new game by units (third-party est. ~18M, ~$147M); 96% of 138k reviews. LESSON: Clip-worthiness is engineered: physics failure, voice chat, and a camera that shows friends reacting. A game without voice or a reaction channel has to work much harder to be clipped.
- Schedule I (TVGS, solo) (2025, $19.99): ~8M units / ~$151M; broke via CaseOh/Cyr streams cut into TikTok compilations; no marketing budget. LESSON: Higher price is survivable only when the one-sentence fantasy is universal and streamers can narrate it; 'multigenre' is not a narratable fantasy.
- Chained Together (Anegar Games) (2024, $2.99 at launch (4.99 now)): 85k CCU at launch, 218k peak Twitch viewers in week one, 10M copies by 2026, publisher deal with Secret Mode. LESSON: Explainable in the title, priced so nobody in the group hesitates, and the failure state (dragging your friends down) is the content.
- Content Warning (Landfall) (2024, $0 for 24h, then 7.99): 6.2M claims in 24 hours, 200k CCU within a day; sustained sales after the free window. LESSON: If the objective is to be clipped, design an in-fiction reason to record, and use a launch lever (free day, 4-pack) to seed groups instead of hoping for organic matchmaking.
- Webfishing (lamedeveloper) (2024, $4.99): 24k CCU from word of mouth, 98% of 6,200 reviews in 12 days; converted wishlists at 67x median. LESSON: Social sandbox virality comes from expressive player identity and hanging out, not from mode count; Cosmic Shore's lava-lamp hub is closer to this than its arcade modes are.
- Buckshot Roulette (Mike Klubnika) (2024, $2.99): 4M copies, 30k+ reviews; two months of solo dev; went viral on itch/TikTok before the Steam release. LESSON: A 15-second vertical clip should contain the whole rule set; if the rules need a paragraph, the clip needs a narrator.
- Balatro (LocalThunk) (2024, $14.99): 1M in a month, 5M by Jan 2025; 98% of 104k reviews; won Best Independent Game. LESSON: A hybrid sells when one half is a universally known noun that does the explaining and the other half is a genre the Steam buyer already loves. 'Space party game' has neither.
- Rocket League (Psyonix) (2015, $19.99): Breakout hybrid after a failed 2008 predecessor; became an esport. (Dated evidence.) LESSON: The same mechanics with a clean noun-plus-twist pitch won on the second try; Cosmic Shore's Astro League ('space soccer with a sword') is the nearest thing it has to this.
- How Many Dudes (Butterscotch Shenanigans) (2026, $14.99): 308k wishlists at launch, 242k units; demo unlocked New & Trending Free (36,946 wishlists in 24 days) and Feb 2026 Next Fest (15,800); YouTube Shorts contributed 769. LESSON: At Cosmic Shore's price and team size, the demo-first Steam-algorithm route is the proven one; but the studio had a decade-old audience.
- Tiny Auto Knights (same studio as Sir, We Have an Orc Problem) (2024, $n/a): 1.5 years of dev, 13k wishlists in 10 months, ~200 reviews after two years; the studio's next 4-month game did $361k in 24 hours. LESSON: Scope and polish did not fix a weak hook; concept and momentum did. A quality game with a fuzzy pitch is the modal outcome, not the exception.
- Keeper (Double Fine) (2025, $n/a): 191 concurrent Steam players despite strong reviews; performed better on Game Pass. LESSON: Reviews and press do not create recommendation; a repeatable 10-15 minute session with a shareable outcome does.
- Supervise (2025, $n/a): Left Early Access in 2025, lost 99% of players within five months, then shut down. LESSON: Multiplayer games die of empty lobbies, not bad reviews; AI backfill and co-op-vs-AI are the insurance, and they must be front and centre in the pitch.
- BOMBANANA (June 2026 Next Fest #1 demo) (2026, $n/a): 675k demo players, ~40k CCU, the most-wishlisted demo of the largest Next Fest ever. LESSON: The 2026 fest winner is still 'co-op + one absurd constraint + one sentence'. Cosmic Shore's demo needs an equivalent sentence to enter Feb 2027 with.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) A one-sentence mechanic hook (genre noun + one twist) that a streamer can put in a stream title and a viewer can grasp in a 15-second vertical clip
- (both, must-have) Co-op play with friends, with proximity voice and physics/failure comedy, at a price a whole group buys without hesitation (under $10, or a 4-pack)
- (both, strong) Sessionable rounds (10-20 min) that end in a result the player can recommend from, plus meta-progression so short sessions still count
- (hardcore, must-have) A demo live on Steam months before any fest, with 2,000-8,000 wishlists banked before Next Fest and 7,000+ before launch
- (hardcore, strong) Store copy and tags that read as ONE known genre so Steam's tag-driven themed fests (Racing, PvP, Couch Co-op) auto-select the game
- (casual, strong) A visible reason to record: an in-fiction or in-system reason clips exist, and human reactions in the clip
- (both, strong) Creator-friendly onboarding: instant lobby with friends or bots, keys distributed via Keymailer/Lurkit to mid-tier co-op streamers rather than press
- (hardcore, must-have) A 90%+ review score and 50+ reviews within days of launch, because Steam's organic surfaces are gated on both
- (both, strong) Simplified Chinese at minimum
- (hardcore, strong) An Early Access day treated as the real launch (hook, demo, fest, creators all pointed at it), since only 21% of EA games earn more at 1.0

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- The tagline verdict: 'a casual game for hardcore gamers' is an internal positioning statement (who it is for and what tone), not a player-facing hook (what you do). Every 2024-26 breakout one-liner is a genre noun plus one twist and none names an audience; Zukowski's standing advice is that 'casual' is a talisman word that signals shallowness to the exact Steam buyer you want, and casual players are not on Steam reading capsules. It therefore risks neither/nor. Keep it in the deck as the design thesis; put a mechanic sentence on the Steam page and zero-to-three words on the capsule.
- The old tagline is not better: 'The party game for pilots' names a format and a fantasy but still no mechanic, and 'party game' on Steam reads as local-multiplayer minigame collection, which the game is not (no couch co-op listed). Both taglines describe the CATALOGUE; the market buys ONE loop.
- Pick a beachhead vessel-mode pair and write three candidate hooks from its fundamentals, then test them (Steam page short description, a Reddit post, three 15-second vertical clips) before any other spend. Candidates the game already contains: 'a drift racer where the track is the trail you just laid' (Squirrel/Skim Race), 'space soccer, but you swing a sword' (Rhino/Astro League), 'lay a trail; it grows a living reef that other players' creatures eat' (the ecosystem). The multi-vessel promise belongs in the 'content' third of the trailer, never the first ten seconds.
- Next Fest timing is now a hard constraint: October 2026 registration closed Aug 31. The only edition left is Feb 22-Mar 1 2027 (register by Jan 10, demo by Feb 8), and an Early Access launch before Mar 1 2027 forfeits Next Fest permanently. Publish the Steam page this month, run Steam Playtests through Q4 to bank 2,000+ wishlists, and set the EA date after March 1 2027; otherwise plan to launch without a Next Fest at all.
- $15 is 2-3x the price band of every co-op breakout (PEAK $7.99, R.E.P.O. $9.99, Chained Together $2.99-4.99, Webfishing $4.99) and games over $10 convert wishlists at roughly 0.10x. For a 1-4 player party game whose value is friends buying together, either price at $9.99, ship a discounted 4-pack (which needs no Steamworks SDK beyond store config), or accept that the friend-group channel is closed.
- The strongest discovery signal of 2025-26 is co-op, and Cosmic Shore's front door is PvP domain races with AI backfill. Co-op scales down to two friends against bots and up to a full lobby with no matchmaking population; PvP without a population is the Supervise failure mode. Promote the co-op-vs-AI variants (2v2 Co-op vs AI, Co-op Wildlife Blitz) to the launch-facing modes, or make every domain mode legibly 'you and your friends vs the bots' in the pitch.
- Clip-worthiness needs a reaction channel. Every breakout carries proximity voice; Cosmic Shore lists none, and the Steamworks SDK (Steam Voice) is cut from EA. UGS Vivox is already in the stack's vendor family; without some voice path the game's clips are silent physics with no faces, which is what the TikTok data says stops working.
- Do not budget for press; budget creator seeding. The press pool shrank 25% and favours listicles. Use Keymailer's free tier plus direct outreach to 50-200 mid-tier co-op/variety streamers, with a lobby that fills instantly with bots so a streamer never waits.
- Localisation was cut, but the Next Fest data says Simplified Chinese is the single cheapest ceiling to remove (8/10 top demos from Taiwanese publishers; 46% of entries localised). English-only Windows-only at $15 stacks three self-imposed ceilings on a game that has no wishlist base yet.
- Treat EA day as THE launch: only 21% of EA games earn more at 1.0, and long EA periods convert at 0.07x. Do not hold the best hook, mode, or vessel back for 1.0; the first-play virality happens once.
- Themed fests to target with tags: PvP Fest (Feb, competitive multiplayer; the domain modes qualify), Racing Fest Apr 12-19 2027 (only if the store page reads as primarily a racer, which a 16-mode catalogue will not), Couch Co-Op Fest Feb 8-15 2027 (requires local co-op; the game has none). None of the H2 2026 fests fit, so the fest calendar cannot substitute for a Next Fest.
- Design the demo as the vertical slice of the one hook, with 20-60 minutes of median playtime (under 10 minutes suppresses fest ranking) and 30-45 minute session shape; a 'sampler of 16 modes' demo will read as unfocused and rank poorly.
- Sessionability is on the game's side: 1-4 player rounds with a race-to-N end condition and a Maelstrom meta already match the 10-15 minute recommendable-run shape. State the round length in the pitch.

### Open questions
- Is there a Steam store page yet, and what is the current wishlist count? Search found only itch.io and Discord; every timeline above starts from that number.
- Is the Early Access date before or after March 1 2027? Before means no Next Fest ever; the go/no-go on that trade should be explicit.
- Which single vessel-mode pair is the beachhead for the first ten seconds of the trailer and the short description: Scarab Scramble (the doc's designated beachhead), Astro League (sword soccer), or Squirrel/Skim Race (trail becomes track)? Has any of them been tested as a 15-second vertical clip?
- Is there any voice chat path (UGS Vivox) given Steamworks SDK is cut? Without it the co-op clip channel is silent.
- Does any mode support local co-op / hotseat (the old site copy mentions hotseat)? That decides Couch Co-Op Fest eligibility and whether 'party game' is honest on Steam.
- What does the game read as when Steam's tag-based auto-selection runs: racing, space, PvP, party, arcade? The 'primarily about the theme' rule means a 16-mode page may qualify for nothing.
- Is a $9.99 price or a discounted 4-pack acceptable to the business plan, and what is the modelled friend-group conversion at $15 vs $10?
- How large and how active is the Discord, and has a weekend playtest cadence (20-30 engaged testers) ever run?
- What is the cost of Simplified Chinese for UI-only text versus the measured discovery ceiling, given the localisation cut?
- Can the AI backfill keep a streamer's lobby full and fun with zero other humans, since that is the on-ramp every creator will hit first?


## LENS: Mobile, handheld, console and cross-play for a premium party/competitive game in 2026

Premium mobile is a real but narrow market in 2026, and it is not the market Cosmic Shore is shaped for. Mobile spending is flat-to-down (IAP -2% YoY to $40B in H1 2026; downloads -7.2% in 2025), ad revenue now exceeds 70% of mobile game revenue in the US, and a paid install costs $3-5 on iOS. The premium wins are Balatro-shaped: single-player, pausable, one-thumb, arriving on phones seven months after a PC reputation was already made. Premium ports of real-time core games bombed (3k-10k units each for 2024's AAA iPhone ports), Vampire Survivors went free-with-optional-ads because "the mobile market doesn't work that way", and the two premium-subscription refuges have retreated: Apple Arcade toward family IP with six-month payment delays, Netflix Games from mobile-first to TV/cloud party games while cutting five studios. Cross-play mobile party games show what the long tail looks like after virality: Among Us at 25M MAU with ~$1.7M/yr of mobile IAP, Stumble Guys -69% YoY with a console build that cannot join mobile/PC lobbies.

Where hardcore gamers actually play "casually" is the handheld PC. GDC's 2026 survey: 28% of developers already build for Steam Deck (4th platform, ahead of iOS/Android at 23%) and interest in Deck equals Switch 2 (40% vs 39%). Deck is ~0.7% of Steam users but ~2.75% of playtime, and Valve's 2025 Deck most-played chart is indies (Balatro, Vampire Survivors, Hades). Valve's Steam Machine (June 2026, $1,049) and Microsoft's Project Helix (runs Windows/Steam games; dev kits 2027; "build once, ship everywhere") mean a Windows x64 Steam build IS the living-room strategy by 2027-28, with no certification. Switch 2 (23.7M units by June 2026) is the one console worth a port conversation for a party game, but Nintendo is gating the eShop, Unity's Switch 2 module needs Nintendo approval, and delayed ports return ~10% of Steam sales.

Console cert for a UGS Relay game is not the blocker people assume (Relay is platform-agnostic; UGS offers console sign-in), but cross-network play adds real requirements (Xbox XR-007: badge Xbox users among non-Xbox players, honour safety settings, disclose; Sony's cross-play royalty with audit rights), and Cosmic Shore's anonymous auth has no cross-progression identity at all.

Recommendation: keep the mobile BUILD alive as a performance gate and input path, not as a product. No storefront presence, no paid mobile SKU, no F2P conversion during EA. Spend the year making the Steam build Deck-Verified-grade (16:10 UI, gamepad text entry, frame-rate floor at Frenzy volume), replace anonymous auth with a linkable account before any second platform, and revisit Switch 2 only after 1.0 with 1,000+ Steam reviews.

### Findings
- [verified] Mobile game IAP revenue fell 2% YoY to $40B in H1 2026 while ad spend rose 7% to $7B; ad revenue is now >70% of mobile game revenue in the US and several other countries. Downloads flat at 24B.
  - evidence: Sensor Tower H1 2026 Digital Gaming Market Index as reported 31 Jul 2026: IAP $40B (-2%), downloads 24B (stable), ad spend $7B (+7%); 'Ad revenue represented more than 70% of total mobile game revenue in several countries, including the United States'; AppLovin + AdMob ~65% of mobile gaming ad revenue.
  - sources: https://www.mobilemarketingreads.com/mobile-gaming-in-app-purchase-revenue-declined-2-in-h1-2026-as-ad-spend-reached-new-highs/
- [likely] 2025 mobile game downloads fell 7.2% to 50.4B and, for the first time, non-gaming apps out-earned games in IAP revenue.
  - evidence: Aggregated 2026 market statistics citing 50.4B downloads in 2025 (-7.2% vs 2024), consumer spend $81.7B (+1.3%), and non-gaming apps overtaking games in IAP revenue in 2025.
  - sources: https://www.blog.udonis.co/mobile-marketing/mobile-games/mobile-gaming-statistics | https://www.globalgamesforum.com/features/state-of-gaming-heres-what-the-mobile-data-actually-says
- [likely] Paid user acquisition on mobile costs ~$4.22/install on iOS and ~$2.97 on Android in 2026; iOS runs 3-4x Android in the same market/genre; gaming CPI rose ~30% in 2026 and North America gaming CPI rose 31% in 2025; the industry spent ~$25B on UA in 2025.
  - evidence: Game Growth Advisor benchmark page (17 Mar 2026, updated 15 Aug 2026) citing TopOn, Liftoff, Adjust, Admiral Media, AppsFlyer: Android US casual $1.50-3.50, 'apply a 3x to 4x multiplier' for iOS. Foxdata/Megadigital 2026 summaries: iOS $4.22 / Android $2.97 averages, Adjust reporting gaming CPI +30% in 2026, NA gaming CPI +31% to $1.68 in 2025, $25B UA spend in 2025.
  - sources: https://gamegrowthadvisor.com/blog/2026-03-17-user-acquisition-cpi-benchmarks-2026/ | https://foxdata.com/en/blogs/2026-mobile-game-user-acquisition-cost-benchmarks-how-much-should-you-spend/ | https://megadigital.ai/en/blog/cpi-mobile-game-guide/
- [likely] Balatro is the premium-mobile exception, not the rule: $9.99, ~$937k gross in its first 7 days (Sep 2024), ~$4.4M gross by Nov 2024, >$9.3M net and 3.1M downloads by Jan 2025, 49% of mobile revenue from the US; 5M units across all platforms by Jan 2025. It launched on mobile 7 months after PC and is single-player, pausable and one-thumb.
  - evidence: PocketGamer.biz / Game World Observer / GameDev Reports coverage of AppMagic estimates; Playstack's 5M announcement (Jan 2025).
  - sources: https://www.pocketgamer.biz/balatro-nears-44m-on-mobile-amid-a-sudden-spending-surge/ | https://gameworldobserver.com/2025/01/21/balatro-another-1-5-million-copies-total-5m-units | https://gamedevreports.substack.com/p/games-and-numbers-january-8-january | https://www.playstack.com/news/balatro-5-million-copies-sold/
- [verified] Hardcore gamers do not buy premium real-time core games on phones: the 2024 AAA iPhone ports sold ~10k (Death Stranding), ~7k (RE4), ~5.75k (RE Village) and ~3k (AC Mirage) copies at $15-50, while a F2P Assassin's Creed spin-off grossed >$900k in the same window.
  - evidence: Appfigures estimates via Mobilegamer.biz, reported by GameRant 27 Jun 2024. Dated evidence; no 2025-26 premium core port has reversed the pattern in the sources found.

- [likely] Vampire Survivors shipped mobile as free-with-optional-rewarded-ads and no IAP because Poncle 'recognized that the mobile market doesn't work that way' for a paid app; it hit 1M downloads in week one (Dec 2022) and 3M by Jan 2023, and Poncle had to build the port in-house because no partner accepted non-predatory monetisation.
  - evidence: PocketGamer.biz interview; Mobilegamer.biz / Game World Observer AppMagic download counts. Dated (2022-23) but it is the canonical indie-to-mobile decision record.

- [verified] Apple Arcade is no longer an indie refuge: developers report payment delays of up to six months, per-play bonus-pool payouts declining since ~Oct 2020, unresponsive support, and greenlights favouring family-friendly IP (Hello Kitty template). Apple has never disclosed a subscriber count.
