  - sources: https://gamedevreports.substack.com/p/newzoo-cross-platform-players-are | https://coopboardgames.com/statistics/cross-platform-gaming-adoption-rates/

### Comparables
- Balatro (mobile, Sep 2024) (2024-2025, $$9.99 (iOS/Android); Balatro+ on Apple Arcade): >$9.3M net mobile by Jan 2025 on 3.1M downloads; 5M units all platforms by Jan 2025; 2024 Mobile GOTY LESSON: Premium mobile works when a PC hit is already made and the design is phone-native. Cosmic Shore is a 4-player real-time Relay game with no PC reputation yet; it is the opposite shape on every axis.
- Vampire Survivors (mobile, Dec 2022) (2022-2023, $Free with optional rewarded ads, no IAP): 1M downloads week one, 3M by Jan 2023; sustained ~60k installs/day early on LESSON: Even a top-tier indie brand went free on phones. A paid $15 game cannot mirror to mobile at $15; a free mobile SKU means an ad/IAP economy Cosmic Shore has ruled out.
- AAA iPhone ports (Death Stranding, RE4, RE Village, AC Mirage) (2024, $$15-50): ~3,000-10,000 purchases each (Appfigures) LESSON: Console-class hardware on phones did not create a premium core-gamer market. Do not expect the 'hardcore' half of the tagline to buy on mobile.
- Among Us (2018-2026, $$4.99 PC; free mobile): 278M MAU peak (Oct 2020) to 25.3M MAU (Mar 2026); mobile IAP $41.6M (2020) to ~$1.5-1.8M/yr (2022-25) LESSON: Cross-play mobile gives reach, not revenue; the paid PC SKU carried the money. Post-viral, the mobile long tail earns under $2M/yr even at 25M MAU.
- Stumble Guys (2021-2026, $F2P with IAP): $72M peak (2022) to ~$9M (2025); -69% YoY IAP through Apr 2026; console build cannot join mobile/PC lobbies LESSON: Console cross-play with mobile was hard enough that a $5B publisher shipped a separate console silo. Mobile-first party games decay fast; adding platforms did not stop the slide.
- Rocket League Sideswipe / Rocket Racing (2021-2026, $F2P): Sideswipe alive (season 28, Jun 2026) at ~200k installs/month; Rocket Racing shuts Oct 2026 LESSON: The credible mobile move for a physics party game was a separate phone-native product, not a port. Even Epic sunsets what does not engage.
- Fall Guys mobile (Aug 2024) (2024-2025, $F2P): Full cross-play/cross-progression with PC/console; ~80-120k concurrent players across platforms in 2025; no public mobile revenue LESSON: Perfect cross-play mobile did not visibly move the franchise. If Epic cannot show a number, a small team should not bet a year on one.
- Netflix Games / Apple Arcade (premium subscription refuges) (2021-2026, $Subscription-funded, no ads/IAP): Netflix: 5 studios cut, pivot to TV cloud party games (~10% of eligible members tried). Apple Arcade: 6-month payment delays, family-IP greenlights LESSON: Both doors narrowed to big family IP. Netflix's move to TV party games is a signal about where 'party' lives (couch/TV), not phones.

### Market wants
- (hardcore, must-have) Steam Deck Playable/Verified at Early Access launch: 16:10 UI, gamepad-only navigation including text entry, readable HUD at 1280x800, stable frame rate on a 15W APU
- (hardcore, strong) Controller-native, short-session play that reads as 'casual' on a handheld PC while keeping depth (the actual 'casual for hardcore' device)
- (casual, must-have) Free entry on phones with optional ads or IAP; premium price only accepted for phone-native single-player games with prior reputation
- (both, strong) Account continuity / cross-progression across whatever platforms exist (unlocks, crystals, vessels follow the player)
- (both, strong) Cross-play that never fragments lobbies (or is invisible thanks to AI backfill); a console silo like Stumble Guys reads as a broken promise
- (casual, nice-to-have) A Switch 2 / TV-couch version of any party game, eventually
- (both, nice-to-have) No platform-holder dependency that can remove the game from players (Fortnite's 5-year iOS absence)

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Answer to the brief's question: keep the mobile BUILD alive, kill the mobile PRODUCT for the EA year. Keep the iOS/Android build targets compiling in CI as (a) the performance ceiling that forces collider-LOD and the Lattice boot-cell budget to hold, and (b) the home of the touch and one-thumb input strategies that the Deck/handheld path also benefits from. Do not refresh TestFlight/itch, do not list on a store, do not do UA. The cost is a few CI minutes; the benefit is that a 2027-28 mobile decision starts from a working build rather than a rotted one.
- A paid mobile SKU at $15 has no comparable: premium real-time core games sell 3-10k units on iPhone; the only premium wins are single-player, pausable, phone-native games arriving after PC fame. A free mobile SKU means an ad/IAP economy the plan has explicitly ruled out, and paid installs cost $3-5 on iOS with nothing to recoup them. Either mobile path during EA is buying users to give them away.
- The 'casual game for hardcore gamers' device is the Steam Deck, not the phone: GDC 2026 has more developers on Deck (28%) than on iOS/Android (23%), Deck interest equals Switch 2 interest, and Valve's 2025 Deck chart is indies. Cosmic Shore's Windows x64 build already reaches Deck through Proton; what is missing is Deck-grade polish. Concrete work: 16:10-safe layouts for the ability lockup row and top bar, a gamepad path through the username-setup panel (no Steamworks SDK means no ShowGamepadTextInput; verify the Steam overlay keyboard suffices or defer name entry), a frame-rate floor measured on a Deck at Frenzy volume in the heaviest cells (Atlantis ~69k, Lattice ~43k at cap), and the noted PS-glyph gap on the control chip (Deck presents as an Xbox pad, so the shipped Xbox art is correct there).
- Verify UGS Relay/Netcode under Proton on a real Deck before EA launch: Unity says Relay is platform-agnostic but hedged it ('should work'), and the project's threading and offline-fallback paths (OfflineModeService, ReconnectService) have never been exercised on SteamOS. A Deck player who cannot host is a 1-star review.
- Consoles are a post-1.0 conversation and the Windows build IS the console strategy for Xbox: Project Helix runs Windows/Steam games natively (dev kits 2027), the unified GDK is 'build once, ship everywhere', and Valve's Steam Machine ($1,049, Jun 2026) puts SteamOS on the TV now. Do not chase ID@Xbox dev kits during EA (approved indies are stuck without them anyway) and do not take on Sony's cross-play royalty/audit terms for a game with no cross-platform revenue to protect.
- Switch 2 is the only console port worth modelling for a party game, and only after Steam proves the genre: Nintendo is gating the Switch 2 eShop against 'slop', Unity's Switch 2 module needs Nintendo approval, and delayed ports return ~10% of Steam sales. Trigger for the conversation: 1.0 shipped with 1,000+ Steam reviews. Budget: $5-9k per platform for a porting house PLUS the multiplayer/auth/cert work those quotes exclude (Relay + platform identity + 6-10 weeks cert + resubmissions).
- Design the lobby for cross-network play now even though no second platform ships: XR-007 requires visually identifying Xbox-network users among non-Xbox players, an opt-out of cross-network play, and comms restricted by platform safety settings. The party roster (ArcadeLobbyList / FriendInfoSlot) should carry a per-player 'network' field and the launch panel an opt-out flag today; the UI is trivial now and a retrofit later. Voice/text chat does not exist yet, which keeps the safety-settings surface small.
- Anonymous UGS auth is the real blocker to any second platform, not certification: with anonymous identity, a player's 4,000-crystal vessel purchases and quest progression are welded to one device, and cross-platform players are the ones who pay (Newzoo: +107% paying share). Add Unity Player Accounts (or a Steam-linked identity) as the account of record before EA ends, so that whichever platform comes second inherits progression on day one.
- AI backfill to 12 is Cosmic Shore's structural defence against cross-play fragmentation (a second platform never splits lobbies below 'playable'), which is a genuine advantage over Stumble Guys' console silo. But it does not solve the economics, and it does not make a phone a good place to play a 4-ability, 4-element, real-time drift/shooter/melee game.
- If a mobile presence must exist for investor or community reasons, the evidence-backed shape is Rocket League Sideswipe's: a separate phone-native product (e.g., a one-thumb Squirrel drift-racer or the Wanderway conveyor as an endless runner) rather than a port of the whole game. That is a 2027 product decision, not an EA-year task.
- Netflix's pivot to TV party games and Valve's Steam Machine both say 'party lives on the couch'. A Steam-first party game reaches the couch via Steam Machine/Big Picture with zero porting. Treat local-play affordances (multiple gamepads on one PC, Remote Play Together) as cheaper party-reach than any mobile build; note Remote Play Together works without the Steamworks SDK.

### Open questions
- Does Unity 6 + NGO 2.5 + UGS Relay/Lobby/CloudSave behave correctly under Proton on SteamOS (TLS, WebSockets, main-thread dispatcher timing)? No public evidence either way; needs a Deck test before EA.
- Without the Steamworks SDK, can a Deck player complete the username-setup panel with the Steam overlay keyboard alone, or does Verified require ShowGamepadTextInput? Valve's review criteria are not public in detail.
- Is Sony's 2019 cross-play royalty (85% ratio, $500k PSN threshold, audit rights) still in force in 2026? Only May 2021 trial exhibits exist; no confirmation or retraction found.
- What are the NDA-only console netcode policies for NGO/Relay (e.g., required use of platform matchmaking or voice APIs)? Unity's docs defer to platform docs; a porting house or Unity partner would need to answer.
- Google Play Pass developer economics in 2026: no primary evidence surfaced; unclear whether it remains a meaningful premium channel at all.
- Fall Guys mobile commercial results are unpublished; the one large-IP 'do cross-play mobile right' case cannot be scored.
- How does a 50-70k-prism cell at Frenzy volume perform on a Steam Deck (Zen 2 / RDNA 2, 15W)? The collider budget was set for mobile-first origins but has never been measured on Deck-class hardware per the docs found.
- Will Switch 2 eShop gating loosen for multiplayer indies by 2027, and what does the Switch 2 Unity add-on cost in practice? New Blood's Mar 2026 account suggests approval remains discretionary.
- Would Apple Arcade or Netflix fund an ad-free multiplayer variant given both have moved to family IP? Untested, and the payment-delay and studio-closure evidence argues against relying on it.
- Should the anonymous-auth to linkable-account migration happen before EA launch (protecting early adopters' progression) or after (less launch risk)? The evidence says before any second platform; the trade-off inside the EA window is a team call.


## LENS: How emergent systems / artificial life / simulation depth is valued and marketed to hardcore players

The market splits emergence into three things that sell very differently. (1) Ecosystem-as-antagonist sells: Rain World (94% of 30,384 Steam reviews, est. 2.4-2.5M copies) pitches "ravenous foes… always on the hunt" and "learn the ecosystem"; Monster Hunter Wilds made "living ecosystem" (herds, turf wars, weather flips) a marketing pillar and sold 8M in 3 days (Mar 2025). (2) Emergence-as-verifiable-rule sells: Noita's whole hook is one sentence a viewer can check in 3 seconds ("every pixel is physically simulated… burn, explode or melt anything"; 95% of 47,390). (3) Artificial-life-as-subject does not sell: Species: ALRE (73% of 972 reviews, abandoned in Early Access), Ecosystem (76% of 599 after 1.0 in Nov 2024), Thrive (~21 average concurrent players in Aug 2026), and the Spore successors Adapt/Elysian Eclipse are still "coming soon" as of Sep 2026. Dwarf Fortress and Kenshi are real (1M by Apr 2025; 3M by May 2026) but their publishers say the conditions (pre-existing legend status, decade of creator lore) do not transfer.

What makes emergence read: a legible cause producing a visible, consequential effect inside one shot; creatures interacting with each other and with the player's stuff; and, per Derek Lieu's Helldivers 2 analysis, showing failures — "make the game look cool and don't show mistakes… goes against the vibe." Observed emergence is not self-evident: Ecosystem's store page has to caption "all the creatures in the trailer evolved on their own."

Hardcore players do not want surprise where it decides a scored outcome. Mario Kart World's high-VR players (June 2025) voted Random ~80% to escape chaotic intermissions and called Knockout "RNG lottery"; Hunt: Showdown players call PvE a "dinner bell… enhancement of the pvp" and resent dying to a zombie mid-fight. Chaos is embraced in single-player where it is the identity (Rain World, Noita) and in co-op where failure is shared comedy (Helldivers 2).

Sandbox hubs retain only through UGC: Garry's Mod averages ~24k CCU at 20 years old because it hosts community game modes; Teardown's sandbox sits at ~2.5k avg with objective updates spiking it to 15k and back; Roblox's 10B monthly hours are UGC games. A toy hub without a Workshop-class surface is ambience.

For Cosmic Shore: the ecosystem is today a developer-facing fundamental. Most shipped modes fence fauna out (Astro League exclusion radius, Peel the Cage removed its fauna, Scarab Scramble holds the crew out until Restless); only Brood Rush and Wildlife Liberation make it the objective. Fauna are client-local per-peer Random spawns, which is exactly the "RNG lottery" complaint waiting for a "hardcore" audience. The lava lamp has no UGC surface (no Steamworks at launch). The lens says: lead with the antagonist and the consequence ("your trail is food"), show it eating you, keep it out of scored PvP until it is deterministic across peers, and do not budget retention on toys.

### Findings
- [verified] Pure artificial-life / evolution simulation is a micro-niche on Steam with no evidence of growth in 2025-2026.
  - evidence: Species: Artificial Life, Real Evolution: Mostly Positive 73% of 972 reviews, in Early Access since Sept 28 2018, last developer update 3+ years ago, $19.99. Ecosystem (Slug Disco): 76% of 599 reviews, 1.0 on Nov 8 2024 after EA from Mar 2021, $19.99. Thrive (free): 20.9 avg players last 30 days, all-time peak 235 (Steamcharts, Sept 2026). Spore successors Adapt (Slug Disco) and Elysian Eclipse both still 'To be announced' with zero reviews on Sept 5 2026. Spore itself averages ~544 concurrent players 18 years after release.
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/774541/Species_Artificial_Life_Real_Evolution/ | https://store.steampowered.com/app/1133120/Ecosystem/ | https://steamcharts.com/app/1779200 | https://store.steampowered.com/app/888780/Adapt/ | https://store.steampowered.com/app/1774190/Elysian_Eclipse/ | https://steamcharts.com/app/17390
- [verified] The ecosystem games that sell frame the ecosystem as an ANTAGONIST and a thing to be learned, not as a simulation to observe.
  - evidence: Rain World store copy: 'Each ravenous foe in your path will be cunning, vicious and always on the hunt'; 'learn the ecosystem and turn their strengths to your advantage'; 'Procedural design and individualistic AI'; Bestiary of 'dangerous predators and delicious prey'. 94% positive of 30,384 reviews (91% recent), $24.99. Unofficial estimates put lifetime at ~2.4-2.5M copies / ~$45M gross (GameRevenueData, undated).
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/312520/Rain_World/ | https://gamerevenuedata.com/games/rain-world/
- [verified] Rain World's ecosystem became a selling point only when creators explained it and when community content was packaged and made visible; the publisher's own lesson is that community creativity is invisible by default.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo (2023): AI-focused YouTube videos hit ~1M views in a June 2022 spike; 280,000 base-game units sold Mar 2022-Feb 2023 (its best year, five years after launch); Downpour DLC (mods bundled into a $15 paid pack + free Workshop update) sold 74,000 units Jan 19-Mar 3 2023 at 93% positive. Quote: 'Just because your community is making a bunch of cool stuff, that doesn't mean other people can see it.'
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/rain-world-how-ugc-headlined-a-big
- [verified] More ecosystem did not fix Rain World's 2025 expansion; emergence without direction reads as 'lost'.
  - evidence: Rain World: The Watcher (PC Mar 28 2025, $14.99) sits at 77% of 2,530 reviews all-time and 57% of 40 recent (Sept 2026). Store pitch is pure ecosystem ('Unknown creatures stalk and climb and dive and hunt'). Complaints cluster on 'no directions on where to find echoes', 'lack of clear progression', 'no in-game prompts or hints', softlocks — not on the creatures.
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/2857120/Rain_World_The_Watcher/ | https://www.metacritic.com/game/rain-world-the-watcher/user-reviews/?platform=pc | https://steamcommunity.com/app/312520/discussions/0/601900462106495210/
- [verified] Rain World's developers stated the core trade-off of ecosystem-as-antagonist: autonomous creatures are less reliably fun than designed enemies, and RNG makes first impressions wildly variable.
  - evidence: Joar Jakobsson: 'If you design an enemy to be fun, it will be fun. Whereas if you design an enemy to care for itself then, that might actually be super difficult and frustrating.' James Therrien: 'We had one streamer who opened up the game and the RNG wasn't with her… a bunch of no-win situations… really early on.' Creatures 'outside of your frame… are all still alive.'
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/crafting-the-complex-chaotic-ecosystem-of-i-rain-world-i-
- [verified] Noita shows the winning shape for marketing simulation depth: one sentence stating a rule the viewer can verify on screen in seconds, plus verbs.
  - evidence: Store page: 'a magical action roguelite set in a world where every pixel is physically simulated'; 'Burn, explode or melt anything'. Overwhelmingly Positive 95% of 47,390 (94% of 450 recent), $19.99, tags Physics / Sandbox / Roguelike. Unofficial revenue estimate ~$89.5M gross lifetime.
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/881100/Noita/ | https://games-stats.com/steam/game/noita/ | https://noitagame.com/
- [verified] Trailers for emergent/systemic games must show failures and 'highs and lows'; the conventional 'look cool, hide mistakes' cut actively hides the hook.
  - evidence: Derek Lieu on Helldivers 2's three trailers (Mar 3 2024): the 'Report For Duty' trailer 'avoids showing the lived experience of playing the game (which by all reports involves a lot of dying and fumbling)'; 'By following conventional game trailer production practices of "make the game look cool and don't show mistakes" it actually goes against the vibe'; 'Seeing these humorous deaths provides context which changes our perception of the later shots.' Post-launch virality came from death/ragdoll compilations on TikTok/Shorts with virtually no paid advertising (Feb 2024 launch).
  - sources: https://www.derek-lieu.com/blog/2024/3/3/helldivers-2-trailers-review | https://www.techtimes.com/articles/314268/20260124/how-helldivers-2-became-viral-gaming-phenomenon-without-spending-millions-marketing.htm
- [likely] Observed emergence is not self-evident on screen — even a game about it has to caption the claim.
  - evidence: Ecosystem's Steam description explicitly states 'all the creatures in the trailer evolved on their own in the game' because nothing in the footage proves it. Immersive-sim community consensus (ResetEra thread): 'Showcases for emergent gameplay really only work on the kinds of people who are already into emergent gameplay in the first place' and 'That these games need to be explained so often is probably why they don't sell that much.'
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/1133120/Ecosystem/ | https://www.resetera.com/threads/can-an-immersive-sim-ever-get-mainstream-sales-numbers.687967/
- [likely] A mainstream 'living ecosystem' pitch works when the ecosystem is spectacle you fight inside — herds, monster-vs-monster turf wars, environment flips — attached to a known combat loop.
  - evidence: Monster Hunter Wilds (Feb 28 2025) marketed a world that 'in one moment [is] a severe wilderness, swarming with packs of attacking monsters, and in the next transforms into a rich natural environment teeming with life', with monsters 'moving in search of food or engaging in turf wars'; Capcom announced 8M units in 3 days (Mar 4 2025), its fastest ever.
  - sources: https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250304186922/en/Capcoms-New-Monster-Hunter-Wilds-Roars-Past-8-Million-Units-Sold-with-Ferocious-Speed | https://www.monsterhunter.com/wilds/en-us/ | https://www.gameshub.com/news/features/monster-hunter-wilds-ecosystem-interview-2648212/
- [verified] Dwarf Fortress and Kenshi prove deep-simulation demand exists, but their own publishers say the conditions do not transfer to a new IP.
  - evidence: Dwarf Fortress: 606,342 copies in two months (target was 160,000), 1M by Apr 17 2025; 37% of store visits were direct navigation. Kitfox: 'Anything I say about Dwarf Fortress probably will not apply to your game'; it 'had already achieved legendary status in some corners of the internet before arriving on Steam.' Kenshi: 1M (Oct 2020) → 2.3M (Apr 2024) → 3M (May 26 2026), credited to modders, content creators and players 'who shared the game'.
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/business/dwarf-fortress-has-topped-1-million-sales-on-steam | https://www.gamedeveloper.com/business/kitfox-breaks-down-i-dwarf-fortress-i-600k-copies-of-success | https://wnhub.io/news/finance/item-50939
- [verified] Hardcore competitive players actively route around chaos in scored play and call it an 'RNG lottery'; casual players do not notice the same chaos.
  - evidence: Mario Kart World (June 2025): high-rated players (~8000+ VR) voted 'Random' to force classic 3-lap races over chaotic intermission tracks; community reports ~80% of the player base at 8000 VR selecting only random/3-lap; Nintendo's June 26 2025 update removed that workaround. Player quotes: 'this just ruined all of that practice'; 'Knockout Tour online feels more like RNG lottery than anything else'; a casual counter-voice: 'the casual audience likely won't even notice this change'.
  - sources: https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2025/06/fans-reckon-nintendo-has-killed-mario-kart-world-with-its-latest-update | https://www.resetera.com/threads/nintendo-updates-mario-kart-worlds-online-removes-the-only-guaranteed-way-to-play-normal-3-lap-races-with-randoms.1228638/ | https://www.gamesradar.com/games/racing/nintendo-has-made-my-biggest-problem-with-mario-kart-world-even-worse/
- [verified] In PvPvE, hardcore players value the environment as INFORMATION and pressure on other players, not as content, and resent it deciding a fight.
  - evidence: Hunt: Showdown Steam thread (dated June 20 2022): 'Environmental serves as a kind of dinner bell / alarm to the actual humans playing'; 'Focus is purely PvP, PvE is for enhancement of the pvp'; 'it sucks to be in the middle of a battle to find yourself dying because some zombie decided to show up'; 'The PvE elements… aren't smart enough for any kind of extended challenge'.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/594650/discussions/8/6086066536291286551/
- [likely] Unfairness from systems is embraced when it is the game's stated identity in single-player, and read as 'abusive' by a vocal minority — it is never sold as balance.
  - evidence: Rain World Steam discussions: the game is 'meant to be insanely unfair… It's the point of the game'; 'The creatures are trying to do their own thing, they don't care about you'; dissent: 'losing doesn't teach you anything… Abusive'. Noita coverage: 'the point of Noita is how unfair it is, persevering despite the impossible odds' vs critics on deaths 'for reasons that aren't their fault'.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/312520/discussions/0/3464975762204754482 | https://gamecritics.com/mike-suskie/noita-review/ | https://hardcoregamer.com/reviews/review-noita/389883/
- [verified] Sandbox/toy hubs retain players only when they host user-made objective modes; a sandbox without UGC is a tail, not a surface.
  - evidence: Garry's Mod (2006, $9.99): 24,320 avg CCU last 30 days, 38,810 peak in Aug 2026, 'stable' ~30k daily peaks; the July 2025 update produced 'no noticeable increase'. Its retention rides Workshop game modes (TTT, Prop Hunt). Teardown (sandbox unlocked via campaign): 1,962 avg CCU Sept 2025 → 4,552 avg / 14,994 all-time peak in Mar 2026 (multiplayer with co-op campaign + competitive modes + sandbox) → back to ~2,560 avg by Aug 2026. Minecraft fan stats (unofficial): Survival 42% vs Creative 33% globally; Creative nearly matches Survival only on Bedrock's casual audience.
  - sources: https://steamcharts.com/app/4000 | https://screenrant.com/garrys-mod-update-july-2025-player-count/ | https://steamcharts.com/app/1167630 | https://worthplaying.com/article/2025/3/13/news/145434-teardown-multiplayer-coming-to-campaign-co-op-competitive-and-sandbox-modes-on-pc-later-this-year-trailer/ | https://blockbase-tools.com/statistics/game-mode-distribution/
- [likely] The 'toy hub' that actually retains at scale is a UGC platform: players making objectives for other players.
  - evidence: Analyst estimate: Roblox players spent ~10.25 billion hours per month in 2025 — more than Steam, PlayStation and Fortnite combined; 144M DAU in Q4 2025.
  - sources: https://www.pcgamer.com/gaming-industry/players-spent-roughly-10-250-000-000-monthly-hours-in-roblox-in-2025-analyst-says-more-than-steam-playstation-and-fortnite-combined/
- [verified] Steam's 2025 hit rates favour deep-systems genres over racing/arcade and punish indie multiplayer shooters; the genre league table barely moves year to year.
  - evidence: Chris Zukowski (Jan 27 2026): 20,282 Steam releases in 2025, 608 reached 1,000 reviews (2.99%, best since 2022); 1,000 reviews 'typically correlates with revenue of $150,000+'. Hit rates: Open World Survival Craft 20.8%, Farming 8.3%, Roguelike Deckbuilder 5.1%, Simulation 4.1%, Management 3.4%, Racing 2.1%, 2D Platformer 0.18%; '9 out of the 10 genres at the top of the list in 2025 were exactly the same as the years previous.' Separate 2025 note: indie devs 'should avoid making [multiplayer shooters] as they quickly die'. GameDiscoverCo (Mar 2025): month 1 is ~25% or less of lifetime revenue.
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2026/01/27/what-the-hell-happened-in-2025/ | https://howtomarketagame.com/2025/04/11/the-hit-games-of-early-2025/ | https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/what-steams-own-data-tells-us-about
- [likely] Systems-driven simulation packaged as SHORT, objective-bounded rounds is a proven commercial shape.
  - evidence: Against the Storm (Eremite/Hooded Horse, $29.99) — a roguelite city builder with 'a short playtime with goals players must achieve' — passed 1.5M sales with ~28,000 Overwhelmingly Positive reviews by 2025 and shipped to consoles June 26 2025. Timberborn (water-physics colony sim) passed 1M copies before its 1.0 on Mar 12 2026.
  - sources: https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2025/04/beloved-fantasy-city-builder-against-the-storm-arrives-on-switch-this-june | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Against_the_Storm_(video_game) | https://mechanistry.com/press/lumberpunk-city-builder-timberborn-launches-into-1-0-with-new-features-bonus-content-more
- [likely] Hardcore PC players in 2025 are explicitly saying they do not want another 'sweaty' competitive shooter; casual-fun co-op with chaos is where the appetite is.
  - evidence: PC Gamer (2025): 'PC gamers aren't looking for another sweaty shooter in their lives right now'; Deep Rock Galactic devs note co-op competition has multiplied since they launched. Helldivers 2's $40 co-op with friendly fire went viral with no paid campaign.
  - sources: https://www.pcgamer.com/games/fps/competitive-shooters-are-at-a-crucial-crossroads-in-2025-sweaty-teamplay-vs-casual-fun/ | https://tech.yahoo.com/gaming/articles/even-developers-steam-fave-deep-003456252.html
- [likely] Tears of the Kingdom's physics went viral through clips of player creations that WORK, not clips of the simulation breaking — emergence reads as competence, not chaos.
  - evidence: 'The viral clips of the game show players building amazing structures and having them work flawlessly, rather than showing the physics system shattering dramatically'; the stacked-bridge Ultrahand solution went viral on Twitter days after launch (May 2023) and developers praised it publicly.
  - sources: https://www.gamesradar.com/game-devs-praise-viral-zelda-tears-of-the-kingdom-puzzle-no-physics-engine-i-ever-worked-with-could-do-this-easily/ | https://tomsguide.com/opinion/zelda-tears-of-the-kingdom-made-me-realize-that-physics-are-more-important-than-graphics

### Comparables
- Rain World (+ Downpour 2023, The Watcher 2025) (2017 / 2023 / 2025, $$24.99 base; $14.99 DLCs): 94% of 30,384 reviews; est. 2.4-2.5M copies; best sales year was year five (280k units) driven by creator AI videos + mods packaged as DLC. The Watcher: 77% all-time, 57% recent. LESSON: Sell the creatures as foes to be learned, not a simulation to admire; make the community's discoveries visible (Workshop, curated packs); and pair emergence with direction, or it reads as 'lost'.
- Noita (2020, $$19.99): 95% of 47,390 reviews; unofficial ~$89M gross lifetime. LESSON: Cosmic Shore needs its one verifiable sentence — a rule a viewer can check on screen in three seconds — not a paragraph about conserved mass.
- Helldivers 2 (2024, $$39.99): Viral phenomenon with almost no paid marketing; growth via death/ragdoll clip compilations. LESSON: Show failures in the trailer (your own trail biting you, fauna eating your lead); chaos is welcome when failure is shared and funny, not when it decides a ranked result.
- Monster Hunter Wilds (2025, $$69.99): 8M units in 3 days (Mar 4 2025), Capcom's fastest ever; 'living ecosystem' (herds, turf wars, weather flips) was a headline pillar. LESSON: Ecosystem reads when creatures interact with each other and with you on camera; herd/turf-war/eat-your-mass moments are the shots to build.
- Dwarf Fortress (Steam) (2022, $$29.99): 606,342 copies in 2 months; 1M by Apr 2025. LESSON: Its publisher says the formula is non-transferable: legend status and two decades of player stories preceded the store page. Do not plan on 'depth sells itself'.
- Kenshi (2018, $$29.99): 1M (2020) → 2.3M (2024) → 3M (May 2026). LESSON: Systems-depth revenue is a long tail powered by creators; budget a creator/explainer pipeline (codex, dev diaries, mod hooks) rather than expecting a launch spike.
- Garry's Mod (2006, $$9.99): ~24k average CCU in Aug 2026 at 20 years old; no bump from a 2025 content update. LESSON: A freestyle hub without UGC/Workshop is ambience, not retention; Cosmic Shore's toys need a public creation surface or should be treated as onboarding.
- Teardown (2022 (1.0) / 2025-26 multiplayer, $$29.99): 1.1M by Aug 2022; ~2k avg CCU in late 2025, spiked to 14,994 peak in Mar 2026 on multiplayer (co-op campaign + competitive + sandbox), settled back to ~2.5k avg. LESSON: Objective modes bring people; the sandbox keeps a small core. Ship toys as the dessert, not the meal.
- Species: ALRE / Ecosystem / Thrive / Adapt / Elysian Eclipse (2018 / 2024 / 2021 / TBA / TBA, $$19.99 / $19.99 / free / TBA / TBA): 972 reviews (abandoned EA); 599 reviews after 1.0; ~21 avg CCU; two Spore successors still unreleased in Sept 2026. LESSON: 'Watch evolution happen' has no audience worth building a launch on; A-life credibility belongs in the codex and dev blog, not the tagline.
- Mario Kart World (2025, $$79.99): Massive seller; June 2025 online update triggered a hardcore revolt over chaotic intermission tracks vs 3-lap races ('RNG lottery'). LESSON: A 'casual game for hardcore gamers' must let the hardcore opt out of outcome-deciding chaos, or make the chaos symmetric and readable enough to be called skill.
- Hunt: Showdown 1896 (2018 / 2024 relaunch, $$39.99 (historical)): Long-running PvPvE; community consensus that AI is 'a dinner bell' for PvP. LESSON: In scored PvP, fauna should telegraph, expose and pressure — never silently decide a race.
- Against the Storm (2023, $$29.99): 1.5M+ sales, ~28k Overwhelmingly Positive by 2025; console launch June 2025. LESSON: Depth plus short scored sessions is a shape hardcore players buy; make each round produce a readable story from the systems.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) One verifiable sentence stating the rule the simulation obeys, provable on screen in seconds (Noita's 'every pixel is simulated').
- (both, must-have) The ecosystem framed as an antagonist and a spectacle — creatures hunting you, fighting each other, eating your stuff — rather than a simulation to observe.
- (both, strong) Trailers and clips that show failure and consequence (highs and lows), not a mistake-free sizzle.
- (hardcore, must-have) In scored/competitive play, surprise that is symmetric, readable and never outcome-deciding; a way to opt out of chaos.
- (hardcore, strong) Environmental threats that act as information and pressure on opponents (a 'dinner bell'), not as a third player that kills you mid-duel.
- (casual, must-have) Direction and feedback alongside emergence — tell the player what the systems just did to them.
- (both, strong) Shared, funny chaos in co-op/PvE contexts where failure is a story rather than a loss.
- (both, strong) A public UGC/Workshop surface so community creations are visible; without it a sandbox hub does not retain.
- (both, strong) Systems depth delivered in short, goal-bounded rounds that still produce a readable story.
- (hardcore, nice-to-have) Creator/explainer content (AI breakdown videos, dev diaries, codex) that makes the depth legible to people who were not already into emergence.

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Today the ecosystem is a developer-facing fundamental, not a player-facing hook. Of ~16 modes, only Brood Rush and Wildlife Liberation make fauna the objective; Astro League and Scarab Scramble hold the fauna outside the court until a phase gate, Peel the Cage removed its fauna entirely, and the racing modes treat the food web as background cleanup. If 'a living ecosystem you fight in' goes on the store page, the trailer can only honestly show two modes plus freestyle — decide whether that is the pitch or whether the ecosystem is texture.
- Fauna are client-local (each peer runs its own spawner off local Random; kills need an RPC; 'the round-trip stays correct once fauna network sync lands'). For a 'hardcore' audience that is the Mario Kart World 'RNG lottery' complaint pre-installed: a player who loses a scored race to a swarm their opponent never saw will not call it emergence. Either make fauna deterministic/synced before EA, or keep ecosystem interference out of scored PvP and confine it to co-op/PvE (Wildlife Liberation), Brood Rush, and freestyle.
- Write the Noita sentence and build the trailer's first five seconds around it: 'Your trail is alive — and it is food.' One uninterrupted shot: lay trail → swarm in the controlling colour eats it → creature starves and withers into a crystal → you collect it → an ability visibly transforms. Cause, effect, consequence, reward, in order. Everything else in the trailer is a vessel doing its genre.
- Show the failures. Per the Helldivers 2 lesson, include the Squirrel's danger trail slamming its own pilot, a lead evaporating as fauna graze it, the Wildlife Liberation cage eroding mid-match. A mistake-free sizzle hides the only thing this lens says is worth selling.
- Do not lead with 'artificial life', 'evolution', 'conserved mass' or 'endogenous selection'. That category has ~600-1,000 reviews per title and ~20 concurrent players. Keep that language in the codex, dev diaries and creator kits, where the Dwarf Fortress/Kenshi crowd will find it and make the explainer videos that carried Rain World.
- Make the ecosystem READ on screen: the legible ecosystem moments are creature-vs-creature (worm colony devouring a shark), creature-vs-your-mass (a swarm stripping your trail), and control flips (fauna colour changing when the nucleus flips). Volume-based control and starvation clocks are invisible; recolouring and eating are not. Instrument those moments with the existing toast feed ('your trail was grazed', 'nucleus lost') and count them in PostHog — if players never see the toast, the feature is not selling anything.
- Hardcore players want the environment as information and pressure, not as a judge. Fauna that expose a hidden trail, that telegraph an opponent's route, or that punish a player for over-laying mass are welcome; fauna that randomly end a Skim Race are not. Audit each scored mode for 'can the ecosystem decide the winner without either player seeing why'.
- The lava-lamp/toy hub is ambience, not retention. Garry's Mod retains through Workshop game modes; Roblox is a UGC platform; Teardown's sandbox holds ~2.5k CCU. With no Steamworks SDK at launch (no Workshop) and only a WebGL painting export, do not budget retention on toys — treat them as onboarding and as trailer B-roll, and put the Rain World lesson on the roadmap: a public gallery for paintings/cells so community output is visible outside the community.
- Cosmic Shore's structural strength in this lens is Maelstrom: systems depth in short, goal-bounded rounds is the Against the Storm shape (1.5M sales) and matches 2025's 'not another sweaty shooter' appetite. Lean the 'casual game for hardcore gamers' tagline on that — short rounds, deep systems, chaos you can read — rather than on the ecosystem alone.
- Steam framing: simulation-adjacent tags hit 1,000 reviews at 4.1% vs racing at 2.1%, and indie multiplayer shooters 'quickly die'. Make solo-vs-AI-plus-ecosystem a complete, satisfying experience (AI backfill already exists) and tag toward systems (Sandbox, Procedural Generation, Artificial Intelligence) rather than toward arena shooter/racing, because the EA multiplayer population will be small and the long tail (month 1 is ≤25% of lifetime) is where depth pays.
- Emergence needs direction or it reads as 'lost' (The Watcher, 57% recent). The quest chain, codex and connecting panel exist; extend them so every ecosystem rule the player is expected to exploit (spawn colour = nucleus colour, shielded mass is not food, starvation drops a crystal) is stated once in-game before it is used against them.
- Expect the Rain World variance problem: an autonomous ecosystem will give some first-time players a no-win first match. Gate ecosystem aggression by intensity tier (already the platform's shape) so tier 1 is the 'designed to be fun' enemy and tier 4 is the 'designed to care for itself' one — and say so on the tier card.

### Open questions
- Will fauna be network-synced (deterministic across peers) before Early Access? If not, which scored modes can honestly include ecosystem interference, and should the store page make any ecosystem claim at all?
- Which single mode is the ecosystem showcase for the trailer — Brood Rush or Wildlife Liberation — and is it playable on the free vessel (Squirrel)? Both are currently locked to other hulls (Wildlife Liberation is Sparrow-only).
- Is there any playtest or PostHog evidence that players notice fauna eating their trail or a nucleus flipping? Without that signal the 'living ecosystem' pitch is unverified against real players.
- Can a 3-5 minute Cosmic Shore round produce a shareable emergent story, or does emergence need the 30-minute-plus sessions Dwarf Fortress, Rain World and Kenshi give it? What is the shortest clip that shows cause → effect → consequence?
- Is a Steam Workshop / public gallery surface feasible inside the 12-month EA given no Steamworks SDK at launch, and what is the cheapest visible-UGC substitute (a hosted painting/cell gallery)?
- What is the store-page tag and genre strategy — arena/racing (2.1% hit rate) or sandbox/simulation-adjacent (4.1%)? Who owns the one-sentence hook?
- Should hardcore players get an explicit 'no wildlife' toggle in scored lobbies (the Mario Kart World lesson), or is ecosystem interference tuned symmetric enough that removing it is unnecessary?
- Do the Monster Hunter Wilds style spectacle moments (creature-vs-creature turf wars) actually occur on screen at gameplay camera distance in Cosmic Shore, or only in the debug/overview view?


## LENS: Multigenre and hero-roster design: how "each character is a different genre" lands with players

The market evidence on multigenre pitches is consistent and unflattering. Novel genre mixes were 11.8% of 2022's 416 hit indie games (HowToMarketAGame, Feb 2023); the 2025 Steam tag data (GameDiscoverCo, Aug 2026) shows hybrids winning only when they ride ONE known hook tag (roguelite is #1/#2). Every celebrated hybrid is a single sentence with a single twist: "soccer with race cars", "a poker roguelike, play five minutes and come back in three hours" (Playstack, Dec 2025), "Minecraft meets Left 4 Dead". None of them is nine genres. The games that tried to be a platform of genres are being cut back: Fortnite is shutting Rocket Racing, Ballistic and Festival Battle Stage in 2026 on a "downturn in engagement", and 2026 commentary blames "fragmentation into too many sub-modes" for losing "the Fortnite magic"; Crimson Desert (2026) drew "jack of all trades, master of none" Mixed reviews for mechanics that "all feel shallow". Sea of Thieves and Mario Party Jamboree's side modes drew the same "mile wide, inch deep" note.

On rosters: Marvel Rivals' 33 launch heroes were called "far too overwhelming, especially for newcomers" (ScreenRant, Dec 2024) and reviewers reported 6.5 hours just to sample the cast; it succeeded anyway because of the IP and because every hero is free. The roster decision players actually punish is gating playable classes behind money or grind: Overwatch 2 reversed its battle-pass hero locks in April 2024; Rivals of Aether II (10 at launch, 16 by April 2026, ~30 staff) made every fighter free forever. Onboarding across LoL, Apex, Supervive, Smash Ultimate and OW2 Stadium converges on a curated starter subset of 5-8 (or 17 of 40+ for Stadium) with the restriction explained by the mode. Deep Rock Galactic has held four classes since 2018 ("no plans to add new classes", 2020) because each class multiplies weapons, balance, VO and cosmetics. Rocket League, the best hybrid ever shipped, has NO classes at all: six standardised hitboxes, identical stats. And the hero+sport hybrids from small studios, Knockout City (12M players) and Omega Strikers, both died on retention and on being too small to make "systemic changes in the live game".

For Cosmic Shore this reads bluntly: "each vessel is a different genre" is a design truth, not a pitch. A $15 paid EA with 9 vessels, 16 mostly vessel-locked modes, 1-4 players and 20 wins per vessel unlock is the exact shape the evidence warns about: a roster too wide to be legible, modes too many to hold a matchmaking population, and progression that gates the very thing the game is selling. The winning shape is one flagship vessel-plus-mode as the store-page sentence, a 5-vessel starter fleet with every vessel reachable in a session, and Maelstrom promoted from meta-mode to the roster's teacher.

### Findings
- [verified] Novel genre mixes are rare among hits and mixing genres tends to alienate both audiences rather than merge them.
  - evidence: Of 416 hit indie games from 2022 (1000+ Steam reviews) only 11.8% were novel genre mixes; 88.2% were variations on an existing genre. The editorial argues a mix 'often alienates the fanbase of both genres instead of merging them' and that development 'stretches on too long' because you implement both games' mechanics; it recommends the MAYA principle (20-30% novelty on a known genre).
  - sources: https://howtomarketagame.com/2023/02/22/editorial-maybe-mixing-genres-is-a-bad-idea/
- [verified] Hybrids that win on Steam in 2025 ride ONE recognisable hook tag (roguelite), not 'everything'; racing's rise is driven by niche indie racers.
  - evidence: GameDiscoverCo's 2021-vs-2025 tag analysis of new games selling >50k copies: Roguelite +39 spots to #1, Roguelike +37 to #2, Racing +22 to #9 ('more indie/niche racers'), while JRPG -24, Turn-Based Strategy -19, RTS -17; the decline is attributed to 'more hybrid-genre, roguelite-y games' replacing traditional categories.
  - sources: https://newsletter.gamediscover.co/p/how-have-the-genres-of-hit-pc-games
- [verified] The celebrated hybrid pitches are one sentence with one twist and one promise; Balatro's PR only worked once it was reduced to that.
  - evidence: Playstack's Wout van Halderen (MIGS 2025): press releases failed ('You can't screenshot trust me, it's addictive'); the breakthrough outreach to Slay the Spire / Isaac streamers was 'it's a Poker roguelike, play for five minutes and come back to me in three hours'. Balatro earned $1M in its first hours and passed 5M copies by Jan 2025. Rocket League's pitch was 'soccer with race cars'; Deep Rock Galactic's was 'a mixture of Minecraft and Left 4 Dead'.
  - sources: https://www.thumbsticks.com/poker-pixels-and-persuasion-the-marketing-bets-behind-balatro/ | https://www.gamedeveloper.com/marketing/developing-a-live-game-that-never-truly-left-early-access-with-deep-rock-galactic | https://time.com/3966126/rocket-league/
- [verified] A 33-hero launch roster was read by press as overwhelming for newcomers, with a measurable sampling cost, even in a game that went on to succeed.
  - evidence: ScreenRant (15 Dec 2024): 'the sheer number available on offer from the get-go is far too overwhelming, especially for newcomers to the hero genre'; trying each hero 'could take six and a half hours'; compares to Overwatch's 21 and TF2's 9 at launch. TheGamer (3 Jan 2025): players 'struggle to identify a main before launch'; marquee heroes exhausted so future adds feel less like events. Roster reached 53 by Season 9.5 (2026) with a new hero every six weeks.
  - sources: https://screenrant.com/marvel-rivals-roster-too-many-characters-op-ed/ | https://www.thegamer.com/marvel-rivals-hero-roster-future-oversaturation/ | https://www.gamesradar.com/games/third-person-shooter/marvel-rivals-exec-isnt-afraid-of-roster-bloat-after-netease-promised-new-heroes-every-6-weeks-theres-no-limit-for-the-sky-we-just-throw-everything-at-it/
- [verified] In a big roster the operative problem becomes ROLE balance and what players actually pick, not the count.
  - evidence: Den of Geek (23 Jun 2026): Marvel Rivals' 51 heroes split 27 Duelists / 13 Vanguards / 11 Strategists vs Overwatch's 18/11/13; every new hero since Season 5 (Nov 2025) has been a Duelist, the role players already over-pick; 'the game doesn't need fewer mutants, it simply needs more reasons for players to choose something other than DPS'.
  - sources: https://www.denofgeek.com/games/marvel-rivals-biggest-roster-problem-isnt-mutants-its-role-balance/
- [verified] Gating playable classes behind money or grind is the roster decision the market has repeatedly punished; the current norm is 'all playable characters free, monetise cosmetics'.
  - evidence: Overwatch 2 launched (Oct 2022) with new heroes at Battle Pass tier 55 (~100 hours for free players per complaints) and reversed it in Season 10 (Apr 2024), making all heroes free. Rivals of Aether II (Oct 2024): 'all future post-launch fighters will be added for free'; Fornace: 'We want to run this game for a really long time. So, we want to make money other than the sales of the game itself' via cosmetics. Marvel Rivals' exec cites 'the rosters are free, so you can play anybody you want' as the growth lever.
  - sources: https://www.forbes.com/sites/krisholt/2024/03/19/overwatch-2-will-stop-paywalling-heroes-and-unlock-them-all-for-free/ | https://paletteswap.site/true-colors/rivals-of-aether-2-interview-offbrand-games-new-characters-ten-year-plan/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivals_of_Aether_II
- [verified] Onboarding rosters converge on a curated starter subset of roughly 5-8 easy characters, with the rest unlocked fast, and the subset must be visibly 'a subset' or new players misread it as the whole game.
  - evidence: Supervive starts new players with 2 heroes, unlocks more after the first match (~5 early), free picks after ~5 matches; a Steam thread shows a newcomer concluding the game 'only has two characters' and quitting, with the community explaining 'the devs let new players only play the easiest characters... because the game is already overwhelming'. LoL gives new accounts a static low-skill-cap set until level 11, then a 20-champion (~10%) weekly rotation. Apex starts with 5 legends. Smash Ultimate starts with 8 of 70+, with unlocks 'streamlined and consistent' and framed as a feature.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/1283700/discussions/0/599647812318189302/ | https://wiki.leagueoflegends.com/en-us/Free_champion_rotation | https://forums.ea.com/discussions/apex-legends-feedback-en/unlock-all-legends---please-respawn/8669462 | https://www.ssbwiki.com/Unlockable_character
- [verified] A mode with a deliberately restricted character pool is accepted when the restriction is explained by the mode's own rules and the pool is widened on a cadence.
  - evidence: Overwatch 2 Stadium launched 22 Apr 2025 with 17 of 40+ heroes. Game director Aaron Keller: 'launch in as sane a format as possible before we start bringing in heroes that have really crazy mobility'; designer Dylan Snyder: the mode is 'more brawl focused', making ranged/hitscan heroes hard to balance. Three more heroes were added in Season 17; reception to the mode was 'mostly positive'.
  - sources: https://www.pcgamesn.com/overwatch-2/stadium-hero-pool | https://esportsinsider.com/2025/04/overwatch-2-stadium-launch-april
- [verified] Random-loadout / random-hero modes are welcomed as the roster's teacher and tend to become permanent by demand.
  - evidence: Halo Infinite's Fiesta (random weapons+equipment) was event-only, 'fans loved it so much it's now a permanent fixture'; GameRant argues it lets beginners 'take what they've been given and go nuts' and learn the whole arsenal without competitive pressure. Overwatch's Mystery Heroes is permanent in Unranked and periodically Competitive; Splatoon 3 Salmon Run ships wildcard rotations where one or all four weapons are random.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/halo-infinite-fiesta-playlists-tenrai-event-permanent-modes-weapon-learning/ | https://esports.gg/news/overwatch/how-the-overwatch-2-competitive-mystery-heroes-game-mode-works/ | https://splatoonwiki.org/wiki/Salmon_Run_Next_Wave
- [verified] A small team's most successful class-based co-op game has held FOUR classes for eight years because each class multiplies content cost and the synergy is the product.
  - evidence: Ghost Ship's Robert_GSG (May 2020 AMA): 'New Classes were pretty much only a topic during the early days of the project. As it stands, we have no plans to add new classes'; a fifth class needs exclusive weapons, balance, VO and art across all class interactions. DRG sold 90,000 copies in its first week (2018 EA) and needed 10,000/month to survive; cosmetics were designed as revenue from day one. The 2026 spin-off Rogue Core only adds a fifth class because its class synergy is looser.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/548430/discussions/1/2254560552310809863/ | https://www.gamedeveloper.com/marketing/developing-a-live-game-that-never-truly-left-early-access-with-deep-rock-galactic | https://www.gamebrief.net/blog/drg-rogue-core-complete-guide-hub
- [likely] The most successful genre hybrid ever shipped has no playstyle roster at all; its 'roster' is cosmetic.
  - evidence: Rocket League standardised every car to one of six hitbox presets in July 2017 with identical stats 'so that players who purchase cosmetics such as new car bodies do not have competitive advantages'. The whole game is one skill expressed through one vehicle model; variety is skins.
  - sources: https://www.epicgames.com/help/c-202300000001622/c-202300000001682/rocket-league-car-hitboxes-a202300000018007 | https://dignitas.gg/articles/an-overview-of-hitboxes-in-rocket-league
- [verified] Polished hero+sport hybrids from small studios died on retention and on being too small to change a live game, not on the pitch.
  - evidence: Knockout City (dodgebrawler, May 2021 to 6 Jun 2023) attracted 12M+ players; Velan: 'several aspects of the game in need of major disruption to better attract and retain enough players' and 'since we are a small, indie studio, it's simply impossible for us to make those kinds of systemic changes in the live game while continuing to support it'. Omega Strikers (3v3 'action sport', ex-Riot team, $19M Series A) launched 27 Apr 2023 on seven platforms and announced on 3 Nov 2023 it was winding down content over 'high player turnover and an inability to grow the game'; ~50% layoffs.
  - sources: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/business/knockout-city-is-shutting-down-after-struggling-to-retain-players | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_Strikers | https://techraptor.net/gaming/news/omega-strikers-odyssey-interactive-layoffs
- [verified] Even at Fortnite scale, side modes that cannot hold a population get culled, and mode fragmentation is now blamed for eroding the core.
  - evidence: Epic is shutting Ballistic and Festival Battle Stage (16 Apr 2026) and Rocket Racing (Oct 2026), citing a 'downturn in player engagement', after 1,000 layoffs in March 2026. Beebom (4 Sep 2026): Fortnite averages ~1.3M daily players but 'the fragmentation of the game into too many sub-modes seem to have taken away from the Fortnite magic'; the menu 'can feel overwhelming'.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/fortnite-game-modes-shutting-down-list-rocket-racing/ | https://beebom.com/is-fortnite-dying/
- [likely] Niche side modes only stay playable on top of a very large core population; splitting a small population by mode and skill breaks matchmaking fast.
  - evidence: Rocket League's Rumble/Hoops/Dropshot/Snow Day queue 1-3 minutes vs sub-30 s core, sustained by 55-60M MAU (Mar 2026) and by rotating extra modes in and out of the competitive playlist. Awesomenauts' developer showed that from 1,000 concurrent players, splitting into two playlists and then skill bands leaves too few for fair matches.
  - sources: https://electronmagazine.com/rocket-league-player-count-2026-complete-stats-trends-what-they-mean-for-the-game/ | http://joostdevblog.blogspot.com/2014/11/why-good-matchmaking-requires-enormous.html | https://us.forums.blizzard.com/en/overwatch/t/is-the-community-being-split-into-too-many-playlists/974512
- [verified] Big vehicle/character rosters do not carry sales; the core mode does, and bolt-on modes are read as bolt-ons.
  - evidence: Sonic Racing CrossWorlds (56 characters, vehicle categories incl. Extreme Gear) passed 1M units by Oct 2025 but SEGA said 'initial performance did not meet our expectations' (Feb 2026), targeting 2M by Mar 2026; Steam peak 13,630 (26 Sep 2025), a few hundred concurrent by 2026. Mario Kart World (50 characters, 40 karts) sold 15.39M by 30 Jun 2026 (Metacritic 86) on the race itself while Free Roam was criticised as 'deserted' and 'does not compete with other open-world racing games'. Super Mario Party Jamboree's 110+ minigames were praised but its extra modes 'come off feeling like the side modes they are'.
  - sources: https://www.pushsquare.com/news/2026/02/sonic-racing-crossworlds-initial-sales-didnt-meet-segas-expectations | https://steamdb.info/app/2486820/charts/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Kart_World | https://www.tomsguide.com/gaming/super-mario-party-jamboree-review
- [likely] 'Lots of shallow systems' is a live 2026 review verdict, and 'sandbox with no toys' is the historical form of the same complaint.
  - evidence: Crimson Desert (2026) launched to 'Mixed' Steam reviews with players calling it 'jack of all trades, master of none', 'stuffed with a bunch of different mechanics' that 'all feel shallow and don't really work well together'. Sea of Thieves (2018) was reviewed as 'the ocean is deep, the game is not' and 'a sandbox with no toys'; No Man's Sky (2016) as 'a mile wide and an inch deep'. Nathan Vella (Capy): 'when you're targeting everyone, you're really targeting no-one'.
  - sources: https://www.gamesradar.com/games/rpg/jack-of-all-trades-master-of-none-crimson-desert-launches-to-mixed-steam-reviews-with-players-praising-the-rpgs-visuals-but-calling-its-story-bland-and-controls-ridiculously-bad/ | https://stevivor.com/reviews/sea-of-thieves-review-the-ocean-is-deep-the-game-is-not/ | https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/opinion-make-games-for-yourself---and-nobody-else
- [likely] Players concentrate: most time goes to one main, and a hero pool's legibility rests on the handful that carry the population.
  - evidence: Among 1,062 Grandmaster NA Overwatch players (Season 8), ~10% were strict one-tricks and the average share of time on a player's main was 42.8%. Marvel Rivals publishes pick/win rates each half-season; Cloak & Dagger tops both platforms and Strategists are 'a staple of virtually every match' while Black Widow, Hawkeye and Adam Warlock sit at the bottom. Beginner guides converge on 'pick one easy hero per role and rotate'.
  - sources: https://medium.com/beezy-work/one-tricking-vs-hero-maining-in-high-elo-overwatch-an-empirical-analysis-6f753068068 | https://www.dexerto.com/marvel-rivals/most-popular-marvel-rivals-characters-hero-pick-rates-3025956/ | https://www.gamespot.com/gallery/best-marvel-rivals-heroes-for-new-players/2900-6339/
- [verified] A ~30-person fighting-game studio shipped 10 characters, adds about one per quarter, and deferred casual features to make the core 'the best game we can'.
  - evidence: Rivals of Aether II launched 23 Oct 2024 with 10 fighters (8 returning, 2 new), 16 by 7 Apr 2026; studio peaked ~30 people; Fornace: 'Let's give them the best game we can' rather than launch with extensive casual features (story mode moved post-launch); tutorials shipped progressively (beginner Jan 2025, intermediate Jul, advanced Dec).
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivals_of_Aether_II | https://paletteswap.site/true-colors/rivals-of-aether-2-interview-offbrand-games-new-characters-ten-year-plan/ | https://fornace.medium.com/aether-studios-2025-recap-and-2026-preview-64bb2d17f3bb
- [likely] Try-before-commit is standard for large loadout rosters; unlock is paced by play, never by a single grind wall.
  - evidence: Splatoon 3 has 65 main weapons / 173 kits (v11.2.0); weapons are bought with Sheldon Licenses earned by level and by 'Freshness' from use, and can be tested on the range 'before exchanging a Sheldon License'. Marvel Rivals ships a Tutorial, a Hero Tutorial for 'a few select Heroes' and per-objective tutorials.
  - sources: https://splatoonwiki.org/wiki/List_of_main_weapons_in_Splatoon_3 | https://www.thegamer.com/splatoon-3-all-weapons-explained-how-to-unlock-guide/ | https://marvelrivals.fandom.com/wiki/Practice_Modes
- [likely] Adding modes and classes to chase a broader audience can dilute the one identity a game had.
  - evidence: Splitgate 2 (2025) launched free with factions/classes and a battle royale, drew 'very mixed' reviews for being 'too rough around the edges' and reducing 'the importance of portals, which were the first game's selling point'; 1047 'unlaunched' it back to beta, laid off 45 more staff and shut the original (Aug 2025), promising to 'reintroduce more portal-focused maps' and 'bring back classic game modes'.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/splitgate-2-unlaunched-splitgate-1-shut-down-layoffs/ | https://www.techradar.com/gaming/splitgate-2-is-being-pushed-back-to-beta-as-1047-games-shuts-down-the-original-splitgate-and-undergoes-more-layoffs

### Comparables
- Rocket League (2015, $$19.99 (later F2P, 2020)): Genre-defining hit; 55-60M MAU in 2026 LESSON: One hybrid, one sentence, one playstyle. The 'roster' is cosmetic (six identical-stat hitboxes since 2017). Its extra modes survive only because the core mode holds tens of millions.
- Balatro (2024, $$14.99): $1M in first hours; 5M+ copies by Jan 2025 LESSON: Traditional PR failed; a five-word pitch to the right streamers worked. A hybrid is sold as a known genre plus one twist plus a time promise, never as a list.
- Deep Rock Galactic (2018 (EA), 1.0 2020, $$29.99): 90k first week in EA; long-running live game LESSON: Four classes, forever: each added class multiplies weapons, balance, VO, cosmetics across every interaction. Depth on few, synergistic classes beats breadth.
- Marvel Rivals (2024, $F2P): Massive hit despite roster-overwhelm criticism; 53 heroes by 2026 LESSON: 33 heroes was called overwhelming (6.5 h to sample), and it worked anyway only because every hero is free and the IP pre-teaches the roster. Without Marvel's brand, the overwhelm is the whole story; the real long-term problem is role imbalance (27/13/11).
- Overwatch 2 (2022, $F2P): Hero battle-pass locks reversed Apr 2024; Stadium (Apr 2025) shipped with 17 of 40+ heroes LESSON: Do not gate playable classes behind grind or money. A mode may restrict its pool if the mode's rules explain it ('launch in as sane a format as possible') and the pool grows on a cadence.
- Rivals of Aether II (2024, $$29.99): 10 fighters at launch, 16 by Apr 2026; modest but sustained population; ~30 staff peak LESSON: Ship ~10 polished characters, add one per quarter for free, monetise cosmetics, defer casual side features until the core is right, ship tutorials progressively.
- Knockout City (2021, $$19.99 then F2P): 12M+ players; shut down Jun 2023 LESSON: 'Cars + soccer' luck is not repeatable by pitch alone; a small team could not make 'systemic changes in the live game while continuing to support it'. Keep the live surface small enough to change.
- Omega Strikers (2023, $F2P): Content wound down 8 months after launch; ~50% layoffs LESSON: Hero + sport is not a safe harbour; 'high player turnover and an inability to grow' killed it, and shipping on seven platforms at once made every later change harder.
- Fortnite (as a platform of modes) (2017; mode cuts 2026, $F2P): Rocket Racing, Ballistic, Festival Battle Stage shut in 2026 after 1,000 layoffs LESSON: Even at ~1.3M daily players, sub-modes that cannot hold a population get culled and fragmentation is blamed for eroding the core. Sixteen modes at indie EA scale is untenable as a live surface.
- Sonic Racing: CrossWorlds (2025, $$69.99): 1M units by Oct 2025; SEGA: 'did not meet our expectations'; Steam peak 13,630 LESSON: Roster breadth and vehicle categories are not a purchase driver; the core race and brand are. Guest characters and DLC are a retention lever, not a launch lever.
- Mario Kart World (2025, $$79.99): 15.39M sold by Jun 2026, Metacritic 86 LESSON: The core mode carries everything; Free Roam ('deserted', 'does not compete with other open-world racing games') and route changes drew the criticism and a review bomb. A hub/sandbox layer is a liability unless it is as deep as the core.
- Supervive (2024, $F2P): Live; progressive hero unlock LESSON: A starter subset must be labelled as a subset on screen, with the unlock cadence visible; otherwise the restriction reads as missing content.
- Splitgate 2 (2025, $F2P): 'Unlaunched' back to beta; original game shut Aug 2025; layoffs LESSON: Widening the genre surface to chase everyone diluted the one thing the audience came for; the recovery plan is to cut back to that thing.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) A one-sentence hook naming ONE known genre and ONE twist (never a list of genres).
- (casual, must-have) A curated starter subset of ~5-8 easy, clearly-roled characters, visibly labelled as a subset, with the rest arriving fast.
- (both, must-have) Every playable class reachable without a paywall or a long grind; monetise or gate cosmetics and difficulty instead.
- (both, strong) Try-before-commit: a practice range / hero tutorial per class before any spend of currency or time.
- (both, must-have) Few modes with healthy populations rather than many modes with thin ones; extra modes rotate rather than sit permanently in the menu.
- (hardcore, strong) Depth and distinct synergy on a few classes over breadth; each class's role readable at a glance.
- (both, strong) A restricted-pool or single-class mode is fine when the mode's rules explain the restriction.
- (casual, nice-to-have) A random/rotation mode that teaches the roster without competitive pressure.
- (both, strong) Side modes and sandbox hubs that are as deep as the core, or kept out of the headline.
- (both, nice-to-have) Something to express identity on the character you main (cosmetics) once you have one.

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- 'Each vessel is a different genre' is a design fact, not a pitch. Nine genre mixes in one box is the shape HowToMarketAGame's data (11.8% of hits are novel mixes) and Crimson Desert's 2026 'jack of all trades' reviews warn about. Pick ONE vessel x mode pairing as the store-page sentence in the Balatro/Rocket League form (known genre + one twist + a time promise), and let the fleet be discovered inside the game. The Squirrel (free, default, F-Zero/Redout tube-riding on trails you lay) or the designated beachhead Scarab Scramble (crystal-forged ball through hoops) are the two candidates; the current 'party game for pilots' and proposed 'a casual game for hardcore gamers' both describe an audience, not what you do, and neither phrase has any traction as a tagline (searched; nothing).
- Roster size vs polish: five vessels meet the fleet contract today (Squirrel, Sparrow, Dolphin, Scarab, Urchin's abilities) while Manta/Rhino/Serpent have 0-2 of 4 ability icons and Urchin has no HUD prefab. Rivals of Aether II shipped 10 finished fighters with ~30 staff; DRG holds 4 classes because each one multiplies content. A half-authored vessel on the select screen reads as the whole game being half-done. Launch EA with the 5 finished vessels as the visible roster, name each by its genre word on the card (Racer / Shooter / Brawler / Blaster / Striker), and treat every later vessel as a Rivals-style quarterly free drop, each with its 4x4 map, HUD, AI and at least one mode complete before it is shown.
- Vessel unlock economy is the one roster decision the market punishes: 4,000 crystals at 200 per win is 20 wins per vessel and ~160 wins to see eight vessels, inside a $15 paid game, with no cosmetics to gate instead. Overwatch 2 reversed hero locks; Rivals II made fighters free forever. Either make every shipped vessel free in the paid game and move progression onto intensity tiers, elemental mastery and the quest chain, or cut the price to a few wins with a first-session try-out (the lava-lamp Vessel Changer already lets anyone fly any hull; make that the practice range and say so on the card).
- Mode count is a matchmaking problem before it is a design problem. Sixteen modes, most vessel-locked, at 1-4 players in a paid EA will run at hundreds of concurrent players at best; Fortnite culls sub-modes at 1.3M daily and Rocket League's extras need 55M MAU. AI backfill keeps lobbies from being empty but turns every niche mode into a bot match, which players read as a dead game. Expose 4-6 curated modes at launch, one per shipped vessel plus Maelstrom, and rotate the remaining modes through the weekly challenge and Maelstrom's pool rather than the main menu.
- Vessel-locked modes are legible when the lock is the mode's rule, per OW2 Stadium ('launch in as sane a format as possible'). Astro League IS 'soccer with a sword', Rampage IS 'the Dolphin's blast economy contested', and the CLAUDE.md records structural reasons for each lock. Put that reason on the card in one line ('Rhino only: the ball resolves on the blade') so the restriction reads as identity rather than as missing content; the Supervive thread shows what happens when a restriction is unexplained.
- Maelstrom is the roster's teacher, not a meta-mode. Fiesta and Mystery Heroes became permanent because random loadouts are how newcomers learn a roster without pressure; Maelstrom already forces a random vessel per round via vessel-locked cards. Make it the default first-session flow after the tutorial and the thing the weekly challenge feeds, so a new pilot samples five vessels in one sitting instead of the Marvel Rivals '6.5 hours to try everyone' problem.
- Role legibility is the next problem after count. Rivals' 53 heroes are fine; 27/13/11 role skew is not. Cosmic Shore layers 3 domains x 9 vessels x 4 elements x 16 modes; the vessel vision band tells you WHO a ship is at range but nothing tells you what it does. Each vessel needs one enemy-readable tell (the Dolphin's Echo Sight cone and jaws are the model) and the codex should lead with 'what this vessel does to you'.
- The lava-lamp hub and toys are the 'Free Roam' risk: Mario Kart World's open world was 'deserted', Sea of Thieves was 'a sandbox with no toys', Mario Party's side modes 'come off feeling like the side modes they are'. Keep the hub as the practice range and vessel/domain changer (that is onboarding), and keep the 16-painting gallery, Wanderway and lifeform bench out of the trailer and store page until they have their own retention evidence.
- Hybrid-sport hero games from small teams (Knockout City, Omega Strikers) died on retention and on being too small to change a live game. An 8-person team with 16 modes and 9 vessels cannot iterate any one surface fast enough; the DRG/Rivals II lesson is to keep the live surface small enough that one playtest's finding can be shipped as a change to the whole game.
- Do not promise a cadence you cannot hold: NetEase ships a hero every six weeks, Aether one fighter per quarter with ~30 people. For eight people, one finished vessel per quarter with its mode is the honest roadmap line; 'more vessels/modes/episodes' with no number will be read against Rivals' cadence.

### Open questions
- What do PostHog/TestFlight sessions show about vessel switching: what share of testers ever fly a second vessel, and how long until the first unlock at 200 crystals per win?
- Which single vessel x mode pairing has the best session-2 return in existing telemetry? That is the store-page sentence; the answer should come from data, not preference.
- What concurrent player count does the EA plan assume, and what does matchmaking look like per vessel-locked mode at that number once split by intensity tier? (The Awesomenauts arithmetic needs redoing with Cosmic Shore's numbers.)
- If all shipped vessels become free in a $15 game, what carries progression? Intensity tiers and the quest chain exist; is that enough, or does a cosmetic layer (the one thing DRG and Rivals II both lean on) need to come back into the EA scope?
- Does the 'each vessel is a genre' claim survive external play: do reviewers actually report F-Zero in the Squirrel and Rocket League in Scarab Scramble, or do they report 'a space game with modes'? A 10-person outside playtest with a one-line recall test would settle this before EA.
- How will the four unfinished vessels (Manta, Rhino, Serpent, Grizzly) be presented if they remain in the build: hidden, 'coming' cards, or playable-but-partial? Each choice has a different legibility cost.
- Is Maelstrom acceptable as the default onboarding path given it draws from a seven-mode pool that includes vessel-locked modes the player has not unlocked the vessel for?
- What is the enemy-readable 'tell' for each vessel at arena range beyond domain colour, and is it authored or emergent today?


## LENS: Real 2026 competitor set for a 3D vessel party game: Steam review-complaint mining + one-date CCU snapshot (steamcharts.com, fetched 2026-09-05)

Twelve comps plus Omega Strikers were snapshotted from one source on one date (steamcharts.com, 2026-09-05, "last 30 days" avg/peak) and their top-rated negative reviews were read, not summarised. The single most important number: every multiplayer comp, regardless of review score, settled at 2-5% of its launch-peak CCU within 6-12 months. Rematch 91,374 -> 3,443 avg (-96%, 14 months, 68% Mixed); Super Battle Golf 20,395 -> 1,017 avg (-95%, 6.5 months, 93% Very Positive); Jump Space 21,933 -> 415 avg (-98%, 11 months, 87%); Void Crew 5,668 -> 98 avg. The three small-team anti-grav racers (Redout 2, Distance, BallisticNG) never exceeded 452 peak CCU in their lives and sit at 9-16 avg today with dead multiplayer; the only living racing population is Trackmania (1,393 avg) and it is free-to-play. The Sparrow comp set has no living PvP audience at all: Squadrons is at 45 avg (65% Mixed, "RIP servers"), Everspace 2 and House of the Dying Sun are single-player, and the two co-op PvE crews (Jump Space, Void Crew) are the only space games that moved units in 2025-26. GameDiscoverCo's data closes the EA question: only 20% (2025, n=225) / 21% (2026, n=91) of EA graduates earned more at 1.0 than at EA launch; median 1.0 revenue was ~40% of EA; "your EA launch is your launch". Complaint themes, ranked by how often they appear across the closest comps: (1) business model on a paid game (Rematch's paid cosmetics/battlepass is its #1 theme); (2) netcode/desync and disconnects with no reconnect (Rematch, Jump Space "lost connection in more than half of them"); (3) dead queues ("30 minutes" ranked 5v5, Feb 2026); (4) locked/un-upgraded content and progression that "doesn't respect your time" (Redout 2, Jump Space, Trackmania paywall); (5) host power and client-side trust ("if you play a bit too well, you just get kicked"; "built mostly on the client side allowing hackers"); (6) onboarding gated on memorisation (Redout 2, BallisticNG). Positive reviews credit exactly two things: playing with friends ("buy copies for three of your friends") and responsive, no-class, equal-footing play (Jump Space, Rematch). Against that, Cosmic Shore already ships four of the six complaint classes: per-mode vessel locks plus 4,000-crystal unlocks at 200/win (=20 wins per hull) in a genre whose winners have no classes; no host migration; a 45 s connecting hold; no rebinding UI and no Steam Input. No multiplayer arena game was found that ships a living NPC ecosystem as a competitive element; the nearest single-player precedent (Ecosystem, 76%) is criticised precisely because its emergence reads as bugs ("sideways-sliding fish with flailing fins"). The ecosystem is therefore an unvalidated bet whose risk is legibility, not novelty.

BallisticNG — difficulty spike 'a third of the way through the campaign' 5, narrow tracks 3, 'sloppy and overcomplicated' controls 3. Trackmania's negatives are not gameplay at all: Ubisoft Connect/account 5, Club subscription paywall 4.
  - evidence: Negative review pages read for all three.

- [verified] There is no living PvP space-dogfight audience on Steam in 2026. Star Wars Squadrons (65% Mixed) negatives: EA login/2FA 6, dead multiplayer 4 ('playerbase is so small that you can never get into multiplayer matches', 'RIP servers'), abandoned 4, 'two modes of multiplayer on, like, five maps each'. Everspace 2 and House of the Dying Sun are single-player (HotDS: 'Only an hour of gameplay?'). Everspace 2's complaints are RPG-loop complaints (level scaling 6, tedium 5, story 4, loot 4, $50 price 3). The only space games moving units in 2025-26 are co-op PvE crews (Jump Space, Void Crew).
  - evidence: Negative review pages read; steamcharts rows.

- [verified] Void Crew (1.0 Nov 2024, $24.99) negatives: repetitive missions 5, bugs 5 ('about half my runs are lost to some bug'), no ship persistence 4, content 4 ('After 4-5 runs you've seen everything'), difficulty 3, loot-box cosmetics 2. Rocket League's negatives are almost entirely a template about Epic (Steam/Linux delisting 9, blueprints 8, trading removal 7, no new content 7, servers 6, smurfs 6) — i.e. the market punishes platform and monetisation moves harder than gameplay.

- [verified] No comp carries a systemic/ecosystem element, and no multiplayer ARENA game was found that ships a living, grazing NPC ecosystem as a competitive layer. The nearest precedents are survival games where players ARE the fauna (The Isle: 61,341 reviews, 77%; Path of Titans; The Undergrowth, coming-soon EA), a co-op civilisation sim (Eco: 6,659 reviews, 78%, 214 CCU Jun 2026, still EA since 2018), and a single-player evolution sim (Ecosystem: 598 reviews, 76%) whose top negatives are that emergence reads as malfunction — 'sideways-sliding fish with flailing fins', 'like AI production with 7 fingers', 'flop around in ways that don't make sense' — and that the simulation is opaque (needing a 'quasi cheat menu').

- [likely] Queue death is a feedback loop with a known floor: '1,000 concurrent players... is a hit for indie' but a fraction of what good matchmaking needs; Awesomenauts sustained matches at ~20 CCU only by guaranteeing a match within 7 minutes via bot-filled flights. Omega Strikers (ex-Riot leads, D.I.C.E. nominee) hit 20-minute EU queues at 421 players online globally before winding down.
  - evidence: Joost van Dongen's dev blog (2014, dated) and Steam forum reports; Wikipedia for Omega Strikers timeline.
  - sources: http://joostdevblog.blogspot.com/2014/11/why-good-matchmaking-requires-enormous.html | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omega_Strikers | https://steamcharts.com/app/1869590
- [likely] Missing key rebinding is a recurring Steam complaint that produces refunds (Going Under: 'not allowing key rebinding is inexcusable', refunds cited; Windbound and Dying Light 2 accessibility complaints; Redout 2's 'Boosting Tutorial is bugged' with custom inputs). Host drop without migration is a named co-op complaint class (Jump Space: host 'dropped out after spending an hour... all that time got lost'; Unrailed dev: 'infrastructure isn't able to handle transferring of hosts').
  - evidence: Search-snippet evidence across several titles plus Jump Space threads read.
