- [verified] Price positioning: $15 sits between SBG ($12.99, no IAP, 93%) and Jump Space ($19.99 EA, disclosed 'gradually raise the price as we release new content'); Rematch's $29.99 plus paid cosmetics/battlepass is the single most-tallied negative theme in the closest sport comp, and SBG drew a negative review for a post-launch price rise. Steam reviews overall drifted from 90% to 84% positive over five years (Jan 2025), so a Mixed badge is easier to earn than it was.
  - evidence: Store pages read; gamedevreports article read.
  - sources: https://store.steampowered.com/app/4069520/Super_Battle_Golf/ | https://store.steampowered.com/app/1757300/Jump_Space/ | https://store.steampowered.com/app/2138720/REMATCH/ | https://gamedevreports.substack.com/p/steam-reviews-have-become-more-negative

### Comparables
- Rematch (Sloclap) (2025 (no EA), $$29.99 + paid cosmetics/battlepass): 1M copies in 5 days; peak 91,374 -> 3,443 avg (Sep 2026); 68% Mixed on ~47.7k reviews LESSON: Netcode that registers contacts and a queue that fills are non-negotiable; a paid game with paid cosmetics becomes the #1 review theme the moment the population drops; players ask for private rooms with bots when queues die
- Super Battle Golf (Brimstone / Oro) (2026-02-19 (no EA), $$12.99, no IAP): 400k units week one; peak 20,395 -> 1,017 avg in 6.5 months; 93% on 11.4k reviews LESSON: Even a 93% game loses 95% of CCU in half a year; 'with friends' is the whole positive story; host-only kick and client-side authority become the top complaints; players want Workshop for longevity; a free weekend buys a one-month spike
- Rocket League (Psyonix/Epic) (2015; F2P 2020; unlisted on Steam, $Free (was $19.99)): 16,317 avg / 28,399 peak on Steam; 227k reviews, 86% overall, 71% recent LESSON: The market punishes platform/monetisation moves (delisting, trading removal) far harder than gameplay; ten years of live-ops is the bar its audience compares against
- Trackmania (Ubisoft Nadeo) (2020; Steam 2023, $F2P + Club subscription (~$15/yr)): 1,393 avg / 3,353 peak; 21,225 reviews, 78% LESSON: Negatives are entirely account/launcher/paywall, not driving — a paid track racer competing with a free one must not add friction of its own
- Redout 2 (34BigThings) (2022-06 (no EA), $$19.99): Lifetime peak 286; 14 avg today; 1,161 reviews, 82% LESSON: Steep on-ramp, memorisation-gated skill and 'forced into this un-upgraded jalopy' locked content killed both the campaign retention (<20% reached Class A) and the multiplayer (mismatched car tiers online)
- Distance (Refract) (EA 2014-12 -> 1.0 2018-09, $n/a (not verified)): Lifetime peak 452; 16 avg; 7,583 reviews, 92% LESSON: 45 months of EA produced a loved game whose multiplayer never had a population; it survives on a level editor and 'thousands of courses'
- BallisticNG (Neognosis) (EA 2016 -> 1.0 2018, $n/a (not verified)): Lifetime peak 109; 9 avg; 1,975 reviews, 95% LESSON: 95% positive and still ~10 CCU: quality does not create a racing multiplayer audience; difficulty spike a third in is the top complaint
- Star Wars Squadrons (EA Motive) (2020-10, $$39.99 at launch): Peak 35,721 -> 45 avg; 27,931 reviews, 65% Mixed LESSON: Even a licensed AAA dogfighter with 35k launch CCU had 'RIP servers' within two years; two modes on five maps was called out as minimal content
- Everspace 2 (Rockfish) (EA 2021-01 -> 1.0 2023-04, $$39.99 EA -> $49.99): EA peak 2,809 -> 1.0 peak 9,735 (3.5x); 300k units by 1.0; 198 avg today; 7,252 reviews, 87% LESSON: The uplift came from a 25-person team, 27 months of updates, an always-on demo and Game Pass — and it is single-player; no multiplayer population was required or built
- House of the Dying Sun (Marauder Interactive) (EA 2015 -> 1.0 2016-11, $~$20): Lifetime peak 197; ~1-6 CCU; 2,028 reviews, 90% LESSON: 'Tight dogfighting, very limited content' — an hour of campaign is the whole game; no multiplayer, no long tail
- Jump Space (Keepsake) (EA 2025-09-19 (1.0 planned 2027), $$19.99 EA, rising): 250k players first weekend; peak 21,933 -> 415 avg; 7,877 reviews, 87% LESSON: 'No classes' and responsive guns are what positives credit; disconnects with no reconnect, a wait screen, and grind that 'doesn't respect your time' are what negatives cite; the store-page 'five months of EA' became 2027
- Void Crew (Hutlihut) (EA 2023-09 -> 1.0 2024-11 -> console 2025-09, $$24.99): EA peak 2,107 -> 1.0 4,363 -> 5,668 -> 98 avg; 8,387 reviews, 86%, 70% recent LESSON: Each milestone roughly doubled the peak, then the population fell to double digits; 'after 4-5 runs you've seen everything' and 'half my runs are lost to some bug' dominate
- Omega Strikers (Odyssey Interactive) (Beta 2022-09 -> 1.0 2023-04 -> wound down 2023-11, $Free): Peak 19,236 -> 147 avg; development ended citing 'high player turnover and an inability to grow the game' LESSON: Prestige team + good reviews + F2P still hit 20-minute queues at ~420 global players; small arena sports die of turnover, not quality

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) Play with friends as the first-class path (invite, private lobby, drop-in) — it is the only thing positive reviews consistently credit
- (hardcore, must-have) Netcode that registers contacts and a match that survives a disconnect (reconnect or host migration)
- (both, must-have) A match to start within a minute at any population — bots/private rooms when queues are thin
- (casual, strong) No classes / equal footing for a newcomer joining friends; no content locks in the way of the party
- (both, strong) Progression that respects time; no paid cosmetics/battlepass on a paid game; price policy disclosed
- (casual, strong) Onboarding that does not gate skill on memorisation; controller-first with an arcade control option; key rebinding
- (hardcore, strong) Server authority / anti-cheat so public lobbies are not ruined by client-side exploits or host abuse
- (both, nice-to-have) Enough variety to survive the first two nights, then user-generated content (Workshop/editor) for the long tail

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Plan for 2-5% of launch peak as the standing population and design for it: every multiplayer comp — including a 93%-positive one — settled there within 6-12 months. At a realistic small-team EA peak of 2,000-5,000 CCU that means 50-250 concurrent players spread across ~16 modes and three regions. AI backfill to 12 is the correct architectural answer to that (Rematch's community is begging for exactly it), but it only works if the bots are good enough to be the default opponent — Jump Space's 'braindead AI' and 'buddy AI... constantly spamming shields' show what a bad bot costs in reviews. Make bot quality a launch gate, not a fallback.
- The per-mode vessel lock plus 4,000-crystal hulls at 200 per win (20 wins per vessel, 0 for third place) reproduces the single most-hated pattern in the racing comps ('forced into this un-upgraded jalopy', mismatched tiers online) inside a genre whose 2025-26 winners (Rematch, Super Battle Golf, Jump Space) are explicitly class-less and lock-free. Confirm whether a mode's forced hull is playable without owning it; if not, ~13 modes are gated behind ~20 wins each and the 'casual game for hardcore gamers' pitch collapses at the first party session. Fix options in order of cost: forced hull is free inside its mode; unlock-by-playing-the-mode; cut the price to a few sessions' worth.
- No host migration is the Jump Space failure mode ('host dropped out after an hour, all that time got lost') and it was cut from EA. Minimum viable answer before EA: the match result is persisted server-side at each turn end so a host quit costs at most one turn, plus a visible 'host left' toast and an auto-return to the party lobby. Netcode for GameObjects 2.x does not offer migration; say so plainly on the store page's EA Q&A rather than let reviews discover it.
- The 45 s connecting panel is the same complaint class as Jump Space's 'Waiting for Crew to Jump' ('one of the game's biggest problems'). Instrument the actual distribution in PostHog before EA; the live preview and roster mitigate it, but a median above ~10 s on a 3-minute party mode will be reviewed as lag, and a 45 s cap that fires often will be reviewed as a stall.
- No rebinding UI and no Steam Input (no Steamworks at launch) doubles the exposure on the one Steam theme that triggers refunds. A rebind screen is cheaper than the reviews; Redout 2 also shows the second-order cost (a tutorial that breaks under custom inputs), so rebinding must be in the QA backlog with the FTUE.
- Client-local fauna and client-reported kills/prism destruction (ReportFaunaKill_ServerRpc, ReportEnvironmentPrismDestroyed_ServerRpc) are the SBG 'built mostly on the client side' trust surface, and with no Steamworks there is also no VAC. At EA scale this is acceptable, but the ecology-scored modes (Wildlife Liberation, Brood Rush) are the ones a cheater will target first; document the authority model and keep those modes out of the weekly-challenge leaderboard pool until fauna sync lands.
- The beachhead ranking the CCU evidence supports is Scarab Scramble first (SBG/Rematch have a living audience of 1k-16k avg), Skim Race second only as a single-player/time-trial product (every small-team AG racer is at 9-16 CCU; the racing multiplayer audience lives at free Trackmania), and the Sparrow PvP modes last (Squadrons at 45 avg is the only PvP dogfight comp and it is dead). If the Sparrow leads anything, it should be the co-op PvE shape (Wildlife Liberation as a hunt, Salvo as co-op demolition) where Jump Space and Void Crew actually sold.
- Pricing is defensible: $15 with no IAP sits between SBG ($12.99) and Jump Space ($19.99) and avoids Rematch's #1 negative theme. Disclose the EA price policy on the store page up front (Jump Space does; SBG's silent rise drew a negative), and treat the EA launch as the launch — GameDiscoverCo's 20-21% 1.0-beats-EA rate says there is no second chance at 1.0 for a multiplayer game.
- The ecosystem is an unvalidated bet, not a moat: no arena or party game has shipped one, and the closest precedent (Ecosystem, 76%) is reviewed as bugs because its emergence is illegible. The moat only exists if a player in a 3-minute round can say what the fauna did and why it mattered. Ship the codex and the game-toast narration of ecosystem events as launch features, and add one playtest metric — 'can the player name the fauna's effect on the match?' — before spending more on simulation depth.
- The 4-human party ceiling is smaller than every party-sport comp (SBG 8, Rematch 5v5, Rocket League 3v3 + parties) and equal to the co-op comps (Jump Space 4, Void Crew 6). With AI backfill the match size is 12, so the pitch should sell 'up to 12 pilots' and the launch panel should make the bots visible; a 4-seat party lobby reads as small next to SBG on a store page.
- Longevity in this set comes from UGC (SBG's top request, Distance's and Trackmania's whole tail). The painting gallery's WebGL share export is adjacent; a cell/track editor or shareable Skim Race seeds is the cheapest long-tail lever the codebase already half-owns (deterministic track seeds exist).

### Open questions
- Is a mode's forced vessel playable without owning it, or are ~13 vessel-locked modes gated behind 4,000 crystals each? This single fact changes the on-ramp verdict.
- What launch-peak CCU is realistic? No wishlist or marketing beat data was in scope; at the observed 2-5% retention, a 5,000 peak yields ~150 standing players across 16 modes.
- What is the measured median of the connecting panel in real 4-player sessions over Relay, and how often does the 45 s cap fire?
- Can a host quit be reduced to a one-turn loss with the current server-authoritative scoring, given NGO 2.x has no host migration?
- Will Steam players read fauna behaviour as strategy inside a 3-minute round, or as noise? No comp evidence exists either way; this needs a playtest metric.
- Does shipping without Steamworks (no overlay invites, no Steam Input, no VAC) add friction the comps' audiences treat as baseline, the way Sloclap found crossplay was 'a basic standard'?
- Are the SteamPulse/Steambase all-language review counts (Rematch 47.7k, SBG 11.4k) or the lower store-page figures (17k, 7.2k) the right denominator for sales estimates?


## LENS: Three-way team competition (Jade/Ruby/Gold) vs the 1v1 / 2v2 players expect

Three-sided team play at small headcounts is rare, and the shipped precedents that matter all needed repair. Splatoon 3's Tricolor is 4v2v2 (not 1v2v1): it forced the halftime leader to defend against both other teams, 46% of a 1,522-vote Nintendo Life poll (Aug 2022) said "defending sucks", Nintendo replaced forced roles with random assignment plus a 1.5x bonus (Dec 2022), and in March 2025 it was still re-cutting five stages for "significant statistical leaning in the win ratio" between attackers and defenders. The Finals made 3v3v3 its casual default and 4-team Cashout its ranked identity; the permanent complaint is being "sandwiched between two groups of enemies through no fault of your own", Embark added a two-team mode almost every season as a release valve, and the game sits near 5% of its launch population. Fall Guys' three-team rounds were disliked enough that Mediatonic (Aug 2020) refused to run them unless every team was equal-sized. Halo solves N-team readability by painting every enemy one colour, which Cosmic Shore cannot do because the world's mass IS the domain's colour. Nintendo's 3/4-team racing shipped online in Jan 2026 as a 24-player, CPU-filled format, not a 4-player one. Theory agrees: three-way conflict yields leader-bashing, kingmaking and "survival of the weakest".

What Cosmic Shore's code actually does: the lobby defaults to three domains above two players; every human starts on Jade and only moves by tapping a tile; AI backfill fills the emptiest domains; nothing checks that humans span two domains or that sides are equal. So a party that touches nothing plays "all of us vs two AI teams" — a co-op-vs-the-world shape the game never names — while a party that taps into 2/1/1 launches with the exact unequal shape Fall Guys banned. The comeback buff sizes both trailing domains against the leader (Splatoon's rejected shape), ties resolve by enum order to Jade (the default domain), and the end banner calls second place DEFEAT while Maelstrom pays it a point and the economy pays it 50 crystals. The palette's names lie: Jade is teal-over-blue, Ruby is magenta-over-purple, Gold is orange-over-pure-red, neutral Blue is blue — three of four hues are cool and nothing but colour identifies a domain in-world.

Recommendation: keep three domains as a platform capability, but derive sides from headcount so they are always equal (2 -> 1v1, 3 -> 1v1v1, 4 -> 2v2), never auto-launch 2/1/1, name the vs-AI shape as the party's front door and tag Steam honestly, show placement rather than VICTORY/DEFEAT in three-way rounds, size comeback to the next place rather than the leader, and fix names-vs-hues plus a colour-lock/glyph channel before EA. Astro League and Brood Rush are already pinned to two; Maelstrom loses its 2/1/0 texture at DC 2 and needs its own default.

### Findings
- [verified] Splatoon 3 Tricolor is 4v2v2 (not the brief's 1v2v1) and its original design institutionalised gang-up-on-the-leader: the team leading at halftime was forced to DEFEND against both other teams. Players hated it: a Nintendo Life poll of 1,522 votes returned 46% 'Defending sucks! I hated it', 35% 'attacking is best', 12% enjoyed defending, 6% 'too easy'. Quoted defender: '10-15 tricolor matches and only won 2'.
  - evidence: Poll and reader comments in the Nintendo Life article (read in full); Gamepur and GameLuster corroborate the demo-weekend complaints and Nintendo's promise to fix it.
  - sources: https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2022/08/poll-what-did-you-think-of-tricolor-turf-war-in-splatoon-3 | https://www.gamepur.com/news/splatoon-3-demo-players-found-tricolor-turf-war-battles-to-be-unbalanced | https://gameluster.com/nintendo-addresses-splatoon-3-tricolor-turf-wars-complaints/
- [verified] Nintendo's fix (v2.0.0, Chill Season) did not change the 4-2-2 team sizes: it randomised attacker/defender assignment regardless of standings and paid a 1.5x clout bonus when the leading team happens to draw defence. i.e. the remedy for structural leader-punishment was random roles plus a bonus, not a different number of sides.
  - evidence: GameRant's Chill Season article (read in full) lists the three original problems — leader forced to defend, defender double objective, attacker Sprinkler of Doom advantage — and the exact changes; Inkipedia snippets confirm the 1.5x rule.
  - sources: https://gamerant.com/splatoon-3-chill-season-splatfest-tricolor-turf-war-changes-game-balance/ | https://splatoonwiki.org/wiki/Tricolor_Battle
- [verified] Two and a half years in, Nintendo was STILL rebalancing the three-way mode: v9.3.0 (12-14 Mar 2025) re-cut five Tricolor stages 'that have significant statistical leaning in the win ratio for the attacking teams or defending team' (adding attacker cover, non-inkable terrain near attacker spawns, bigger Sprinkler zones) and promised further changes 'based on long-term analysis of battle data'. Team sizes never changed.
  - evidence: Nintendo's own update-history page and Nintendo Life's patch-notes article, both read in full.
  - sources: https://en-americas-support.nintendo.com/app/answers/detail/a_id/61257/ | https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2025/03/splatoon-3-version-9-3-0-out-now-here-are-the-full-patch-notes
- [verified] The Finals is the one mainstream game whose casual default (Quick Cash, 3v3v3) and ranked identity (4-team Cashout) are multi-team — and it shows both faces of the format. The standing complaint from launch week is being 'sandwiched between two groups of enemies through no fault of your own' (thread 'Ranked 3v3v3 ruins this game', 10 Dec 2023) and 'third partying is ruining the game' (Jan 2025); fans answer 'The total chaos makes it so damn fun'. Embark shipped a two-team release valve almost every season: Power Shift 5v5 (S2, Mar 2024), Terminal Attack 5v5 (May 2024), TDM 5v5 (S6), Head-2-Head 3v3 (S8, Sept 2025), Point Break 8v8 (S9). When it made 5v5 Terminal Attack the ranked mode (S3, June 2024) the community forced 4-team Cashout back by 26 Sept 2024.
  - evidence: Two Steam threads read in full; Wikipedia mode history read in full; videogamer/sportskeeda on the S3 ranked reversal.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/app/2073850/discussions/0/4031348273658377594/ | https://steamcommunity.com/app/2073850/discussions/0/737034982498921453/ | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Finals | https://www.videogamer.com/news/the-finals-why-season-3-removes-cashout-from-ranked/ | https://www.thefinals.wiki/wiki/Gamemodes
- [likely] The Finals' population has fallen to roughly 5% of launch: all-time Steam peak 242,044 (Dec 2023) vs ~12,600 concurrent / 15,031 24h-peak in Sept 2026, with 30-day averages of 7-18k over the past year. Multi-team formats need population to fill three or four sides every match; at these numbers queue health becomes the design constraint.
  - evidence: SteamDB/steamcount/tracker aggregates in search snippets; not read in full.
  - sources: https://www.steamdb.com/en/tools/steam-charts/2073850-the-finals | https://steamcount.org/game/2073850 | https://www.g2a.com/news/latest/the-finals-player-count-hits-10-month-high-as-season-11-wins-over-fans/
- [verified] Splitgate 2 shipped a three-faction 24-player Onslaught mode (2v2v2v2 / 3v3v3 / 8v8v8) on 6 June 2025 — the mode 1047 called its favourite thing the team had made — then announced layoffs and 'back to beta' on 22 July 2025, disabled Onslaught matchmaking on 18 Sept 2025, and relaunched on 17 Dec 2025 as Arena Reloaded with the factions removed; post-relaunch Steam peak was 2,297 (<10% of launch). The multi-team mode was the first thing cut when population fell.
  - evidence: Wikipedia timeline and the Arena Reloaded wiki mode page read in full; PlayStation Blog alpha post for the 8v8v8 framing.
  - sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Splitgate_2 | https://splitgatearenareloaded.wiki.gg/wiki/Game_Modes | https://blog.playstation.com/2025/02/12/splitgate-2-alpha-out-on-ps5-feb-27-features-8v8v8-multi-team-portal-warfare/
- [verified] Fall Guys is the party-game reference for three-team rounds: five of seven team games (Egg Scramble, Hoarders, Rock 'n' Roll, Hoopsie Daisy, Team Tail Tag) are three-team, the LOWEST team is eliminated, and stealing is allowed — so the published strategy is to bully the weakest team, not the leader. Lowest-out formats invert bash-the-leader into pile-on-the-loser, which is the pattern Goomba Stomp describes as 'one team with an initial disadvantage just gets piled on until the round is over'.
  - evidence: Dexerto's per-round guide read in full; Goomba Stomp critique read in full.
  - sources: https://www.dexerto.com/fall-guys/fall-guys-team-mini-game-guide-egg-scramble-hoopsie-daisy-1408056/ | https://goombastomp.com/fall-guys-team-games/
- [verified] Mediatonic's answer to the hatred of team rounds (Aug 2020) was to refuse UNEQUAL sides: the round selector only picks Team Tail Tag / Egg Scramble / Fall Ball when equal-sized teams can be formed, and team rounds were made rarer. Quoted player: 'The team games are terrible and make me not want to play it.' A market-leading party game explicitly treats a 2/1/1-style split as unshippable.
  - evidence: Push Square article read in full.
  - sources: https://www.pushsquare.com/news/2020/08/fall_guys_patch_may_reduce_how_often_team_games_are_selected
- [verified] Halo's Multi-Team (2v2v2v2 in Infinite's June 2024 Combat Workshop) solves N-team readability by collapsing all enemies to ONE colour: earlier Halos gave each squad its own colour; Infinite shows only teammates distinctly and asked players how 'friend vs ALL foes' detection reads. Historically the format was niche and unstable — Reach launched it as 6 teams of 2, changed to 4x3 on 8 Feb 2011; Halo 5 cycled it out of playlists.
  - evidence: Halo Waypoint post and Halopedia read in full.
  - sources: https://www.halowaypoint.com/news/combat-workshop-multi-team | https://www.halopedia.org/Multi-Team | https://www.purexbox.com/news/2024/06/halo-infinite-finally-adds-multi-team-game-modes-into-online-matchmaking
- [verified] Nintendo's own racing games treat two teams as the baseline: Mario Kart 8 Deluxe team races are two teams only; Mario Kart World (2025) allows 2/3/4 teams offline only, and online three/four-team racing arrived on 22 Jan 2026 (v1.5.0) as a 24-player Knockout Tour format (12v12 / 8v8v8 / 6v6v6v6) with CPU drivers filling empty seats and the team winning as a unit. Three-plus sides shipped at 24 players, not at 4.
  - evidence: Kotaku article read in full; Game8 and esports rulebooks via snippets for MK8D/MKW offline.
  - sources: https://kotaku.com/mario-kart-worlds-update-knockout-tour-team-battles-2000662201 | https://game8.co/games/Mario-Kart-World/archives/527948 | https://help.playvs.com/en/articles/5872507-mario-kart-8-deluxe-rulebook
- [verified] Design theory names the three failure modes of three-sided conflict — leader bashing, kingmaking ('the winner is determined not by the best player but arbitrarily by a non-winning player'), and turtling — and the mitigations: reward second place, make it hard to target a specific opponent, forecast sudden victory, hidden victory info, or lean in openly (Root). In game theory the truel result is 'survival of the weakest': the strongest shooter has the lowest survival because the others rationally target it.
  - evidence: Skeleton Code Machine essay read in full; truel via Wikipedia/Scientific Reports snippets.
  - sources: https://www.skeletoncodemachine.com/p/three-player-problem | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truel | https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-019-45253-5
- [likely] The modal party is two people and the expected shapes at four are 2v2 or free-for-all — not 2/1/1. Fortnite BR mode split: Solos 36.2%, Duos 35.4%, Squads 21.6%, Trios 6.8%; Rocket League: 3v3 most populated ranked playlist, 2v2 second; forum consensus that a three-player free-for-all 'breaks down into a 2-on-1', and party-game buyers complain about 2v1 at three players (Genital Jousting).
  - evidence: fortnite.gg snapshot and Rocket League stats via snippets (fortnite.gg fetch was blocked); Heroscapers and Steam threads via snippets.
  - sources: https://fortnite.gg/player-count | https://rocket-league.com/stats | https://www.heroscapers.com/threads/three-player-free-for-alls-how-to-structure-them.33851/ | https://steamcommunity.com/app/469820/discussions/0/154641879455076104
- [verified] Steam buyers police the word 'co-op': a May 2025 thread complains that team-vs-team games (Marvel Rivals, Overwatch 2, Among Us, Dead by Daylight, War Thunder) wear the Co-op tag and pollute searches for games 'where you are not against any other person'; the definition is contested but the PvE reading is loud. A domain-sum game marketed as co-op will be judged by that reading.
  - evidence: Steam discussion read in full.
  - sources: https://steamcommunity.com/discussions/forum/0/601905389491012928
- [likely] Players object to UNLABELLED bots, not to bots: Marvel Rivals' 2025 quick-play system (after 2-3 losses, 4 humans + 2 bot teammates vs 6 bots, all at Account Level 1, never acknowledged by NetEase) drew sustained backlash even though the game has a separate, labelled vs-AI mode.
  - evidence: Multiple outlets (Dexerto, TheGamer, ResetEra, Steam) agree in snippets; not read in full.
  - sources: https://www.dexerto.com/gaming/marvel-rivals-player-proves-devs-snuck-bots-into-quickplay-matches-3016959/ | https://www.thegamer.com/marvel-rivals-quick-play-matchmaking-forced-into-bot-lobbies/ | https://steamcommunity.com/app/2767030/discussions/0/600766744404954718/
- [verified] Accessibility baseline: 'Ensure no essential information is conveyed by a fixed colour alone' — red/green deficiency affects ~8-10% of males; alternatives are labels, symbols, patterns, shapes and contrast; Splatoon's Color Lock (fixed high-contrast team colours) is cited as best practice. Splatoon can afford per-match colour PAIRS/triads chosen for contrast; Cosmic Shore ships one fixed triad.
  - evidence: Game Accessibility Guidelines page read in full; Splatoon Color Lock via Twinfinite/Inkipedia snippets.
  - sources: https://gameaccessibilityguidelines.com/ensure-no-essential-information-is-conveyed-by-a-fixed-colour-alone/ | https://twinfinite.net/guides/how-to-lock-colors-splatoon-3/
- [verified] REPO: the live palette's names do not match its hues. OriginalColorSetSO (the active asset per Docs/PALETTE.md): Jade trail = teal (0.055, 0.753, 0.714) over a blue core (0, 0.388, 0.749); Ruby = magenta (0.784, 0, 0.765) over purple (0.275, 0, 0.749); Gold = orange (1, 0.657, 0) over a PURE RED core (1, 0, 0); the neutral 'Blue' sentinel = (0.4, 0.5, 1) over (0, 0, 0.5). So the reddest thing on screen is Gold, 'Ruby' is purple, 'Jade' is blue-teal, and three of the four painted identities are cool hues. The shipped bug report 'I selected gold and got the blue domain' was a Jade default read as blue.
  - evidence: Assets/_SO_Assets/Color Palettes/OriginalColorSetSO.asset lines 18-101; Docs/PALETTE.md §1 names it as live; ServerPlayerVesselInitializerWithAI.cs comment records the playtest report.

- [verified] REPO: the lobby defaults to THREE domains above two players and never spreads humans. ArcadeGameConfigureModal.DefaultDomainCount = 3, clamped by ComputeMaxDomainCount = min(PlayerCount, 3, game.MaxDomainsAllowed) (2 players -> DC 2). Player.NetDomain initialises to Jade, the menu resets it to Jade, and a human only changes domain by tapping a tile; NormalizeUnassignedHumans only moves humans off INACTIVE domains; AI backfill goes to the emptiest domain first. Consequences: (a) a party that taps nothing plays 'all humans on Jade vs AI on Ruby and Gold' — a co-op-vs-the-world shape the UI never names; (b) a 4-human party that taps into 2/1/1 launches that way — there is no equal-sides guard (the only start gate found is the weekly challenge); (c) a 2-human, 2-seat Joust with no taps launches with both humans on Jade and the opposing panel hidden (MultiplayerHUD hides domains with no players).
  - evidence: ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs lines 172-186, 1311-1326, 1655-1720, 2413-2419; Player.cs line 31; GameDataSO.BuildHumanCounts (1195-1208); ServerPlayerVesselInitializerWithAI.cs 96-127, 373-394; MultiplayerHUD.cs 215-250.

- [verified] REPO: three-way scoring surfaces disagree with each other. The end-of-round reveal is binary — Joust/Rampage rules return 'VICTORY' or 'DEFEAT … LOST BY N' so the SECOND of three domains is told it lost exactly like the third — while Maelstrom pays placement 2/1/0 (unit tests: 2-domain loser earns 0) and the economy pays 50 crystals for second. ScoringRuleSO.ResolvePlacementOrder breaks equal sums by enum order (Jade < Ruby < Gold), so the default domain wins ties. ElementalComebackSystem sizes EVERY trailing domain's buff by its deficit to the single leader, so at DC 3 the leader faces two buffed teams — Splatoon's original leader-punishment shape. Astro League and Brood Rush pin MaxDomainsAllowed 2; Peel the Cage, Wildlife Liberation, Dog Fight, Bends, Salvo, Scarab Scramble and Maelstrom allow 3; every domain mode requires >= 2 (MinDomainsAllowed 2).
  - evidence: JoustScoringRuleSO.cs 65-70, RampageScoringRuleSO.cs 86-89, ScoringRuleSO.cs 90-110, ElementalComebackSystem.cs 405-430, MaelstromDataSOTests.cs 348-361, ArcadeGame*.asset MaxDomainsAllowed/MinDomainsAllowed fields.

- [speculative] No shipped game was found that runs THREE sides in a FOUR-player match. Every three-plus-sided precedent is 8-24 players (Splatoon 8, The Finals 9-12, Fall Guys ~30-per-round, Mario Kart World 24, Halo 8-12, Splitgate 24) and most fill seats with matchmaking or CPUs. A 2/1/1 human split with AI on the singleton domains is therefore an unprecedented shape, and the closest analogue (Fall Guys' unequal teams) was removed.
  - evidence: Inference across the comparables above; absence of evidence after 20+ searches.
  - sources: https://www.halopedia.org/Multi-Team | https://kotaku.com/mario-kart-worlds-update-knockout-tour-team-battles-2000662201 | https://www.pushsquare.com/news/2020/08/fall_guys_patch_may_reduce_how_often_team_games_are_selected

### Comparables
- Splatoon 3 — Tricolor Turf War (Splatfest) (2022-2025, $): 46% 'hated defending' at launch (1,522 votes); roles randomised + 1.5x bonus Dec 2022; five stages still being rebalanced for attacker/defender win-ratio skew in Mar 2025; 4-2-2 sizes never changed; kept as the centrepiece of the Sept 2024 Grand Festival. LESSON: A three-way mode can be a flagship, but structural leader-punishment reads as unfair immediately and never stops needing tuning; fix it with role randomisation and rewards, not by pretending the third side is free.
- The Finals — Quick Cash 3v3v3 / Cashout 3v3v3v3 (2023-2026, $): Perennial 'sandwiched / third-partied through no fault of your own' complaints; a two-team mode added nearly every season; ranked briefly moved to 5v5 (June 2024) and was forced back to four-team Cashout by Sept 2024; Steam CCU ~242k at launch to ~12-15k in 2026. LESSON: Multi-team can be an identity only if the game is built around it (single objective, third-party counterplay) and even then it needs a two-team release valve and a large population to fill sides.
- Fall Guys — three-team rounds (Egg Scramble, Hoarders, Rock 'n' Roll, Team Tail Tag) (2020, $): Widely hated; Mediatonic (Aug 2020) restricted team rounds to equal-sized teams only and lowered their frequency. LESSON: Casual players reject unequal sides outright; with lowest-out elimination the gang-up targets the weakest, with first-to-target it targets the leader — either way the third side needs a designed role.
- Halo — Multi-Team (Halo 2 through Infinite Combat Workshop) (2004-2024, $): Team sizes churned (Reach 6x2 to 4x3 in 2011), Halo 5 cycled it out, Infinite reintroduced it in 2024 as 2v2v2v2 rendering ALL enemies one colour and asking for feedback on friend-vs-all-foes. LESSON: When more than two sides exist, AAA collapses enemies to a single colour; Cosmic Shore cannot, because the world's mass carries the domain colour — its readability problem is Splatoon's, not Halo's.
- Mario Kart World — team Knockout Tour (v1.5.0) (2026, $): 12v12 / 8v8v8 / 6v6v6v6 at 24 players with CPU fill; team wins as a unit; eliminated players stay to cheer/troll; MK8D never offered more than two teams. LESSON: Three-plus sides are shipped at large headcounts with CPU fill and a per-team placement result, not as a four-player default.
- Splitgate 2 — Onslaught 8v8v8 (2025, $): Launched 6 June 2025; layoffs 22 July; Onslaught matchmaking disabled 18 Sept; relaunched 17 Dec 2025 without factions; post-relaunch Steam peak 2,297. LESSON: Multi-team is the first casualty of a thin population: it needs N full sides per match, so a small EA audience should not depend on it as the default.
- Marvel Rivals — hidden quick-play bot lobbies (2025, $): Sustained backlash to unlabelled 4-humans-plus-bots lobbies despite a separate labelled vs-AI mode. LESSON: Bots on a side are acceptable when labelled and chosen; they poison trust when disguised. Cosmic Shore already shows AI chips in the lobby — carry that labelling into the HUD columns and results.
- Rocket League / Fortnite — playlist and party-size splits (2025-2026, $): Rocket League 3v3 most populated, 2v2 second; Fortnite Solos 36.2% / Duos 35.4% / Squads 21.6% / Trios 6.8%. LESSON: Design the two-human flows first (1v1 and duo-vs-AI), then 2v2; the three-human free-for-all is a real but secondary case.

### Market wants
- (both, must-have) Equal-sized sides by default; never an auto-launched 2/1/1
- (casual, strong) A named 'us vs the world' front door for a duo or party (all humans one domain, labelled AI on the others)
- (both, strong) Second place must mean something in a three-way round (placement result, not binary VICTORY/DEFEAT)
- (hardcore, strong) Do not structurally punish the leader with two buffed rivals; if comeback exists, size it to the next place or cap the combined buff
- (both, must-have) Side identity readable beyond colour (names that match hues, a glyph/shape channel, a colour-lock or colourblind palette)
- (hardcore, strong) A two-sided option always available when three-way chaos is not wanted, and three-way offered as 'chaos' rather than the default
- (both, must-have) Bots that are visibly bots, in lobby, HUD and results
- (casual, nice-to-have) Keep the three-way option for the players who love the chaos

### Implications for Cosmic Shore
- Default the lobby to EQUAL sides derived from headcount, not to DC=3: 2 players -> 1v1, 3 -> 1v1v1 (free-for-all, which players understand; 2v1 is the shape they hate), 4 -> 2v2, 6 -> 3v3 or 2v2v2. Change ComputeDefaultDomainCount to compute 'largest DC that divides PlayerCount' and add a Fall Guys-style guard that refuses to launch unequal human splits without an explicit 'Uneven teams' toggle. Astro League and Brood Rush already pin 2 and are unaffected.
- Name the shape the code already defaults to. Every human starts on Jade and AI fills Ruby/Gold, so a party that touches nothing is playing co-op-vs-the-HyperSea — but the lobby shows three tiles and never says so. Put two explicit lobby presets on the card: 'Party vs the HyperSea' (all humans one domain, AI on the rest, labelled) and 'Split up' (humans spread). This is also what makes a Steam 'Online Co-op' tag honest; without a named vs-AI shape, use 'Online PvP' only, or the May-2025 co-op-tag crowd will punish the listing.
- Three-way rounds must report PLACEMENT, not VICTORY/DEFEAT. Today the second of three domains sees 'DEFEAT — LOST BY N' while Maelstrom pays it 1 point and the economy 50 crystals. Add a 2ND PLACE reveal (and a third) when RequestedDomainCount == 3; the Maelstrom meta already models it.
- Re-derive the comeback buff for three sides. ElementalComebackSystem gives BOTH trailing domains a buff sized to the single leader's lead — the Splatoon launch shape that 46% hated and Nintendo removed. Size the buff to the gap to the NEXT place (third chases second, second chases first), or cap the sum of active buffs, so leading is not a double penalty.
- Fix the tie-break: ResolvePlacementOrder resolves equal sums by enum order, so Jade — the domain every human defaults to — wins ties. Break ties by time the sum was reached (or share the placement).
- Fix names-vs-hues before EA. 'Ruby' is purple, 'Gold' has a pure-red core, 'Jade' is blue-teal, neutral 'Blue' is blue: voice callouts ('go after Ruby') and the scoreboard's colour cue disagree. Either re-author the SO_ColorSet so each name is its hue or rename the domains to their hues; then add a non-colour channel (a per-domain glyph on the top-bar columns, lobby tiles, kill/toast feed) per the Game Accessibility Guidelines, and ship colourblind palette variants (the palette-set architecture already exists) as Cosmic Shore's Color Lock. Run a deuteranopia/protanopia simulation of the live triad — it has never been measured and three of four painted identities are cool hues.
- Accept that Cosmic Shore cannot use Halo's trick (all enemies one colour) because prisms carry the domain in-world; its readability problem is Splatoon's, and Splatoon budgets per-match contrast-selected triads plus Color Lock. Treat the three-colour world as a colour-science deliverable, not a UI tweak.
- Keep three domains as a platform capability and sell it as the chaos option ('Tricolor'/'Free-for-all'), the way The Finals' Quick Cash and Root lean in — but let two-sided be the shipped default for parties. Modes that lose texture at DC 2 and need their own default: Maelstrom (2/1/0 collapses to 2/0, so race-to-6 becomes three wins), and any mode whose target/comeback rate was tuned against three sums (Dog Fight, Bends, Wildlife Liberation generators assert comeback against the TARGET, not the side count — re-run them at DC 2).
- Design the two-human flows first: the duo is the modal party (Fortnite duos ~= solos), so 1v1 and duo-vs-AI must be the most polished paths; 2v2 second; the three-human free-for-all third. Instrument PostHog now with DC, human split (4/0/0 vs 2/2 vs 2/1/1) and AI count per match so the EA default can be moved on data rather than argued.
- Label AI sides everywhere, not only as lobby chips: the top-bar column, the winner banner and the results list should say 'AI' or 'Bots' for a domain with no humans. Marvel Rivals shows the cost of letting a bot side pass as players.

### Open questions
- Has any playtest run a full 4-human 2/1/1 match end to end, and what did the two singleton pilots (each facing a 2-human domain plus the other singleton) report? No comparable exists in the market, so this is the one shape that must be measured in-house.
- Does PostHog record RequestedDomainCount, the human split per domain, and AI count per match today? Without it the EA default is a guess in both directions.
- Does the Maelstrom 2/1/0 meta produce cross-round kingmaking (a hopeless third domain deciding whether a leader takes 2 or 1 in the final round)? The race-to-6 length makes that a live possibility with three sides.
- What does the live palette look like under deuteranopia/protanopia/tritanopia simulation, and does the Jade-vs-Ruby (blue vs purple) pair survive protanopia at all?
- With equal-sides-by-default, what happens at odd AI-backfilled counts (5 -> 2/2/1, 7 -> 3/2/2)? The AI balancer fills lowest-total first, so the rule needs a definition of 'equal' that tolerates one-off seats or forces the PlayerCount stepper to even values.
- Should a domain get a non-colour channel on prisms themselves (pattern/tier-independent marking) or is HUD/toast/vessel-band glyphing enough? The prism shader budget and the clock-material law make the in-world channel expensive.
- Is 'Tricolor'/three-way worth a named card in Maelstrom's pool as a chaos round, given The Finals' evidence that a subset of hardcore players actively prefers it?


## COMPLETENESS CRITIC
### Strongest consensus
- Sixteen mostly vessel-locked modes x four intensities x three domains fragments a population that will realistically be tens to low hundreds of CCU; collapse the public front door to a handful of modes at EA and fold the rest into Maelstrom, rotation, or a lab tab (L1, L2, L4, L5, L6, L10).
- The Early Access day is the only launch the game gets: only ~20% of 2025 EA graduates did better at 1.0 and median 1.0 revenue was ~40% of the EA window, so nothing should be held back for 1.0 and the roadmap should be dated content events, not a graduation (L2, L4, L6, L7).
- Cutting Next Fest and the demo is the most expensive cut in the plan; a demo months ahead of a fest is the strongest free wishlist accelerator in the 2025-26 data (L1, L2, L3, L4, L7).
- 'A casual game for hardcore gamers' is a positioning statement, not a hook: the store page, capsule and first ten seconds of trailer must name one known genre plus one twist via one vessel-mode pair, and 'multigenre' cannot be a Steam tag (L1, L2, L3, L7, L10).
- $15 sits above the price band where 2025-26 short-session multiplayer hits live ($5-10) and below the single-player quality band; model $9.99, a launch discount, or a 4-pack (L1, L2, L3, L7).
- The 4,000-crystal vessel price at 200/50/0 per match (20 wins per hull) is a free-to-play grind gate inside a paid, no-IAP game and will be an early negative-review theme; unlock vessels for EA and move progression elsewhere (L1, L2, L3, L4, L5, L6, L10).
- Paid PvP at indie scale collapses to ~6% of launch CCU within weeks; AI backfill is the game's structural hedge and must be first-class, instant, and honest rather than a fallback (L1, L2, L4, L5, L6, L9, L10).
- Co-op / friend-group play is where 2025-26 multiplayer revenue and virality are; the emergent ecosystem is the one asset no competitor has and should be the co-op antagonist in a hero mode rather than background cleanup (L1, L2, L3, L5, L7, L8-conditionally).
- Skipping the Steamworks SDK is more costly than it looks: Steam overlay invites are the friend-recruitment channel that carried the co-op hits, and Steam Input plus achievements are cheap review-negatives to remove; reconsider before EA (L2, L3, L4, L5, L6).
- Cutting host migration on player-hosted Relay is a retention risk: a host drop ends everyone's match, and every comparable shows launch-window instability as the first churn driver (L1, L3, L6).
- Skim Race's deterministic seed is Trackmania for free: seed-of-the-day, medals, ghosts and friends-scoped leaderboards are the cheapest hardcore hook that needs zero live population, and the rewind system already exists to power ghosts (L1, L4, L5, L6).
- Hardcore hygiene is non-negotiable and currently unverified: in-game rebinding, uncapped/60+ fps at 1080p on mainstream GPUs in the heaviest cells, readable HUD text, device-correct glyphs, published anti-cheat posture (L4, L5, L9).
- Client-local fauna (per-peer Random spawns) undermine competitive integrity in ecology-scored modes; either sync them or keep ecosystem interference out of anything scored (L5, L8).
- The roadmap should buy cadence and depth in existing modes (new cells, episodes, earned looks, a vessel that plays across modes) rather than breadth ('more vessel-locked modes'), because every winner went deeper on one thing and every multiplayer loser split its population (L2, L4, L6, L10).
- In-game voice and a clip/replay surface are missing pieces of the friend-group and streamer channel; without them the game's moments are silent physics with no faces (L1, L3, L7).
### Contradictions
- Beachhead disagreement: L4/L7/L10 lean Squirrel + Skim Race (trail becomes track), L3/L10 and the product doc name Scarab Scramble, L7 offers Astro League (sword soccer), L1/L3/L5 want a co-op-vs-ecosystem hero mode, L2 suggests the Sparrow ecology hunt, L1/L8/L10 want Maelstrom as the default button. The lenses agree the game needs ONE front door and then name six different ones.
- Bots - lead with them or hide them: L2/L4/L6 say AI backfill IS the product and 'fully playable solo with bots' belongs in the first store-page paragraph; L5 and L10 say hardcore players read a bot-filled match as a dead game and bot-filled ranked is rejected; L1 says visible bots are accepted for solo play. Nobody reconciles disclosure with the dead-game read.
- Steam tag strategy: L8 recommends tagging toward Sandbox / Procedural Generation / Artificial Intelligence (4.1% hit rate vs racing 2.1%), while L2/L7/L10 insist the page name ONE known genre and L7 targets PvP Fest by competitive-multiplayer tags; L2 separately says do not tag 'online multiplayer' at all.
- Next Fest feasibility: L2 tells the team to enter the October 2026 Next Fest; L7 states October registration closed Aug 31 2026, only Feb 22-Mar 1 2027 remains, and an EA launch before Mar 1 2027 forfeits Next Fest permanently. L2's recommendation is impossible on L7's dates.
- Who the buyer is: L1 offers two shapes (solo forever-run vs friendslop), L3 assumes the under-35 friend-group cohort, L4 builds around solo time-attack players with ghosts, L5 around Balatro/Trackmania async mastery, L8 around the systems/simulation crowd, L9 around the Steam Deck handheld player. The recommended front door, price and marketing differ by persona and no lens settles it.
- Mode count at EA: L6 says one or two entry points, L1/L2/L4 say 2-3, L5 says 3-4 hero modes, L10 says 4-6 curated modes plus rotation. A 1-to-6 range is not a consensus number.
- Achievements: L3 calls achievements irrelevant (only Steam invites matter) while L1, L2 and L4 call them table stakes / the cheapest review-negative to remove; L6 calls them hygiene.
- Cosmetics: L3 says return reasons should be content 'not cosmetics'; L6, L4 and L10 say earned cosmetics are the most-requested D30 hook and should come back into EA scope.
- Steam Deck priority: L2 says Deck is not a channel (0.7% of users) and Verified should not take engineering before Next Fest; L9 makes Deck Playable/Verified-grade a must-have for EA launch and calls the Deck the actual 'casual for hardcore' device.
- The elemental system: L1 calls the 4x4 element/ability contract the game's 'break the run' engine to surface and sell; L4 calls mid-run crystals hardcore-hostile noise on any leaderboard (needs fixed loadout); L5 wants the comeback element buff switched OFF wherever a number is published.
- Voice defaults: L3 wants proximity voice default-on as the comedy engine; L5 wants safe defaults with no forced voice/text; L9 notes the absence of voice is what keeps the cross-play safety surface small.
- Co-op mode existence: L1 diagnoses 'no co-op-versus-the-ecosystem mode' while L2, L3, L5 and L7 name Co-op Wildlife Blitz and 2v2 Co-op vs AI as existing modes to promote; L1's diagnosis is partly built on a factual misread.
- Ecosystem as the pitch: L1/L3/L5 make the living ecosystem the flagship hook and co-op antagonist; L8 says only two of ~16 modes make fauna the objective, fauna are client-local, and 'artificial life' language should stay out of the pitch; L10 says keep the hub and toys out of the trailer entirely.
- Intensity tiers: L10 proposes moving progression onto intensity tiers, L2/L6 count the four intensities as matchmaking fragmentation to remove, and L8 wants intensity to gate ecosystem aggression - three different jobs for one dial.
- Price arithmetic: L3 says $15 is 'above the $15.64 median' of top-selling new Steam games (it is below it) while L2 uses the same $15.64 figure to place $15 inside the range and argues from conversion (0.10x above $10) instead; both reach 'lower the price' by inconsistent routes.
- Weekly challenge: L5 calls the weekly challenge 'the right shape'; L4 calls its one-attempt-per-UTC-day rule anti-'one more run' and wants unlimited attempts; L6 calls it a supplement that needs a daily seed.
### Weakest evidence
- The same '340k CCU' figure is attributed to Slay the Spire 2's day one (L1, March 2026) and to Meccha Chameleon (L3); Meccha's '20M copies by Aug 2026', Mewgenics' '1M in seven days', and Big Walk's '1M in six days' are all unsourced headline numbers.
- L5 admits its search budget was exhausted after seven queries: Mario Kart World's custom item rules, the Knockout City Aug-2026 relaunch and Valve's anti-cheat disclosure text were reached via snippets or not at all - yet L10 relies on Knockout City as a dead comparable and L5 uses MKW rule presets as a design model.
- L6's GameAnalytics 2026 median PC retention of D1/D7/D30 ~7%/4%/1% is implausibly low against every commonly cited benchmark and may be a misread of the report's denominator; it underpins the 'thin reasons to return at week four' argument.
- L7's Next Fest calendar (Oct 2026 registration closed Aug 31; Feb 22-Mar 1 2027 fest; register by Jan 10; demo by Feb 8; EA before Mar 1 2027 forfeits Next Fest) is stated without a source and is the single most load-bearing date claim in the sweep.
- L9's forward-dated platform claims (Steam Machine at $1,049 in June 2026, Project Helix dev kits 2027, Switch 2 at 23.68M, Sony's cross-play royalty still in force) are several years past any verifiable record and the lens itself flags the Sony terms as unconfirmed.
- L8's 'simulation-adjacent tags reach 1,000 reviews at 4.1% vs racing 2.1%' and Rain World's 2.4-2.5M copies are secondary estimates with no named source, and they drive the tag recommendation that contradicts L2/L7.
- L2's launch arithmetic (8k wishlists -> ~800 week-one sales -> ~$8.4k gross -> ~25 reviews -> peak CCU in the tens) stacks three median ratios into a point estimate; it is a model, not an observation, and is presented as a finding.
- L1's product claims include at least one factual error ('no co-op-versus-the-ecosystem mode' - Co-op Wildlife Blitz and 2v2 Co-op vs AI exist), so its diagnosis of 'what the product lacks' is partly a misread of the product rather than evidence about the market.
- L4/L5's on-ramp risk ('dual-WASD / dual-stick as the default hull steering') is asserted from Redout 2 reviews; the product context says one-thumb hulls already use a single-stick mouse scheme, and no playtest data on Cosmic Shore's own on-ramp is cited.
- L6's 'UGC adds +75% CCU retention at two years' is a single GameDiscoverCo summary drawn from Workshop-heavy titles (Garry's Mod, Rain World) and may not transfer to a 1-4 player party game with a painting export.
- Fortnite 'shutting Rocket Racing, Ballistic and Festival Battle Stage in 2026' appears in L1 and L10; if both draw on one report, cross-lens agreement is not independent confirmation, and Rocket Racing's failure inside Fortnite is a weak analogue for a standalone paid game.
- L10's roster counts (nine vessels; 'five meet the fleet contract' including the Urchin) do not match the product doc (eleven class types, Urchin lacking any HUD prefab), so its 'launch with the five finished vessels' prescription is built on a miscount.
- L3's claim that '$15 is above the $15.64 median' is arithmetically wrong and the '1.5-3x the wave's norm' framing depends on which sub-$10 titles are counted as 'the wave'.
